**Rank is a ratchet.** The rating falls as you master the material — that is the deflation this section wants, and the board says so in words when there is nothing left to measure — but **the rank it bought does not fall**, because rank gates tools and this layer never removes a tool you own. `call.ratingDetail(calls, N, {rank})` floors the computed rank on the rank held — **`rankFor(ceiling, { floor })`**, the price being the next paragraph and not optional decoration — and all three writers pass it: `state.applyTarget`, `state.endJob` and `screens/mock.js applyMockCall`. The demotion it closes was a **continuous slide, not the `n = 0` corner** — the expressible honest ceiling is `5 + 2·k·(w·E[c])/N` in the number of informative slots `k`, so the recomputed rank walked 5 → 4 → 3 → 2 with `measured === true` at every step. `tests/job-call.test.mjs` asserts that a **measured** window with a **binding** floor holds its rank, that a **non-binding** floor still reports `held === false` and still lets an earned rating fall, and that over the whole slide from a full informative window to an all-blank one the rank is non-decreasing at every step and ends where it started. **The cost, published rather than left for a later round:** a student who genuinely becomes badly calibrated keeps the 95 rung and guardMult 0.75, `ε` stays pinned at its lowest floor so the guard reads their habits hardest, and an over-caller who once reached a band keeps it. G2's two-sided tension now runs in one direction only.

**THE CAP — the rank is priced off the CALLS, not off the dice, and this document had never published it.** A ratchet on a statistic that rewards variance is a lottery: `max` over time of a noisy score is won by whoever reports widest, whatever their calibration. So the shipped rule is not `rankFor(value, …)`. It is

```
ceiling = RATING.base + RATING.scale · Σ slotCeiling(w, p, q̂) / N   slotCeiling(w, p, q̂) = w · E[c](p, q̂)
earned  = ceiling                      rank = rankFor(earned, { floor: rank held })
```

**`earned` is `ceiling` ALONE, and the `min(value, …)` that shipped with the cap was removed at verify round 2 because it was measured to break the very theorem the cap exists to prove.** The two quantities are taken at two different rates: `ceiling` is the value of a report conditional on the ESTIMATE `q̂`, while `value` realises at the student's TRUE rate — and a window is selected into the honest rung precisely when `q̂` has run *above* that true rate. At a true `q` of 0.55, over 60 lives × 200 calls: the honest player's rating sat at or above their own ceiling in **4.7 %** of windows (mean gap −1.501) while a branch-conditional over-caller's sat there in **45.9 %** (mean gap −0.098). So the `min` threw the truthful ceiling away 19 windows in 20 and the liar's away 1 in 2, and that policy out-banked truth at four of six true `q` *even with the `q̂` stored*. Priced off `ceiling` alone, truth is the **strict** argmax over all eight policies at all six rates. The `min` also bought no safeguard it was claimed to: a ceiling above the neutral needs `w ≥ 0.25` **and** a rung worth more than the 50's flat 0, which is `q̂ ≥ 0.7`, so the ceiling *is* a competence measurement and not a substitute for one.

— **`ceiling` is the rating the student's own REPORTS are worth on this material**, and it reads `ok` on no slot, so **no outcome moves it**: at every `q̂` the game can compute, a window's ceiling is the same number at 0, 1, 25, 49 and 50 clears, and a window of mastered-material calls is worth exactly `5.00` (`tests/job-call.test.mjs` S3-CAP §2). Five consequences, each an arm of that suite and none of them decoration:

- **The cap is maximised slot by slot by the rung that is truthful ABOUT `q̂`, at EVERY `q̂` the game can compute.** Because the credit `c` is strictly proper, the rung that maximises `E[c](p, q̂)` is the truthful one by definition, so the cap is maximised by reporting `q̂` — with no appeal to luck and no horizon. **Read that with its argument.** `q̂` is the slot's stored trailing-10 CLEAR RATE, so this is propriety in the report against the student's RECORD on the make, not against their belief about the target in front of them; the two come apart whenever the student knows more than their window does, and §3.1's "the rank is capped at what the reports were worth" carries the measured gap. Over the informative **reachable** grid — which is NOT the deciles, and this line published it as exactly `{0.1 … 0.9}` for four rounds: the reachable `q̂` are `h/of` with **`of = min(RATING.qHatWindow, sittings on that make)`** — `qHatDetail` divides by the sittings the make HAS, not by the window, so a make reports sevenths until its eighth sitting and tenths only from its tenth, so the informative reachable set has **31** members, among them 1/9, 1/8, 1/7, 2/7, 3/8, 2/3, 3/4, 5/6, 6/7, 7/8 and 8/9 — no rung out-caps the truthful one at any `q̂` and at least one rung is strictly worse at every `q̂`. (The identity holds at all 31, with 0 mismatches; the defect was that it was ASSERTED over 9 of them. `call.reachableQHats` / `call.informativeQHats` now publish the grid and `tests/job-call.test.mjs` §1/§4/§6/§8 sweep it, with a floor of 31 members so the arm cannot shrink back to the deciles.) That is §3.8 #3's "truthful self-assessment is the dominant reporting policy" as an identity rather than a hope. An honest window's ceiling **is** `expectedRating(q̂)`, i.e. the *rating* column of §3.1's Sanity table (7.69 · 9.48 · 9.54 at q̂ .70 / .80 / .90), asserted equal to 1e-9.
- **F1 — the double-root hole, and why that sentence above can now say "every".** `w = 4q̂(1−q̂)` has two roots symmetric about a half, so a slot that stored only `(w, p)` could not tell an 85 lie on material cleared 1 time in 10 from an honest 85 on material cleared 9 times in 10 — asserted, in one line, as an equality. The old cap guessed the root that *flattered* the report (every `CALL_LEVELS` `p` is ≥ ½, so it always guessed the higher one), which priced that lie at the honest master's own worth against 0 for the honest 50. **It is closed at the root, in the save schema: `callEntry` now stores the slot's own `q` and `slotCeiling(w, p, q)` reads the material instead of guessing it**, so the same lie is priced *below* the honest 50 it used to beat. The one reading that keeps the double root is a slot with **no** `q̂` to store — `screens/mock.js`'s defined-weight form, which has no make and therefore no clear rate — and there it can only ever cap TOO HIGH, so it demotes nobody and no card answer reaches it. Windows written before the change keep the old reading for the same reason. (`tests/job-call.test.mjs` S3-CAP §1 and §1b; §1 carries the pre-fix reading as its negative control, so the arm measures the fix rather than restating it.)
- **At the exact indifference of both ladders the rating ladder now hands the over-caller nothing.** At `q̂ = 3/5` — where carry EV and Brier are both exactly tied between 50 and 70 — both rungs cap at exactly `5.00` and both print **Called 2** at every clear count from 0 to 50; before the cap, the same lucky 70 window's raw rating clamped to **Called 5** while the honest 50 printed Called 2 whatever the dice did. That is what leaves the **carry** ladder as the only thing separating the two rungs there, which is what §3.7 #9 claims.
- **Driven, not only proved.** Over the shipped `windowPush → ratingDetail({rank}) → persisted rank` path, at six true clear rates from 0.50 to 0.80, the truthful policy banks at least as much rank as one rung **over** and one rung **under**, on the rank the save actually carries (§4). The same draws ratcheted the *pre-fix* way — `max` of `rankFor(rating)` — still hand the over-caller a strictly higher rank at at least one rate, and that is asserted as the anti-tautology control, so the arm cannot pass on a scorer that never had the defect (§5).
- **The price, published rather than left for a later round: `player.rank` can sit on EITHER side of `rankFor(player.rating.value)`.** The legal rungs are read off the rank (`callsFor(player.rank)`, which is what `state.lockCall` gates on), never off the rating band. A student whose dice ran ahead of their reports holds a rank *below* their rating's printed band (`ratingDetail` reports that as `capped`); one whose dice ran behind holds a rank *above* it, the same direction the ratchet's floor already produces. **Both directions are the exception G9 #4 names, and both are PRINTED.** `ratingDetail().offBand` is true exactly when the printed rank is not the printed rating's band, and `screens/settings.js ratingAuditParts` and `screens/stats.js ledgerRatingParts` then print `· your calls were worth N.NN` beside the rank, with a sentence under it saying which number is which. Without that, the Settings panel printed `10.00 · Called 2 · 50 of 50 informative calls` directly above its own legend saying 10.00 is Called 5, and nothing on the screen reconciled them. `tests/job-call.test.mjs` S3-CAP §7 drives **both shipped line builders** over a corpus of shipped windows and asserts the ceiling is in the string whenever the two bands differ. What the price does **not** do is close the top: it never blocks a rank the floor already holds, and the best a truthful window can be *worth* is still at or above the **8.9** Called 5 asks for (§6, all asserted).

**And `player.rank` is a stored high-water rather than a recomputation**, so the rating that earned it is stored beside it — `player.records.bestRating`, written at all three writers of `p.rating.value` — and printed on the two audit surfaces as `Called 5 · best rating 9.90`. It is deliberately **not** on the board line, which already carries the rating and the informative count. See G7's save table and G9 #4, which carries the exception this creates to “recomputable from the save”.

**And the audit record beside it is the number that BOUGHT the rank, not the number the dice paid.** Since THE CAP, `player.rank` is a high-water of **`earned = ceiling`** — and so is `player.records.bestRating`: all three writers of `p.rating.value` (`state.applyTarget`, `state.endJob`, `screens/mock.js applyMockCall`) store `detail.earned`. `rankFor` is monotone, so a high-water over `earned` is exactly what the held rank recomputes from: **`player.rank === max(the rank this save started at, rankFor(player.records.bestRating))`**, which is what keeps G9 #4's exception to one clause rather than two. Storing `detail.value` there instead — which is what shipped until verify round 2 — published a record that bought nothing: on a capped window it printed pairs like `Called 3 · best rating 10.00`, which no reviewer can reconcile, and that defeats the whole point of printing the record. `bestRating20` is the separate, honestly-labelled high-water of the **printed** rating and stays on `value`, because it is a record of what was shown, not of what was bought.

### The Fault Index — the one new collection

68 entries, one per tag in `data/misconceptions.js`, grouped by that file's own 11 `AREAS`. A tag is **sealed** when it has been resolved cleanly 3 times across 3 distinct days with zero re-triggers in that window. The completion certificate reads *"here are the sixty-eight mistakes I no longer make."* Live tells pay ×1.25, so **fixing your own specific error is the best-paying single thing in the game** — right up to the moment you have fixed it, which is the point.

### Backchecks — the only consumable, and it is minted by the dullest work

Max 3 held. On a miss you may spend one: **the chain holds and LOOSE is not taken. The rating credit is always taken.** A Backcheck shields the stake and only the stake — it does not change the answer, the bucket drop, the mastery hit, the error-log entry, the Rematch, **or the `calls[]` entry the rating window reads**. Without that last clause, holding 3 Backchecks would convert the first three targets of every job into downside-free 95 calls and the scoring rule would stop being proper for them (G12 #26); J7 asserts a shielded miss writes a byte-identical `calls[]` entry to an unshielded one.

Minted: **1 per day for a day on which `dues ≥ 1` and every one of them was cleared** (a day with zero dues mints nothing — G12 #38), and 1 per vault cracked with no Backcheck spent (cap 1/day). Not available on the vault itself. *The least fun, highest-value study action is the source of the game's only scarce resource.*

### Where the economy ends

There is nothing to buy, so there is no tree to finish and no rate to grind. The three progress curves are **rating** (a trailing 50-call window that stops moving when you stop improving), **crew capacity** (a function of level and boss stamps, i.e. of answering), and **the Fault Index** (68, finite). And the economy deliberately deflates: `cold` falls as buckets rise, `scope` falls to 0.5 on mastered makes, `tell` retires as tags seal, and the rating weight `4q̂(1−q̂)` collapses as you get good. The debrief says it in one line: **`posted falls as you master the material. That is the point.`** At `crew held ∧ Readiness ≥ 88` the board stops leading with a job: `Board quiet · Readiness 89 · 0 due` — with the job still one tap away. **The game has a terminus and admits it.** No prestige, no rebirth, no season, no ladder above it.

---

## G3 Game-theoretic mechanics

### 3.1 The Call — a strictly proper scoring rule, twice

Two ladders on the same four buttons. Carry pays in multipliers of `L·ρ·m·scope·wing`; rating pays a Brier credit.

| call | W (clear) | P (miss) | rating credit clear | rating credit miss |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 50 | ×1.0 | ×0.0 | 0.0 | 0.0 |
| 70 | ×1.4 | ×0.6 | +6.4 | −9.6 |
| 85 | ×1.8 | ×2.0 | +9.1 | −18.9 |
| 95 (Called ≥ 3) | ×2.2 | ×5.0 | +9.9 | −26.1 |

**Carry EV** (`EV = q·W − (1−q)·P`, units of `L·ρ·m·scope·wing`) — **on a pile that can pay the loss (`S ≥ L·m·P`, G3.2's deep branch). The argmax column below is the DEEP-pile argmax and it is not unconditional:**

| q | 50 | 70 | 85 | 95 | argmax |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.50 | **0.50** | 0.40 | −0.10 | −1.40 | 50 |
| 0.60 | **0.60** | **0.60** | 0.28 | −0.68 | 50 ≡ 70 |
| 0.70 | 0.70 | **0.80** | 0.66 | 0.04 | 70 |
| 0.80 | 0.80 | 1.00 | **1.04** | 0.76 | 85 |
| 0.90 | 0.90 | 1.20 | 1.42 | **1.48** | 95 |
| 0.95 | 0.95 | 1.30 | 1.61 | **1.84** | 95 |

Indifference exactly at `q = 0.600` (50↔70), `0.778` (70↔85), `0.882` (85↔95).

**The condition, stated here rather than buried in a data comment (round-3 exploit-hunt).** `EV = q·W − (1−q)·P` is not G2's miss branch. G2 caps the miss at the pile: `Δloose = −min(LOOSE, L·m·P·wing_pen)`. **At `S = 0` every rung's downside is exactly 0**, so a clear branch paying the full `W` is strictly increasing in `W`, and the highest call the rank allows was **weakly dominant at every `q`** — `evMaxCallAt` returned 95 (or 85 at Called 1–2) whether `q` was 0.05 or 0.99, and `econ.carryEVAt` on one tier-1 opener at `q = 0.40` read `50 → 2.40, 70 → 3.36, 85 → 4.32, 95 → 5.28`. **That is closed at verify round 1 by the COVER, at the end of this paragraph: the premium is paid only in the proportion the pile can pay the price, so those four numbers are now `2.40` four times and no rung is preferred.** At `S = 120, q = 0.50, L = 18` the published ladder returns: `50 → 9.00, 70 → 7.20, 85 → −1.80, 95 → −25.20`, argmax 50. **The state is reached on target 1 of every job, immediately after every BAG, and after any miss that emptied LOOSE**, so it is not a corner case — it is the opening beat. The table above is therefore the ladder of the *pile-deep* half of the game; `econ.evMaxCallAt(state)` is the argmax at a real state, and `job-econ.test.mjs` §3b pins both, the `S = 0` dominance included. **There is no EV brake on over-calling at `S = 0`, and since the ratchet there is no rank brake either — this paragraph used to claim a rank one and that claim stays withdrawn here as well as at G3.7 #9. What the cover adds is not a brake in that sense: it removes the PRIZE rather than restoring the price. At `S = 0` an over-call costs nothing and now buys nothing — the rungs tie, and the only thing still reading the call is the rating.** The 95 rung is gated at Called 3 **on the way up only**: rank is a ratchet (G2 "Rank"), so a student who has ever reached Called 3 keeps the rung however badly they call afterwards, and the rating falling to the clamp does not take it back. Measured through the shipped scorer on a full fifty-slot window of systematic 95-calls at q̂ = 0.5 (`tests/job-call.test.mjs` S3 item 5): `n = 50` — every slot a real measurement — `rating 0.00`, the window **on its own** prints `Called 1`, and under a held rank the printed rank does not move and `held === true`. Before the ratchet the bare recomputation was the brake, and it was a real one: the rung withdrew itself. That brake was spent to close a worse defect — mastering your material emptied the window and demoted an honest student three ranks (G2 "Rank") — and the price is published there and repeated here, because this is the paragraph where a reader meets the EV hole. **What is left is the CARRY ladder** (G3.7 #9): `P(95) = 5.0`, so on a deep pile one over-call costs `min(LOOSE, L·m·5·wing_pen)` — a whole job's bag on one target. At `S = 0` that floor is what makes the downside exactly 0, which is why the hole and the loss of the rank brake were the same sentence rather than two.

**THE COVER — the cap, applied to both sides of the wager (`econ.coverFor`, verify round 1).** BAG is offered at every beat but the last and sets `LOOSE = 0`, so "the opening beat" was every beat for one tap. Measured through the shipped machine (`startJob → lockCall → applyTarget → bag → endJob`, 16 seeded saves × 10 days × JOB-10, rank pinned once so the 95 rung is legal): **max call + bag every beat beat honest play by +95.6 % at `q = 0.50`, on 16/16 seeds, and paid more the weaker the student** — the `162` G3.7 #9 names as the brake was paid zero times. The close is one line of arithmetic:

```
cover = min(1, LOOSE / |nominal miss|)          W_eff = W(50) + (W_call − W(50))·cover
```

**You are paid a bold call's premium in the same proportion as its penalty is collectable.** On a **deep pile the cover is 1** — `isDeepPile` is the cover's own ratio — so every number in the table above, and every published deep-pile threshold, is the ladder the player actually faces. At `S = 0` the cover is 0, every rung pays `W = 1.0`, **no rung is preferred**, and the call is settled by the rating, which is strictly proper: honest calling becomes *strictly* best at the exact state where the top rung used to be free. The exploit collapses onto "call 50 and bag every beat", which the 10 % fee already prices below honest play — measured on the same harness, `95 always · BAG` banks 6 263 against honest play's 8 170 at `q = 0.50` and 7 595 against 10 531 at `q = 0.65`, and `tests/job-exploit.test.mjs` pins that it may never exceed it. Two things the cover does not touch: the **50 rung** (no price, no premium) and **crew forgiveness** (at rank ≥ 1 the miss is forgiven rather than capped, so the premium stands — that is a separate open finding). The other candidate close, **flooring the miss against BAGGED-this-job, was rejected**: BAGGED is only ever added to (G1 "The two piles"), and a repair that takes it back contradicts a published law to mend another.

**Rating credit** `c(p,o) = 10 − 40(p−o)²`. `E[c] = 10 − 40[q(1−p)² + (1−q)p²]`, `dE/dp = 80[q(1−p) − (1−q)p] = 0 ⟺ p = q`, second derivative `−80 < 0`. Strictly proper: **truthful reporting is the unique maximiser**. Discrete indifference at `0.600 / 0.775 / 0.900`.

**The rating is the measurement; the RANK is capped at what the reports were worth — a second rule on the same four buttons, and it belongs here.** `ratingDetail` prices the rank off `earned = ceiling`, where `ceiling = RATING.base + RATING.scale · Σ w·E[c](p, q̂)/N` — the rating this student's own **reporting policy** earns in expectation on this material. It reads no slot's `ok`, so the propriety above stops being a statement about one call's expectation and becomes a statement about the tool the student walks away with: because `c` is strictly proper, `ceiling` is maximised **slot by slot** by the rung that is truthful **about `q̂`** — the slot's stored trailing-10 clear rate — so reporting your RECORD ON THE MAKE is the best RANK policy as an identity rather than as a horizon-dependent bet.

**THE QUALIFIER IS LOAD-BEARING, and it was missing for a round: the rank is proper in `q̂`, not in belief.** `slotCeiling` takes its expectation against the slot's stored `q̂`, so `argmax_p` is the rung nearest the RECORD and never the rung nearest what the student believes about THIS target. On the app's own m60 material the two disagree: `RUNG_BANDS[60]` gives a true clear rate of 0.92 while a 7-of-10 record reads `q̂ = 0.70`, so `honestCall(0.70) = 70` and `honestCall(0.92) = 95`. Fifty slots of each, through `callEntry → ratingDetail`:

| report | per-slot `w·E[c](p, q̂)` | ceiling | rank | per-slot `E[w·c]` at the TRUE rate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 70 — honest about the record | 1.3440 | 7.688 | **Called 3** | 4.3008 |
| 85 | 0.5880 | 6.176 | Called 2 | 5.7624 |
| 95 — honest about the target | −0.7560 | 3.488 | **Called 1** | **5.8968** |

So the report that is TRUE about this student's clearing earns the **highest rating** in expectation (+1.596 a slot) and the **lowest rank** — two bands, and Called 3 is the gate on the 95 button itself. **This is not a defect to fix in code.** `ok` is the only other thing a slot carries, and pricing the rank off an outcome is exactly the S3 lottery the ratchet exists to refuse; before the r4 repair the rank was `rankFor(value, {floor})`, which IS proper in belief, and moving it onto `ceiling` is what closed the ratchet hole. It is a **scope**, and it is published rather than left for the student to meet at the 95 button: `screens/settings.js`'s "the only way to score well is to say what you actually believe" is now scoped to the RATING (where it is true), and the rank formula block beside it says that `E[cᵢ]` is taken against `q̂`, so a call that is right about this target but far from your record is worth more RATING than it is worth RANK. `tests/job-call.test.mjs` §8 drives the table above. (Verify r3, call-propriety.) An honest window's `ceiling` is exactly `expectedRating(q̂)` — the **rating** column of the Sanity table below, asserted equal to it at every computable `q̂`. The full rule, the double-root hole it used to leave open on `q̂ < ½` (F1, closed at verify round 2 by storing the slot's own `q̂`), the `min(value, ceiling)` it used to take (**removed** at the same round: measured, the `min` destroyed the honest player's ceiling in 19 windows out of 20 and left a branch-conditional over-caller's standing in 1 out of 2, so it broke the identity above rather than guarding it) and its cost are published in **G2 "Rank" → THE CAP**; the rating VALUE in this section is untouched by it, because the value is the measurement and it is allowed to be lucky. (G12 #78.)

**The theorem needs two assumptions and both are load-bearing, so both are stated here rather than assumed: an outcome the student does not choose, and a weight fixed at the seal.** The snapshot is `call.qHatDetail`'s **default**, not a courtesy a caller does — the history is cut at `inProgress.game.locked.at`, the instant `state.lockCall` sealed the call, so a caller that forgets the option still gets an exogenous weight. `before: null` is the explicit opt-out, it may not feed `callEntry`, and `tests/job-call.test.mjs` asserts the set of `before:` opt-outs under `site/js/` is **empty** and must stay empty. With an endogenous weight the argmax moved to `p* = q·w_clear/(q·w_clear + (1 − q)·w_miss) < q` whenever q̂ > 0.5 — lying paid, and the pre-fix read is kept alive in the suite as the control (w 0.64 after a clear against 0.84 after a miss, ratio 1.3125). Where the student CAN choose the outcome the guarantee is carried by the rank ratchet (G2 “Rank”), not by this rule.

**The two ladders disagree in exactly two bands** — `q ∈ [0.775, 0.778)` and `q ∈ [0.882, 0.900)` — and they disagree in OPPOSITE directions, which is the point (corrected at integration; notes/J2.md §5.2, notes/J7.md §7). In the upper band `q ∈ [0.882, 0.900)` money prefers the bolder call (95) and rank prefers the honest one (85). In the lower band `q ∈ [0.775, 0.778)` it is the mirror: money prefers **70** (carry EV 0.9520 against 0.9488 at q = 0.776) and rank prefers **85** (Brier 2.828 against 2.816). The bands exist because the carry ladder's cut sits above the Brier's at one rung and below it at the next, so neither ladder is uniformly the bolder one. Both are printed in Settings. It is the only place in the game where the player must choose what they are playing for, and it is **2.04 percentage points wide in total** — 0.28 pp in the lower band and 1.76 pp in the upper — on purpose. (It was published as 2.1; `call.js` and `tests/job-call.test.mjs` both compute 2.04 from the band endpoints, and Settings prints each band's own width, 0.3 and 1.8.)

**The anti-farming weight, and why the rating is a SUM over a fixed window.** `w = 4·q̂(1−q̂)`, `q̂` = your **CLEAR rate on that make over the trailing 10 sittings** — the same event `econ.settle` prices, so one button forecasts one event. (It was published as the first-attempt rate over the trailing 10 attempts, which is a different event. The code computes the clear rate and is right: re-pointing q̂ at the first attempt would make one button forecast two different events, and a student would honestly call 50 on m60 material they clear 92 % of the time.) `q̂ = .5 → w = 1.00`; `.8 → .64`; `.9 → .36`; `.933 → .25`; `.97 → .116`.

> **A call is *informative* iff `w ≥ 0.25`, i.e. `q̂ ∈ [0.067, 0.933]`. Only informative calls enter the window.**
>
> ```
> rating = clamp(0, 10, 5 + 2 · Σ(w_i · c_i) / N)        N = 50, fixed
> ```
>
> **`N` is the window size, not `Σw`.** A weighted *mean* is invariant to the weights whenever every call in the window is the same kind, which made farming mastered tier-1 locks the fastest route to the top rank: at `q̂ = .97` a truthful 95 call scores `E[c] = 8.84` and the mean does not care that `w` is only 0.116. Dividing by a fixed `N` fixes it twice over: those calls are no longer informative (`w = .116 < .25`) so they never enter the window at all, and **an unfilled slot contributes 0, which pulls the rating toward exactly 5.0.** A pure tier-1-mastered farmer's rating is not 9.41. It is **5.00**, and the board leads with the rank the ratchet holds and then says so in words: `Called 5 · rating unchanged · no measurement · 0/50 informative calls` on an unmeasured window, against `Called 4 · rating 8.66 · 37/50 informative calls` on a measured one (`COPY.ratingLine`). It used to print a bare `rating 5.00 · 0/50 informative calls` beside `Called 5`, which reads as a demotion to a student playing the best they ever have. (G12 #1.)

Sanity, all pinned by J2 — and the last column is new, because two of these rows are 5.00 for opposite reasons and the table used to print them as one behaviour:

| behaviour | mean `w·c` per call | rating | what the rating IS |
|---|---|---|---|
| calling 50 on everything | 0.000 | **5.00** | a **measurement**: 50 informative calls, all worth 0 |
| farming mastered tier-1 (no informative calls at all) | 0.000 | **5.00** | **no measurement** — the window is empty. It is not a “farmer's ceiling” and never was; it is also what an honest master prints |
| honest calls at `q̂ = .70` | 1.344 | 7.69 | a measurement |
| honest calls at `q̂ = .80` | 2.240 | 9.48 | a measurement |
| honest calls at `q̂ = .85` | **2.499** | 9.998 | a measurement, and the **continuous** peak of `w·E[c]`. `q̂ = .85` needs 17 clears in 20 and the window is 10, so this row is a bound, not a policy — but the bound is nearly tight: the reachable best (row below) is 2.4980 |
| honest calls at `q̂ = .90` | 2.268 | 9.54 | a measurement — and **not** the best a 10-sitting window can express, which this row claimed for four rounds. the reachable `q̂` are `h/of` with **`of = min(RATING.qHatWindow, sittings on that make)`** — `qHatDetail` divides by the sittings the make HAS, not by the window, so a make reports sevenths until its eighth sitting and tenths only from its tenth, so **2.4980 at `q̂ = 6/7`** is the reachable maximum, with 2.4630 (5/6), 2.4500 (7/8) and 2.3660 (8/9) also above 2.2680. 6/7 is 10.1 % above this row and within 0.0018 of the continuous peak |
| systematic over-calling (95 on `q̂ = .5` material) | **−8.10** | **0.00** (clamped) | a measurement. (−4.90 was published here; that is the **85** call on the same material. The rating column clamps either way, so nothing downstream moved.) |

**The central alignment result, restated so that it is true.** The rating is a **calibration** score, and a coin flip is not something calibration skill can be demonstrated on: `E[c] = 10 − 40q(1−q)` is exactly 0 at `q = 0.5`. So the honest two-part claim, which replaces the old one-liner:

1. **Uncertain material is where the rating is *decided*.** `w = 4q̂(1−q̂)` peaks at `q̂ = 0.5`, so calls on material you genuinely cannot predict carry the most weight — in both directions. That is leverage, not gain.
2. **Consolidating material is where the rating is *earned*.** `w · E[c] = 4q̂(1−q̂)·(10 − 40q̂(1−q̂))` is single-peaked, with its maximum at **`q̂ = 0.854`** (`u = q̂(1−q̂) = 0.125`, `f(u) = 40u − 160u²`, `f'(u) = 40 − 320u = 0`), value 2.50. The band holding ≥ 80 % of that peak is **`q̂ ∈ [0.763, 0.925]`** — material you have just learned and are still fumbling one time in six.

*The rank-maximising player is a student staking on what they have just learned and have not yet nailed.* That is the desirable-difficulty band, it is where mastery gain per minute is highest, and it is the band the app names. J1 asserts the peak of `w·E[c]` by grid search; there is no assertion anywhere that `q̂ = 0.5` maximises anything but the weight.

**And the two currencies pull in different directions, on purpose.** Carry EV is strictly increasing in `q` (you want the target you are sure of); rating is maximal at `q̂ ≈ 0.85` (you want the target you are nearly sure of). The gap is the decision the game is about, it is printed in Settings, and it is why the loot brakes in G3.7(3) have to exist at all.

### 3.2 Push or bag — push-your-luck whose bust condition is being wrong

State: `S` = LOOSE, `c` = chain, `m = 1 + 0.2·min(c,8)`, next target `L`, chosen call `(W,P)`, expected clear rung `ρ̄`, fee `φ = 0.10`.

- **BAG then answer:** `0.9S + q·L·ρ̄·1·W` — the miss term vanishes, because `LOOSE = 0` makes `min(LOOSE, ·) = 0`, and the `1` is `m_chain` after the bag's chain reset (G2). Bagging buys one target of literal invulnerability at the price of the fee **and the chain**.
- **PUSH:** `S + q·L_gain·ρ̄·m·W − (1−q)·min(S, L_loss·m·P)`

```
PUSH − BAG = 0.10·S + q·L_gain·ρ̄·W·(m − 1) − (1 − q)·min(S, L_loss·m·P)
```

**There are two `L`s, because G2 gives the two branches different multiplier sets** — and since verify round 2 a third, the STAKE (`L · ×2`), which is the base the CALL's premium is paid on (`econ.stakeLFor`; see G2). The clear branch's LOOT term charges `L · scope · wing · cold · tell · ×2`; the miss branch charges `L · wing_pen · ×2` and nothing else — the asymmetry is deliberate and it is stated in G2. A threshold built on one `L` is the threshold of a different economy: it printed the same `q*` whether a guarded-wing miss cost 22 or 43, and on five realistic states (a cold tagged review, a guarded drill, a mastered target, a ×2 posting) the verdict it printed was the opposite of the one the payout pays. They are `econ.gainLFor` / `econ.lossLFor`, which are `carryFor`'s and `missFor`'s own factor lists, and `job-econ.test.mjs` asserts the printed threshold is the root of a `PUSH − BAG` rebuilt out of those two payout functions rather than out of a second copy of this formula. `L_loss` is **0** where the crew forgives the miss, because `missFor` charges nothing there. Below, where a formula says plain `L`, both are the bare `L` — that is what "at `ρ̄ = 1`, bare target" means. (Round 2, econ-math.)

**Deep pile** (`S ≥ L_loss·m·P`), dropping the fee term for the clean asymptotic form: `θ* = (L_loss·m·P) / (L_gain·ρ̄·W·(m−1))`, `q* = θ*/(1+θ*)` — on a bare target the two `L`s cancel to `θ* = m·P / (ρ̄·W·(m−1))`, which is the table below. At `ρ̄ = 1` (the optimistic bound; the app computes `ρ̄` per target from your own rung distribution and prints the true threshold — `econ.breakevenQ`, which keeps the fee and is *not* the table's form; see the scope note below the shallow branch):

| chain c | m | call 70 q* | call 85 q* | call 95 q* |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1.2 | 0.720 | 0.870 | 0.932 |
| 2 | 1.4 | 0.600 | 0.795 | 0.888 |
| 4 | 1.8 | 0.491 | 0.714 | 0.836 |
| 8 | 2.6 | 0.411 | 0.644 | 0.787 |

**Shallow pile** (`S < L_loss·m·P`, early in a job or just after a bag) the loss truncates at `S`, so only the gain branch's `L` survives and solving `0.10S + q·L_gain·ρ̄·W·(m−1) − (1−q)·S = 0` gives

```
q* = 0.9·S / ( L_gain·ρ̄·W·(m − 1) + S )
```

At `c = 0` this is exactly **0.9** regardless of `L`: with no chain, the `m − 1` term is zero and pushing buys nothing but the fee. **So the escalation runs the other way from a slot machine: early, with a shallow pile and no chain, the threshold is brutal (0.9) and the stakes are trivial; as the chain deepens the threshold *falls* — 0.900 → 0.143 **at `S = 12`, `L = 18`, call 95** (the start is 0.9 at every state; the `c = 8` end is a function of both parameters and runs 0.042 at `L = 70` to 0.683 at `S = 200`, a 16× spread, published cell by cell as `PUBLISHED.shallowQWalk`), 0.932 → 0.787 in the deep table above — while the amount at risk *grows*.** The curve is in the stake, not in the odds, and it comes entirely from your own miss rate. No dice, no bust die, no wheel. (The previous text claimed pushing was "nearly free at the start", which the algebra contradicts.)

**Scope, because the number on screen is not the number in the table.** That falling direction is a property of the two *closed forms* — the shallow form above and the fee-free deep table. The app prints `econ.breakevenQ`, the true root of `PUSH − BAG` **with the fee kept in both branches**, and on a pile deep enough to pay the loss it runs the other way:

| `S = 200`, `L = 18`, `ρ̄ = 1` | c = 0 | 1 | 2 | 4 | 6 | 8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| printed `q*`, call 70 | 0.000 | 0.000 | 0.000 | 0.000 | 0.061 | 0.107 |
| printed `q*`, call 85 | 0.317 | 0.362 | 0.391 | 0.426 | 0.447 | 0.460 |
| printed `q*`, call 95 | 0.627 | 0.640 | 0.649 | 0.660 | 0.666 | 0.650 |

The deep root is `(L·m·P − 0.10·S) / (L_gain·ρ̄·(m·W − 1) + L·m·P)`, and it produces **17 of the 18 cells above**. The eighteenth does not come from it: at call 95, `c = 8`, `L_loss·m·P = 234` has overtaken `S = 200`, so that cell is on the **shallow** branch, `0.9·S / (L_gain·ρ̄·(m·W − 1) + S) = 0.650` — the deep root would read 0.671 there. (**Every cell of this table moved at verify round 1**, and the gain term is why: it is `m·W_push − W_bag`, not `W·(m − 1)`, because a bag empties the pile that backs the next call, so `W_bag` falls to `W(50) = 1.0` under the cover — G3.1. Pushing keeps a premium bagging forfeits, so every printed threshold fell. The two closed forms below and the deep table above are untouched: they are the *uncovered* forms, and on a deep pile the cover is 1.) The cells, their parameters and the one branch switch are `PUBLISHED.printedQ`, recomputed from `econ.breakevenQ` with each cell's branch proved from `econ.isDeepPile` and the shallow census pinned at exactly one cell (`job-econ.test.mjs` §5). The fee enters as a **fixed** credit toward pushing, and the loss term it is subtracted from grows with the chain, so the credit is worth proportionally less at every step; at a shallow chain it can swamp `L·m·P` entirely and clamp `q*` to 0 ("push at any `q`"). Drop the fee and the same branch falls, which is exactly why the table above falls. **So: at a fixed pile, on a shallow one the chain buys you a lower threshold; on a deep one at 70 and 85 it does not.** *At a fixed pile* is load-bearing and is not decoration — see the round-3 paragraph below. The half of the sentence that holds everywhere is *the amount at risk grows*. Both directions are pinned in `job-econ.test.mjs` §5 so neither can be restated as unconditional. (Round 2, econ-math.)

**What the app itself says, exactly, and what it does not (round 3, econ-math).** The earlier version of the sentence above ended by asserting that the app already scopes this. It does not, and `job-shape-measured.test.mjs` §3 now fails if that attribution is ever written back. The one place the app states the direction is the Settings "chain, and when to bank it" card, mid-paragraph, immediately after the shallow closed form — *"As the chain deepens the threshold falls and the amount at risk grows"* — with no scope on either side of it, and the next sentence pivots to the number the app prints (`econ.breakevenQ`). **Scoping that paragraph is a copy change in `site/js/screens/settings.js`, which this lane does not own; it is filed as a Request in `notes/econ-fix.md` and this document does not get to lean on it in the meantime.** **And the obvious defence of the copy — "but on a real play path the pile grows with the chain, so it falls anyway" — is false, which is the sharper half of this finding.** Every falling number in this section is a *comparative static*: hold `S` fixed, vary `c`. Walk a job instead — clear each target, bank nothing, let `S` accumulate — and the direction does not survive the move. Eight tier-1 clears at call 95:

| | beat 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| printed `q*` (`breakevenQ`) | 0.000 | 0.584 | 0.608 | 0.625 | 0.638 | 0.647 | 0.656 | **0.662** |
| shallow closed form | 0.000 | 0.542 | 0.589 | 0.625 | 0.654 | 0.677 | 0.699 | **0.718** |
| deep closed form (`θ*`, no `S` in it) | 1.000 | 0.932 | 0.888 | 0.858 | 0.836 | 0.820 | 0.806 | **0.796** |

**`shallowQStar` itself rises on a played path** — it is `0.9·S / (A + S)` and `S` outruns `A` once the chain is paying — so the falling direction is not even a property of "the shallow branch"; it is a property of the shallow branch *at a pile that does not move*. The only form that falls on a played path is the **deep** one, and it falls trivially, because `θ* = m·P / (ρ̄·W·(m−1))` does not mention the pile at all. The printed number is neither: on the T2-heavy job the composer actually drafts, at call 85, it prints `0.000 · 0.391 · 0.571 · 0.515 · 0.549 · 0.356 · 0.512 · 0.479 · 0.176 · 0.437` — no direction a player could act on. **The honest statement, and the one this document will not walk back: the threshold falls with the chain only in a comparison that holds the pile still; over a session it does not fall, and the half that is true everywhere is that the stake grows.** `job-econ.test.mjs` §5 pins the played path for all three forms, so this cannot be restated as a session-level claim anywhere.

Two worked rows the tests pin:
- `S = 300, c = 0 (m = 1), next T4 (L = 70), call 85, q = 0.5, ρ̄ = 1`: `L·m·P = 140 < 300` (deep) → `30 + 0.5·70·(1·1.8 − 1) − 0.5·140 = 30 + 28 − 70 = −12` → **BAG**.
- Same with `q = 0.8, c = 6 (m = 2.2)`: `L·m·P = 308 > 300` (shallow, so the call is covered at `300/308 = 0.974` and `W_push = 1.779`) → `30 + 0.8·70·(2.2·1.779 − 1) − 0.2·300 = 30 + 163.20 − 60 = +133.20` → **PUSH**.

**Where to stake, and where not to.** There is **no interior growth optimum in `q` for loot**: `E[Δloose] = q·L·ρ̄·m·W − (1−q)·L·m·P` is strictly increasing in `q`, because LOOSE is not multiplied by the pile — the gain term contains no `S`, and the loss is capped by `S` rather than proportional to it. The "growth-optimal band 0.52–0.63" was imported from an economy this one is not, and it is deleted along with its test. (G12 #6.)

What *is* single-peaked here is the rating's `w·E[c]` (§3.1), so the band the app names is the **stake band `q̂ ∈ [0.763, 0.925]`** — computed in `econ.stakeBand()` from the **continuous** `w(q̂)·E[c](q̂)`, printed in Settings with its own substitution (`u = q̂(1 − q̂)`, `w·E[c] = 40u − 160u²`, peak `u = 0.125`), and asserted in `job-econ.test.mjs` as its ≥ 80 %-of-peak region. **The four-rung DISCRETE object is a different one and its band is `[0.779, 0.925]`** — the published band is not “over the four call rungs”, and this document said it was. Never a card you know cold, never a card you cannot do. The optimal door is different for every student and moves every time they learn something.

### 3.3 Why bagging is not automatic (the fee's proof of work)

Without the fee, at `c = 0` we get `m − 1 = 0`, so `PUSH − BAG = −(1−q)·min(S, LP) ≤ 0` in every state: *bag immediately after every miss* would be a dominant sub-strategy and one of the two per-target decisions would be dead. With `φ = 0.10`, at `c = 0`, `L = 6`, call 70, `S = 50.76`: `min(S, L·1·P) = 3.6`, so `PUSH − BAG = 5.076 − 3.6(1−q) > 0` for all `q ≥ 0`. The decision is live at every chain value and flips on pile size, target grade, rung distribution, crew rank and call — a five-variable decision, not a habit.

### 3.4 The Guard — matching pennies on your own habits, with published odds

Four **wings** partition the 19 skills; weights from `data/skills.js` sum to 100 exactly.

| wing | skills | Σw |
|---|---|---|
| RECALL | VOC 7, NOTE 8, CLASS 3, ASN-PLP 7, ASN-ANG 7 | 32 |
| FIGURES | PAIRS 8, FIG-ALG 8, BISECT-L 4, BISECT-Q 3, SEG-ALG 3 | 26 |
| WORDS | CSARITH 5, CS-LIN 9, CS-RATIO 5, CS-QUAD 4 | 23 |
| ALGEBRA | SYS 4, FAC1 3, FAC2 6, QUAD-SOLVE 4, QUAD-CTX 2 | 19 |

You press 3 tokens across the **support** — the wings the drafted contracts actually touch — sealed. `composeBundles` guarantees the 5 posted contracts span ≥ 3 wings and that **any** legal 3-of-5 draft spans ≥ 2; the board prints the support size (`guard: 3 wings on the board`) so `n` is never a surprise. (Contracts are labelled by dominant make and can carry locks from more than one wing; VOC + NOTE + ASN are all RECALL, so a skill-pure reading of the board could have produced a one-wing draft, where `Σy = 1` forces `y = 1 > 0.75` and the whole fixed point evaporates. G12 #11.)

**Sealed at the board AND inside a brief window.** A brief press is one atomic move: the student stages it on the screen, submits it, and *then* the guard redraws from the same published distribution, pinned to `${jobSeed}|brief${n}` so pressing twice or reloading cannot re-roll it. The machine enforces four refusals — `repress-step` (more than one token moved in one call), `repress-spent` (a second lift in the same window), `repress-lift-first` (a place before a lift) and `repress-unavailable` (fewer than `GUARD.tokens` down). **A press that moves nothing, and a lift that places nothing, are not options**, because a free redraw is weakly dominant and therefore not a decision (G11). The round-3 form of this redrew on the **LIFT**, which published the new wing before the freed token was placed — and because the redraw seed is pinned, the second half of the two-step then faced a wing known with certainty. **The module-level residual is named rather than closed:** `state.press` still permits lift-then-place as two calls, because `tests/job-state-r3.test.mjs` pins that shape at module level; the law lives in `screens/job.js` for as long as that pin stands, and the screen is where the suite asserts it.

A token is worth `+0.25×` on that wing's loot **only if the wing is unguarded**. The House guards exactly one wing, drawn from a **published distribution** `y = project((1−ε)·x̂ + ε·uniform_n, cap)`, with `cap = 0.75` for `n = 3` and `n = 2` alike. The bars and the percentages are on screen before you press. On job 1 there is no `x̂`, so the distribution is uniform and the board prints `no data — uniform 1/3` (no fabricated rating, no cold-start lie).

**The cap projection, stated so it can be implemented** (capping alone left `Σy ≠ 1` and failed J3's own acceptance — G12 #10):

```
project(y, cap):                     // cap-and-share; terminates in ≤ n−1 passes
  loop:
    over = { i : y_i > cap }
    if over is empty: return y
    excess = Σ_{i∈over} (y_i − cap)
    for i in over: y_i = cap
    free = { i : i not capped }      // an index that has been capped STAYS capped
    if Σ_{i∈free} y_i > 0: distribute excess over `free` in proportion to current y_i
    else:                  distribute excess over `free` uniformly
```

Implemented literally with `free = { i : i ∉ over }` the same limit is still reached, but by asymptote rather than by retirement: 40 passes on `(0.60, 0.25, 0.10, 0.05)` at `cap = 0.30` and 92 on `(0.50, 0.30, 0.15, 0.05)` at `cap = 0.28`, against 2 for the shipped loop — so the `≤ n−1` bound belongs to the capped-stays-capped form and this block is the definition. “Water-filling” is also a loose name for it: the excess is shared in **proportion** to the uncapped entries, not levelled. On `(0.90, 0.08, 0.02)` at `cap = 0.75` this rule gives `(0.750, 0.200, 0.050)` where `y_i = min(cap, y_i + λ)` would give `(0.750, 0.155, 0.095)`.

Worked, and pinned in J3: `n = 3`, `ε = 0.10`, `x̂ = (1,0,0)` → `(0.9333, 0.0333, 0.0333)` → one pass → **`(0.750, 0.125, 0.125)`, Σ = 1.000**. `n = 2`, `ε = 0.10`, `x̂ = (1,0)` → `(0.95, 0.05)` → **`(0.75, 0.25)`**. The pseudo-code above is what the Settings panel prints, because the panel claims to print the real formula.

**`x̂` is stake-weighted, so it cannot be farmed with throwaway jobs.** Unweighted token shares over the last 10 jobs let a player press the wing they do not care about through three 6-target RUNs (free), walk the guard onto it, then press the wing they do care about on the VAULT. So:

```
x̂_i = ( Σ_j ω_j · share_ij + β/n ) / ( Σ_j ω_j + β ) ,   ω_j = min( posted_j , 0.25 · Σ_k posted_k )
β   = max( 0 , max(ω)/0.25 − Σω )     // uniform ballast: added until the largest job IS 25 % of the window
```

so `ω_j / (Σω + β) ≤ 0.25` for every job at every window size, job 1 included. The ballast was in the code and in neither the document nor the Settings panel: without `β` the ratio above puts **52 %** of the window on one job in the 3-RUN + 1-VAULT attack named below and **85 %** in a window of nine tiny jobs, and the next sentence would be false. A RUN contributes about a fifth of a VAULT, and no single job may contribute more than 25 % of the window. (`X_HAT_FORMULA` in `js/job/guard.js` is the law, exported from the implementation and executed against `xHatFrom` in `tests/job-guard.test.mjs` §5, so the printed shape and the running shape are one object; Settings renders that export rather than a hand-written string. G12 #12.)

Marginal value of a token on wing *i* is `0.25·v_i·(1 − y_i)`, where `v_i = Σ_targets L · scope · cold` is the wing's **study value** (`cold` is now the capped, interval-scaled term of G2). Best response: dump tokens on `argmax v_i(1−y_i)`; doing that drives `y_i → 1`, the payoff decays, you switch. **Fixed point** (support size *n*): `v_i(1−y_i) = k` for all *i* in support with `Σy_i = 1` ⟹ `k = (n−1)/Σ(1/v_i)`.

Both bullets below are the **ε = 0 closed form** — the idealisation, stated with its condition rather than as the shipped behaviour. At every rank the game actually ships, the target is the ε-floored equilibrium instead (an unpressed wing sits at `ε/n`; it never leaves the support), and the shipped ten-job × three-token dynamic settles at `(7/11, 4/11)` and `(4/11, 4/11, 3/11)` when a tied argmax is settled by wing order — stable to 5 000 jobs, because ten jobs pressed in three whole tokens tie `v_i(1 − y_i)` exactly on most jobs.

- Equal values `(v,v,v)`, ε = 0: `k = 2v/3`, `y = (⅓,⅓,⅓)` — pure rotation. Shipped: `(4/11, 4/11, 3/11)`, 0.061 off uniform.
- `v = (30,20,10)`, ε = 0: wing 3 leaves the support; `n = 2`, `k = 12.0`, `y = (0.60, 0.40)`. Shipped: `(7/11, 4/11)`, 0.036 off.

**Three different vectors live in this paragraph and the document used to print one of them under all three names.** What the board pre-presses is `pᵢ = A − B/vᵢ`, `A = (1 − ε/n)/(2(1 − ε))`, `B` set so `Σp = 1` — the best reply to the guard this layer actually ships, whose `y` is a published mirror of your own last 10 jobs and not a minimiser. `p` never presses a wing harder than one **a token is worth more on**, and is positive on every wing **the press keeps** — a wing worth too little to press leaves that support exactly as a wing worth too little to defend leaves the guard's, and so does a wing the guard is CERTAIN to take, however high its study value (`pressAdvice` zeroes it as `vPress` before the press is solved, because a token there pays `guardMult` rather than the token bonus). A drop on a four-wing board is the ordinary case and not a corner (**ROUND 5**: measured through the shipped `postBoard` over 500 seeded saves, a support wing is pressed at 0 on **55.6 %** of four-wing boards; the real board `v = (58.5, 131.25, 71.25, 18)` presses `(0.25, 0.44, 0.31, 0)` at every rank. The sentence used to read "positive on every wing the composer deals", which is false on 17 % of live boards, and the only board the suite checked it on was the hand-made three-wing one where the drop cannot fire). So spreading pressure across the wings means across the wings the press keeps. Against a guard that *did* read the press and minimise, the unexploitable press is the **maximin** `xᵢ ∝ 1/vᵢ` — the reciprocal ordering, most weight on the LOWEST-value wing, because that is what equalises `xᵢ·vᵢ`. Neither of them is `yᵢ = 1 − k/vᵢ`, which is the **HOUSE's** mixing (§3.4 derives it from the player's indifference condition, and the side a condition pins is the other one). The three are `PRESS_FORMULA`, `MAXIMIN_FORMULA` and `GUARD_MIX_FORMULA` in `js/job/guard.js`, each executed against its own function over 600 random boards at every rank's ε; on `v = (30, 20, 10)` at the default rank (ε = 0.20) they are `(0.447, 0.379, 0.174)`, `(0.400, 0.600, 0)` and `(0.600, 0.400, 0)`, and pressing the House's mix is worth **8.0** of an available **12.0**. The ε floor guarantees no cluster becomes dead weight; the 0.75 cap guarantees the guard is never a certainty **on a board with two or more wings in play that you have not already walked out on** — the two exceptions are printed before the press and are in G4 “Mercy”: a held board, and a blocked wing on a two-wing board. (**ROUND 5**: the support size is a third condition, not an exception to hunt for. A board whose whole due list is one make posts ONE wing — `n·cap = 0.75 < 1`, so `projectWithPasses` has nothing feasible to project onto and returns `y = 1` with neither `blocked` nor `held` set. The board already prints that certainty before the press — "one wing tonight · <wing> · no press" — so it was only the legend's enumeration that was short. `CAP_PANEL_COPY` states the bound on boards with two or more wings and names the one-wing board; §6b of `tests/job-guard.test.mjs` sweeps `n ∈ {1,2,3,4}` rather than `{2,3,4}`.) Guarded wings pay `guardMult(rank)` instead of the token bonus and carry `wing_pen = 2` on misses, so walking into the guard with bold calls is the high-variance line — available, priced, never forced.

### 3.5 Elo — difficulty from YOUR rating, volume from the plan

`E_player = 1/(1 + 10^((R_house − R_player)/400))`, `K = 24`, outcome `1` if `BAGGED ≥ posted` else `0`, both ratings updating symmetrically. Both seed at `clamp(1000 + 4·(placementScore − 50), 800, 1400)` for `R_player` and a flat **1000** for `R_house`; **if placement is skipped (S7 allows it) both seed at 1000, and Settings says so** (G12 #40c).

| dial | driven by | range | what it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| vault grade | **`R_player`** | `< 1000` → tier ≤ 2 · `1000–1199` → tier 3 · `≥ 1200` → tier 4 | the difficulty of the final all-in only |
| guard multiplier | rank | ×0.50 → ×0.75 | how much a guarded wing costs you |
| call ladder | rank | 3 → 4 rungs | how deep a hole a beginner can dig (answer: shallow) |

**The vault grade is driven by `R_player`, which rises when you win.** Symmetric Elo moves `R_house` *down* on a player win, so driving the vault grade from `R_house` made a strong player's final target get easier with every win, and made the flow-control adjustment indistinguishable in sign from losing. `R_house` survives only as the symmetric opponent number on the Ledger panel. J3 asserts: two simulated win streaks **raise** the vault grade; two loss streaks lower it. (G12 #8.)

**`R_player` never touches which reviews are due — that is Leitner's job alone.** A stronger student therefore faces the same number of problems at a higher vault grade, not more problems, which is what keeps every shape inside 10–25 minutes at every rating.

**Flow control, printed never silent:** after two consecutive jobs with `BAGGED < 0.5·posted`, **`R_player −40`** and the next board opens with a **FOOTHOLD** — contract 1 is three tier-1 dues, guard ×0.5, stated on the board.

### 3.6 Every random object in the layer, with its real odds

There are exactly four, all seeded from `js/rng.js` (`no-random.test.mjs` already forbids `Math.random`), all replayable, all printed *before* the decision they affect, and all of them multiply both branches identically.

| object | distribution | where it is printed |
|---|---|---|
| the guard draw | `y = project((1−ε)x̂ + ε·uniform_n, 0.75)`, ε by rank | three bars with percentages, above the token row, before you press |
| the ×2 posting | **independent `p = 1/6` per target**, drawn by `cyrb53(dateISO \| jobIndex \| targetIndex)` | a `×2` mark on the envelope **before the call**, the realised count on the board (`×2: 2 targets`) |
| the bundle partition | seeded partition of `composePage`'s queue with critical replication and draft-time dedupe | the **board sheet**: the exact remaining composition of every contract, open at all times, so the draw distribution is exactly computable by the player |
| which Variant a generated target is | the existing seeded generators, unchanged | the seed tag in the card corner (existing) |

The ×2 is seeded per **job index**, not per day, so after job 1 the placement is not already known; and it is defined as an independent 1-in-6 per target rather than "exactly one target in six", which was undefined for an 8- or 10-target shape and untestable (G12 #30). Expected count on a JOB-10 is 1.67; the realised count is computed and printed before the draft. J5 asserts the per-target rate is 1/6 ± 0.01 over 10⁴ day seeds.

That is the entire use of variable-ratio reinforcement in this design, and it is stripped of every property that makes a slot machine: no hidden odds, no post-hoc reveal, no near-miss theatre, no escalation, bounded at ×2, stake-only.

### 3.7 Anti-exploit proofs

**(1) Luck never flips an outcome.** No random term appears in any payoff function. The guard and the ×2 multiply both the clear and the miss branch identically. The bundle partition **does** change which legal item you face next — that is stated in Global law 3 rather than denied — but never how it is graded, and every item in every bundle came from `composePage` (G12 #29).

**(2) A wrong answer can never beat a right one.** For fixed `(L, m, scope, wing, call)`: a clear pays `+L·ρ_eff·m·W·… ≥ 0` with `W ≥ 1.0`; a miss pays `−min(LOOSE, L·m·P·wing_pen) ≤ 0` and sets `chain → 0`, weakly lowering every future payout. Rating: clear credit `10 − 40(1−p)² ≥ 0` for every `p ≥ 0.5`; miss credit `10 − 40p² ≤ 0`. So `clear ≥ miss` pointwise in both currencies. **The caveat that matters** (pushluck): monotonicity requires the policy to be *revisable* at every beat — under a frozen threshold policy there are orders where a miss pays more. The engine therefore (a) offers BAG/PUSH at every single target, (b) auto-bags at the getaway, (c) prints `1 target left — a chain of 4 bags at 13.1` one beat early, and (d) ships `tests/job-monotone.test.mjs`, which brute-forces all `2ⁿ` outcome vectors for `n ≤ 11` under optimal play and asserts the final score is monotone non-decreasing in the number of clears **on every shape the composer deals** — 0 violations in 46 080 miss→clear flips over RUN 6 · JOB 10 · VAULT 7 at 8 seeds each (138 240 at 24 seeds, off-suite).

> **Scope of (2), as measured — integration ruling on notes/J9.md F2.** The clause above is scoped to the dealt shapes because the unqualified claim is FALSE and the formula is the authority: `m_chain` multiplies the **miss** branch as well as the clear branch (G2), so an order in which a *cheap* clear precedes an *expensive* miss can lower the optimum — the clear is worth `+5` and it makes the next miss cost `+18`. Measured over arbitrary orders, all `2ⁿ` vectors, `n ≤ 11`: **0.334 % of flips — 137 of 40 962 — at most 2.32 % of the bag**, worst absolute drop **22**, on an *eleven*-target order. (**This blockquote published 0.190 % / 1.92 % for one round after verify round 2 widened it**, while the paragraph in G2 three hundred lines above published the corrected pair and `tests/job-monotone.test.mjs` asserted a two-sided band the stale numeral sat outside. Corrected in verify round 3, test-integrity; the same test now reads all five numerals in this sentence — rate, worst relative, worst absolute, violations, flips — back out of this paragraph and compares each with the sweep it just ran, so the two cannot disagree again.) The four-target counterexample `tests/job-monotone.test.mjs` keeps **frozen** is the smallest exhibit, not the worst one: it drops **16**, `476 → 460` — it was `483 → 467` before the econ lane moved the call's premium onto `missFor`'s base, and the drop is unchanged at 16. (It was 0.234 % of flips on the pre-COVER economy, on an order whose pinned outcome vector the cover repaired outright — 331 → 336. The cover narrows this exception; it does not remove it, which is why the clause stays scoped.) J9 offered three resolutions; (b) "drop `m_chain` from the miss branch" rewrites §3.2's whole threshold table and (c) "cap the miss branch's `m_chain` at the chain entering the run of clears" makes the chain history-dependent, so **(a) scope the claim** is taken. Both halves — zero violations on the dealt shapes, and the bounded exception off them — are asserted in `tests/job-monotone.test.mjs`, and the pointwise statement above (a clear pays ≥ a miss **at its own beat**) holds without qualification in 12 000 comparisons.

**(3) Easy-card farming.** Five independent brakes. (a) Loot per minute is monotone non-decreasing in tier on both published rows (12.0/12.0/12.7/14.0 answer-only, 8.2/9.5/10.9/12.5 as experienced), so tier-1 spam is the *worst* carry rate available. (b) `xp.scopeFor` pays 0.5 on mastered makes and 0 on the Bonus Bank. (c) The rating window takes **only informative calls (`w ≥ 0.25`)**, so mastered-material calls never enter it and the unfilled slots hold the rating at exactly 5.00 — **true as arithmetic, false as the brake this document used to call it.** An empty window is *no measurement*, not a ceiling: it is what an honest master prints too, and it is the exact mechanism of the rank demotion the ratchet closes (G2 “Rank”). The brake it was credited with is the ratchet, and the label “the farmer's ceiling” is withdrawn (G11). (d) **Decay** (conquest): a target pays `cold ≤ 1.5` only while it is due, and a clean answer advances the bucket, so the renewable loot rate from a fixed card set is `Σ_c L_c/I(b_c)` with `I = [0,1,2,4,7,14]` — **strictly decreasing as mastery rises**, and raisable either by clearing *more of the packet* — Readiness's own `C` term — **or by dropping buckets, which raises the stock rate and LOWERS realised loot, because a job serves a fixed number of targets and a thrown one pays 0.** “Raisable only by clearing more of the packet” was false and is withdrawn. Measured over 14 simulated days × 70 cards at throw rates 0 → 1/8 → 1/4 → 1/2, four directions are asserted at once: the take falls at every step, the mean Leitner bucket falls at every step, the renewable rate rises at 1/8 and again at 1/4 and ends at least **1.3×** its honest value, and the honest arm's own rate decays over the same fortnight — so the rise is a property of the throws and not of the calendar. The mechanism carries its own control: remove the fixed target count, serve every due card however many that is, and the same lever **pays**. The lever exists; it is a stock lever, and against a job of fixed length it is priced against you. (e) `tell` retires on resolution and on sealing, so a stockpile of deliberate errors is a depreciating asset. Test: 200 jobs of tier-1-only mastered play leave `rating == 5.00` exactly and loot/hour below the mixed-play baseline; a 400-day simulation with no new cards shows monotone non-increasing loot/day.

**(4) Re-answering knowns.** Structural, not economic: carry and rating accrue **only** on targets the job composer drew from the schedule (`role ∈ {review, rematch, new, weak, floor}`, `page.js ROLES`) and served out of `inProgress.game`'s own queue. **The mechanism is the call graph, not a field on the item.** `state.applyTarget` is the only function that writes LOOSE, the chain or a `calls[]` entry; `screens/job.js:1174` is its only caller in the tree, and it runs the job screen's queue. The only other `call.callEntry` call site under `site/js/` is `screens/mock.js applyMockCall` — the Mock's single prediction per run, which has no make and therefore no q̂ and enters the window at `w = min(4ŝ(1−ŝ), 0.25)`, where ŝ is the mean score of its own trailing ten papers — it has no MAKE and therefore no `q̂`, but its weight is **measured off that history rather than defined**, and capped at the smallest weight the window counts, so one Mock slot can never pay more than **2.50** of Σ(w·c). It is **0** on a first-ever paper and 0 whenever ŝ leaves the informative band (`screens/mock.js mockCallWeight`; verify r3 — this document published `w = 1.0` at six sites while the shipped cap was 0.25, and `screens/settings.js` rendered the 1.0 to the student, a 4× overstatement of the app's only calibration surface outside a job), and is not a card answer at all. So there is no code path from any other surface to a `calls[]` entry or to LOOSE. (**Round-4 repair:** this proof used to cite a field `drawnBy: 'job'` as the guarantee. `grep -rn "drawnBy" site/ tests/` returns **zero** hits — it exists on no queue item, in no serialiser and in no test, and never did; it was inherited from `designs/game-heist.md:272`. The claim was true and its stated defence was fictional, which is worse than a false claim, because it reads as checked. G12 #75.) A card opened from the Binder, an Infinite Variant, a Drill 5, BLITZ and Full 36 pay **zero carry and zero rating** — they pay exactly the XP they pay today. There is no surface on which to re-farm a known card because the game's numbers do not exist outside the job queue. J5 additionally asserts **no job contains two targets of the same card** (the dedupe of G12 #17) and that a 30-run simulated week never repeats a card inside its Leitner interval.

**(5) Clock stalling.** `tick = one answered target`; no term in any payoff reads elapsed time (`job-exploit.test.mjs` greps `js/job/*.js` for `Date`, `now`, `elapsed`, `ms` outside display code and replays a full job with every `ms × 10` asserting byte-identical output). The one real stall exploit — *solve it, then call 95* — is closed by the sealed envelope: the call is locked before the stem renders. And no pre-call surface names the EV-max rung (Global law 6), so the call measures self-knowledge rather than button-following; J2 greps every pre-call template for `argmaxCall`.

**(6) Quit-scumming.** At any state, BAG-and-continue dominates BAG-and-quit: after bagging, `LOOSE = 0` and the call-50 option has payoff `q·L·ρ̄ ≥ 0` with **zero** downside, so continuing has non-negative EV in every state and quitting forfeits the +10 % completion bonus. Quitting also auto-bags at 50 %, so it is never *better* than banking. And the job seed is pinned in `inProgress.game`, so re-opening restores the same guard draw, the same bundle partition and the same ×2 placement: **no reroll exists.** That pin defeats RELOAD-scumming; the other half is WALK-scumming, and it was free until round 1 — `endJob` logs every outcome, `jobIndexFor` counted log entries and the day's seed is `job|profile|day|jobIndex`, so one keystroke at the board (W → Leave) re-rolled the guard wing, the partition, the ×2 placement and the posted value at a cost of nothing: twelve walks gave twelve distinct boards, ×2 counts 0…5 and posted 177…279, and walking until the board showed ≥ 3 ×2 marks lifted mean posted from 225 to 319. **The day's job index now counts only jobs that answered a target** (`targets > 0`), so a walk that answered nothing re-posts the byte-identical board — asserted over eight consecutive walks in `tests/job-board.test.mjs` (“WALK-SCUMMING IS DEAD”). A walk that *answered* a target is a job on record and the next board is a new one, which is what G3.6 asks for (the ×2 is seeded per job index so that after job 1 the placement is not already known); best-of-16 over a day's indices is worth **+46 to +62 %** of the first board's posted value on the measured saves. **The second board is not free, and it is the page that holds the door, not the seed:** the walked job's targets came off Today's Page, so `startJob` refuses the next one with `page-in-progress: N left on Today's Page` until the residue is cleared by hand — nine unstaked answers on the measured save. Both halves are asserted beside the eight-walk test (“a walk that ANSWERED a target is a job on record — and the page is what holds the door”); the old sentence claimed the byte-identity for every walk. The corollary matters more: because a zero-downside call always exists, **there is no state from which a student is better off closing the app than answering one more problem.**

**(7) Draft-skipping a due review.** The 5 posted contracts are built from one priority pool. Every *critical* item (`bucket ≤ 2`, or `overdue ≥ 1 day`, or test-clamped) **that the shape has room for** is replicated into **≥ 3 of the 5** bundles, and any 3-of-5 draft therefore contains every critical in that **core**. The core is `targets − draft·u` locks (7 of a JOB-10's 10, with `u = 1` choice lock per contract — see G4), most overdue first, because a 10-target job cannot hold 14 dues: **the shape is the only thing that keeps a critical out of the core, and nothing is ever dropped.** Each of the three parts is named on the board object — `critical[]` (the core, in every draft), `criticalOptional[]` (posted as a choice lock, so a 3-of-5 draft carries 3 of the 5) and `deferred[]` (not posted tonight) — and their union is exactly the set of criticals the composer put on the Page. A critical that is not answered **stays due and returns on a later board**, rising on the overdue sort — 1 162 of 1 162 boards over 400 saves × every shape, with **4 of them crowded off the NEXT Page by `LIMITS.dues` and 14 not leading its review block (a tier-4 deferred critical never can: a hard item is never first)**, so "leads the next board" is withdrawn and the schedule is what is claimed (SPEC-CORRECTIONS A-5): measured end to end in `tests/job-board.test.mjs` ("a target a job does not reach stays due and LEADS the next board"), which answers a real drafted union through `markItem`/`finishPage` and re-composes the day. Round-1 correction: the sentence this replaces said *every* critical due, while the board capped them at `targets`; on 57 % of drafts a critical on the Page was not in the draft, and the test measured the capped list instead of the Page. Replicated items are **deduplicated at draft time** — a card appears exactly once in the answered union, never twice — and because that changes the arithmetic the board *already printed*, the board prints the drafted total net (`posted 105 (−5 shared)`, on its own line — the button's payout segment is the flat `posted 100`, VR2-POSTED) and each contract gross. Test: all `C(5,3) = 10` drafts × 50 seeded saves = 500 cases, **core** coverage 100 % (measured against the criticals on `composePage`'s own output, not against the board's list), `critical ⊎ criticalOptional ⊎ deferred = critical(Page)`, `Σ targets(drafted) == |union|`, and the printed net posted equals the post-dedupe value for all ten drafts. (G12 #17.)

**(8) Mastery tanking.** Could you keep a make weak because STEADY forgives more there? No: `E[ρ]` at m85 r0 is 0.940 against m40 r1's 0.725 — **mastering the make is worth `+0.378` of ρ against forgiveness's `+0.163`, and it frees the crew slot** (which is scarcer now that at most 12 makes may be manned). On top of that, every thrown card costs `qual = 0` XP instead of 1.5, drops a Leitner bucket, writes an error-log entry, and lowers Readiness. Any `(1 − m)` term in the layer reads the **start-of-day snapshot** of `m_shown`, so today's tanking cannot pay today.

**It does NOT lower `q̂` off the `w·E[c]` peak — for a student who knows the material it moves them ONTO it, and that clause is withdrawn.** On the reachable grid — the reachable `q̂` are `h/of` with **`of = min(RATING.qHatWindow, sittings on that make)`** — `qHatDetail` divides by the sittings the make HAS, not by the window, so a make reports sevenths until its eighth sitting and tenths only from its tenth, so the deciles this paragraph used to name are a ninth of it — `w·E[c]` is **0** at q̂ ≤ 0.6 (the honest rung is 50 and `creditOf(50)` is 0 both ways) and **0** at 1.0 (`w = 0 < 0.25`, so nothing enters the window at all), and it **peaks at 2.4980 at `q̂ = 6/7` — one thrown sitting in SEVEN**, ahead of 2.4630 (5/6), 2.4500 (7/8), 2.3660 (8/9) and the **2.2680 at 0.9** this paragraph published as the top. Throwing one in seven therefore walks a master from a blank window to essentially the CONTINUOUS ceiling 2.4998, not to the 91 % of it priced here — the channel is worth **10.1 % more** than the figure the residual below was stated against. What does NOT move is the rank prize: fifty honest slots are worth 9.996 at 6/7 and 9.536 at 9/10, and both are Called 5, so "a throw buys a tool already owned" is unaffected; it is the `w·E[c]` figure and the throw RATE that were wrong (verify r3, call-propriety).

**What dominates a throw is the RATCHET — for a student who has a rung to keep.** Rank never falls (G2 “Rank”), so the rung a student earned while learning is the rung they keep, and *for them* a throw buys a tool they already own: the whole residual prize is guardMult 0.75 against 0.70 on the guarded wing only, which is at most **2.5 %** overall and is asserted as a bound read straight off `RANKS` rather than typed. Against that, the throw costs the target itself, the chain, the XP, the bucket and the Readiness — measured through the shipped `econ.settle` over eight seeded weeks, realised carry is **non-increasing in the throw rate at a common rank and strictly down by φ = 1/8**, the measured cost at 1/8 is asserted **above 9 %** and above **3×** the rank prize, and at a fixed true ability a student who throws one target in two banks **under 60 %** of honest loot on 8 of 8 seeds.

**The rank half of that is conditional on the student having a rung, and the measurement above holds the rank COMMON across its arms.** Every arm of the throw-rate pin prices its targets at a pinned `rank: 3` — which is exactly what lets the throw's *cost* be measured apart from the rank *prize*, and is also why none of those arms can see a student for whom the rank is not common. That student exists and is ordinary: someone who is already competent on the unit when they meet it. Their window is empty (`w = 0.116 < 0.25` at q̂ = 0.97), their rating is exactly 5.00 and their rank is Called 2; a throw rate that keeps material inside the informative band moves a full window to **9.536 · Called 5**, and the ratchet holds it. For them the throw buys the rung outright rather than re-buying one they own, and the ≤ 2.5 % bound above does not price it. **This is the case the claim does not cover, and it is published here rather than left to a later round** (G2 “what persists”, G12 #74).

**VERIFY ROUND 2: the throw that buys the rank need not be a job target at all — and then it costs nothing this document prices.** `call.qHatFor` reads `save.cards[*].history`, and `screens/card.js` pushes an entry there on **every graded original**, a staked job target and a free Today's Page sitting alike. So `w = 4q̂(1−q̂)` moves on evidence the game layer never charges for: a card failed outside a job pays no carry, no chain and no rating — proof 4's own result, *there is no carry and no rating without `inProgress.game`* — while every cost the paragraph above prices (“the target itself, the chain, the XP, the bucket and the Readiness”) is measured on a throw that **is** a staked target. The first two terms are zero for the throw an exploiter actually makes. Measured over two arms with the same start save, **every job target cleared in both**, the same call policy (honest rung for jobs 0–7, then the top rung the rank unlocked), differing **only** in the `ok` flag on free study sittings — `node notes/repair-meta-evidence.mjs` §1, 8 seeds × 24 jobs: the honest master is **Called 2 on 8 of 8 seeds, for ever**; the student who marks one free sitting in five wrong is **Called 4 on 8 of 8** and banks **+6.91 % post-climb loot** (+2.98 % overall), winning both currencies on 6 of 8 — with `cards` box / due / reps / lapses / ease / rarity, `skills` and `xp` **byte-identical between the arms on 8 of 8** (asserted in the script, printed per seed), because no `schedule.applyOutcome` runs on a sitting that is only a history entry. The published residual, “at most **2.5 %** overall”, prices a different action and is **1.19× too small** for this one on the overall basis it is stated on (2.98 % measured against a 2.5 % bound) and **2.76×** too small on the post-climb basis, which is the currency the climb is actually spent in. **The pair in this sentence was published as +11.6 % / +5.6 % — and the multiple as 4.6× — for one round while the script cited beside it printed 6.91 % / 2.98 %; the numbers here are that script's, re-run in verify round 3 (test-integrity), and `tests/job-meta-constants.test.mjs` §8 now spawns it and reads these numerals back out of this document, so no figure in this paragraph can outlive its own evidence again.** **The cap does not stop it, and the reason is the thing to keep: the student is not lying about their report — they are choosing their evidence, and then reporting it honestly.** The finding also carried a second half, and that one is now **CLOSED**: `slotCeiling` used to resolve `w`'s two roots by taking the flattering one, so a *chosen* q̂ = 0.2 at the 85 call was priced exactly as an earned q̂ = 0.8 (measured at Called 3/4, the same band). The call lane landed the save-schema change while this round was open — `callEntry` stores `q`, `slotCeiling` uses it when present — and §2 of the same script now reads **ceiling 0.00 · Called 2 at q̂ ≤ 0.5**, against 8.28 / 6.76 · Called 4 at q̂ = 0.8. **§1 is byte-for-byte unchanged by that fix**, which is the whole point: the cap prices the REPORT, and a sandbagger's report is true. **The mechanic that would close the remaining half prices the action instead of the report: build `q̂` only from sittings that were themselves staked job targets, so manufacturing evidence costs what the throw-rate pin already measures.** That is a `js/job/call.js` change, it is **OPEN**, and it is filed as a Request in `notes/repair-meta.md`. Until it lands, this document does not claim the rank is unfarmable (G9 #6 says so in the criterion itself), G3.8 #4's “only path” is withdrawn (G11), and Settings prints the mechanic and its real cost in the rating panel's **the evidence** row. (G12, “Verify round 2 — the meta lane”, entry **VR2-FARM**.)

**Tanking is dominated in the two currencies that gate a tool — the rank and the carry — for a student who earned their rank on the way up, and this document no longer says “strictly dominated” full stop.** What it is not dominated in is the *printed rating*, against one particular counterfactual: an honest master whose makes are all mastered generates no informative calls at all and prints exactly 5.00, while a student who throws occasionally keeps material inside the informative band and keeps a window to be measured on. `player.records.bestRating20` and the `calibrated` trophy read the printed number, so that is published rather than claimed away (G2 “what persists”). Note what IS asserted about the rating, because it is the stronger half: at a fixed true ability, with informative calls on both sides, throwing scores a **strictly lower** rating on 8 of 8 seeds — the weight is sealed at the call, so a chosen outcome cannot buy a better weight.

**(9) Call-tanking.** Calling 50 forever scores exactly 5.0 (credit 0 both ways) → Called 2 forever, no 95 call, guard ×0.55, and the carry ladder pays `W = 1.0`. Deliberately *mis*-calling is worse: the rule is strictly proper, so every lie has negative expected credit, and the fixed-`N` divisor means a run of bad calls is not diluted by padding. **The brake on systematic over-calling is the CARRY ladder, not the rating** — the rank is a ratchet and no longer falls with the rating, so the price of calling 95 on material you clear half the time is `P(95) = 5.0`, i.e. `min(LOOSE, L·m·5·wing_pen)` = `18 × 1.8 × 5 = 162` on a tier-2 at chain 4: a whole job's bag on one target. **And that sentence pointed the wrong way until verify round 2 on every target the board dresses, which is most of them.** The `162` was charged on the LOSS set and the matching prize was paid on the GAIN set, so `scope · wing · cold · tell` bought upside and no downside: on a due review at `scope 1.25`, `cold 1.50` with two tokens, the carry argmax was one rung ABOVE the honest one at every `q` in `[0.50, 0.90]`. The ladder is the brake as claimed only because G2's clear branch now pays the call's premium on the STAKE — see “The clear branch has TWO terms” there for the measurement, the repair and what it cost. The rating still falls to 0.00 on that policy; it no longer takes the rank with it, and the old attribution (“systematic over-calling drives the rating that gates it to 0”, offered as *the* brake) is withdrawn.

> **And that brake was dodgeable until verify round 1, which is why the sentence above may be read as written only with the next one beside it.** `min(LOOSE, ·)` is 0 at `LOOSE = 0`, BAG sets `LOOSE = 0`, and BAG is offered at every beat but the last — so the maximising policy was *bag, then call the top rung*, on which the `162` is paid **zero times**: +95.6 % over honest play at `q = 0.50`, on 16 of 16 seeded boards, measured end to end through `state.startJob → lockCall → applyTarget → bag → endJob`. G3.1 now carries **the cover** that closes it: `W_eff = W(50) + (W_call − W(50))·min(1, LOOSE/|nominal miss|)`, i.e. the premium is paid only in the proportion the price is collectable. With it the ladder *is* the brake as claimed — a pile deep enough to collect the premium is a pile deep enough to pay the `162`, and a pile too shallow to pay it buys no premium to dodge with. `tests/job-exploit.test.mjs` pins the realised take of "max call + bag every beat" at or below honest play at `q ∈ {0.50, 0.65}`; on the shipped economy it was 95.6 % above it.
