## G7 Integration and build plan

### Reused unchanged (zero edits)

`site/js/grader/*` · `site/js/gen/*` (**existing files**; one new file is added — see below) · `site/js/figure/*` · `site/js/widgets/*` · `site/js/mathfmt.js` · `site/js/rng.js` (`cyrb53`, `mulberry32`, `rngFrom` seed every draw) · `site/js/xp.js` (`xpFor`, `scopeFor`, `comboTransition`, `comboPitch`, `levelFor` — read, never written) · `site/js/mastery.js` · `site/js/schedule.js` (`dueList`, `overdueDays`, `intervalDays`, `applyOutcome`, `isMastered`, `mShown`, `needsMet`, `pendingRematches`, `freezeVariant`, `clampDue`) · `site/js/readiness.js` (read-only) · `site/js/rarity.js` · `site/js/days.js` (`todayISO`, `daysUntilTest`, `isQuietHours`, `testMoment`) · `site/data/cards/*`, `data/cards.js`, `data/skills.js`, `data/modules.js`, `data/sheets.js`, `data/blueprint.js`, `data/misconceptions.js` · `site/js/screens/card.js` (**the only place a grade is written** — the job mounts its answering body as-is and must not duplicate one line of it) · `screens/night.js`, `screens/sheet.js`, `screens/binder.js`, `screens/report.js`, `screens/onboard.js`.

Per BUILD-POLICY §1 there is no `teacherKey`, no `orig`, no `site/content/` and no PNG anywhere in this layer.

### Extended (small, additive, line by line)

| file | change |
|---|---|
| `site/js/page.js` | `+ composeBundles(save, opts) → {bundles[5], critical[], supply, minutes}` (partitions the existing queue with critical replication), `+ draftUnion(bundles, picks)` (dedupe → `spreadSkills` → tier ramp) and `+ jobBudget(shape)`. **`composePage` itself is untouched** — the FUNCTION is byte-identical to pre-ticket (`git diff` removes exactly two lines from `page.js`, both `import` statements), and the composed PAGE reproduces pre-ticket for the same seed **except on the 0.65 % of saves where the J5b registry entry changes what the weak slot resolves to: 13 of 2 000, 12 that draw `T-asn-reason` and one whose Page comes out an item shorter** (SPEC-CORRECTIONS A-4). |
| `site/js/screens/run.js` | one `KIND_META` entry `job: { title: 'The Job', back: '/today', mode: 'card' }` and one `DELEGATES` entry `job: { mod: './job.js', fns: ['mountJob'], fallback: 'stub' }` — the same pattern `baseline`/`night` already use. **`ROUTE_PATTERNS` stays 13**; the job is `#/run/job?seed=`. Plus the **debrief** extension to the Page Summary (owned by J6b). |
| `site/js/plan.js` | `nextAction` gains one branch: when `modeFor() === 'page'` and `settings.game !== false` and `D ≥ 2`, the primary label becomes `JOB · N targets · ~M min · ends HH:MM · S % game`; at `D = 2` it becomes `REVIEW BOARD`; at `D ≤ 1` unchanged (night/morning win). |
| `site/js/screens/home.js` | the Board panel above the primary button + the cold-crew strip, **painted in two passes** (see below). |
| `site/js/screens/app.js` | during a job, hide `hdr-level`, `hdr-streak`, `hdr-xp`, `hdr-combo`; show LOOSE · BAG · chain. Five items, pinned. |
| `site/js/screens/stats.js` | three panels: Ledger (records, both Elo numbers), Fault Index (68 cells), reliability + calibration beside the existing Mock-prediction line. Plus the crew allocation grid with the `manned ≤ min(capacity, 12)` counter. No new route. |
| `site/js/screens/settings.js` | `game` toggle (default on, one switch kills the layer), and five printed-formula panels in the style of "How Readiness is computed": **How the Call is scored** (both ladders, all indifference points, the two disagreement bands, **and the EV-max table — the one place it is allowed to appear before a call, because it is not attached to a target**), **How the Guard draws** (`y = project((1−ε)x̂ + ε·uniform, 0.75)` with the water-filling pseudo-code and the stake weighting of `x̂`), **How the payout ladder works** (ρ, crew forgiveness, the HELD chain-hold at chain ≥ 3), **How posted is computed** (`L`, scope, the capped `cold`, the live-only `tell`, ×2 at 1-in-6 per target), **How the rating is computed** (`5 + 2Σ(w·c)/50`, the `w ≥ 0.25` informative gate, and why an empty slot scores neutral). |
| `site/js/screens/boss.js` | the VAULT shape's final target delegates here; hearts, CONTINUE? and KO semantics unchanged. |
| `site/js/screens/mock.js` | **no calls, no stakes, no crew, no chain — a test is a test.** Its existing prediction slider is scored by `job/call.js credit()` and enters the rating window as one call at `w = min(4ŝ(1−ŝ), 0.25)`, where ŝ is the mean score of its own trailing ten papers — it has no MAKE and therefore no `q̂`, but its weight is **measured off that history rather than defined**, and capped at the smallest weight the window counts, so one Mock slot can never pay more than **2.50** of Σ(w·c). It is **0** on a first-ever paper and 0 whenever ŝ leaves the informative band (`screens/mock.js mockCallWeight`; verify r3 — this document published `w = 1.0` at six sites while the shipped cap was 0.25, and `screens/settings.js` rendered the 1.0 to the student, a 4× overstatement of the app's only calibration surface outside a job), and Settings prints that law (G12 #40d). |
| `site/js/store.js` | `SAVE_VERSION 1 → 2`, `MIGRATIONS[1]` adds `save.player` and `save.game` with defaults; `CAPS.game = { calls: 50, log: 30, tags: 68, bundles: 5 }`; `KEPT_KEYS += 'player'`, `ARCHIVED_KEYS += 'game'`. |
| `site/js/sound.js` | four cues appended to `CUES`. |
| `site/data/templates.js` | **one new entry**, `T-asn-reason`, pointing at the new generator (J5b). This is the only content-adjacent edit in the layer. |
| `site/data/trophies.js` | six pure predicates: `crew-held` (every wing has a manned, non-idle crew mark at board time), `index-25`, `index-68`, `chain-8`, `calibrated` (rolling Brier ≤ 0.10 over 20 informative calls), `clean-getaway`. |
| `site/css/screens.css` | one appended `/* === G-job === */ … /* === /G-job === */` block per BUILD-POLICY §2. |
| `site/index.html` | **one line**: `<link rel="stylesheet" href="css/job.css">`. (The file was linked from nowhere in the previous draft — G12 #28.) |
| `site/sw.js` | the new files added to the precache list (`sw.test.mjs` enforces it). |

**Home paints the board in two passes** (G12 #20). `tests/home-r2.test.mjs:147-148` asserts Home has **no static import** of `page.js` or `plan.js` — they carry 233 KB of cards and 311 KB of generators, and that test exists because S9 #1's paint budget already fails at 2.8 s. So a board that needs `composePage` cannot be painted "from save state alone":

- **Pass 1 (static, from `store` + `schedule.dueList` + `data/job.js`):** the week line, the row frame at its full reserved height, the dues count, the days cold, whether a cold-crew box is possible at all, and the shape's NAME only when the week itself settled it (REVIEW BOARD, school window). **Nothing per-contract and nothing per-draft.** A contract's label, its wing, its lock count, its cold days, its posted value, the per-wing supply, `~N min`, the end time and the projected split are ALL `--muted` placeholders of their final width, because every one of them needs `data/cards.js` — the import G12 #21 forbids — or tonight's own draft. `tests/job-week.test.mjs` asserts pass 1 prints `null` for every per-draft numeral on every week state and seed that posts a board, and that no spinner and no `aria-busy` exists.
- **Pass 2 (after the dynamic `page.js` import, on the same frame budget as the existing primary button):** **every** numeral is written in place — the labels, lock counts, cold days, posted values, the per-wing supply and the whole meta line — because `job/board.js` is the only thing that knows tonight's draft.

**This list used to give pass 1 the meta line, and that was the defect, not a simplification.** Measured over 16 (seed × ledger) boards and recorded in `screens/home.js`, pass 1's shape-table estimate missed the board that replaced it by up to **13 points of split** and **6 minutes** (a 1.46× wall clock), and on **2 of the 16** it did not even name the same shape. G1 statement 1 is *the board prints your own number, not the brochure's*; the published row was no longer reachable from `screens/home.js` at all.

J13 asserts **no layout shift between passes and no spinner** — the numerals occupy their final width as `--muted` placeholders in pass 1 — rather than the unimplementable "no import".

### New files — every one under `js/job/` is DOM-free and Node-testable

```
site/js/job/econ.js    LOOT, LADDER, CHAIN, FEE, TELL, COMPLETION, COLD
                       carryFor(target, call, chain, rungs, crew) · missFor(…) · bagFee(s)
                       chainAfterBag() · chainAfterTarget(rung, crewRank, chain)
                       pushMinusBag(state) · breakevenQ(state) · rhoFor(rung, rank) · regretLine(order)
site/js/job/call.js    CALL_LEVELS, credit(p, ok), weightFor(qHat), INFORMATIVE_MIN = 0.25,
                       ratingFrom(calls, N = 50), rankFor(rating), evTable(q), argmaxCall(q),
                       qHatFor(save, skillId)        // argmaxCall is Settings-and-debrief only
site/js/job/guard.js   WINGS, wingOf(skillId), wingOfTag(tag), wingValues(save, queue),
                       guardDist(save, eps, cap), project(y, cap), drawGuard(dist, seed),
                       pressAdvice(dist, values), elo(rp, rh, outcome), vaultGradeFor(rPlayer)
site/js/job/crew.js    capacityFor(save), MANNED_MAX = 12, COSTS = {STEADY: 1, HELD: 2},
                       allocate(save, make, rank), forgivenessOf(save, make),
                       isIdleFor(save, make, target), crewValue(save, make, shape)
site/js/job/board.js   SHAPES, postBoard(save, today), draftFrom(bundles, picks), declinePrice(n),
                       buildJob(save, opts) → inProgress.game, vaultFor(save)  // most-overdue tier-3/4
site/js/job/state.js   the machine: board → envelope → call → answer → payout → bag/push → brief →
                       getaway → debrief; applyTarget(), bag(), crack(), walk(), callIt(),
                       backcheck(), commitBind(); serialises to inProgress.game
site/js/job/index.js   the 68-tag Fault Index: resolve(), trigger(), sealedOf(), areaRollup(),
                       tellFor(save, skillId)        // most-triggered unsealed unresolved tag
site/js/gen/asn-reason.js   NEW generator: reason-chip drills from data/asn.js's 54 reasons (J5b)
site/js/screens/job.js the only DOM file: board sheet, envelope, call row, mounts card.js's answering
                       body, payout line, bag/push, brief sheet, getaway
site/data/job.js       SHAPES, LOOT, LADDER, CALL ladders, RANKS, WINGS, AREA→WING map, copy tables
site/css/job.css       board rows, envelope, chain ticks, guard bars   (linked from index.html, 1 line)
tests/job-econ · job-call · job-guard · job-crew · job-board · job-state · job-split · job-supply
tests/job-exploit · job-monotone · job-ledger · job-align · job-save · job-coldopen · job-copy
tests/job-debrief · job-juice · job-week
```

### Save-schema delta (v1 → v2), **two** new top-level keys

The previous draft sent `game.rating`, `game.rank`, `game.elo` and `game.ledger.records` to `KEPT_KEYS` and `game.crew`, `game.tags`, `game.heat`, `game.log` to `ARCHIVED_KEYS`. **`site/js/store.js:191-205` archives top-level keys only** (`for (const k of ARCHIVED_KEYS) entry[k] = save[k]`); there is no sub-key path support. So the split is expressed as two top-level keys, one rename, zero new machinery (G12 #19):

```js
// KEPT_KEYS — measures the student, survives the unit handoff
settings: { …, game: true },    // THE JOB's master switch (13 B): DECLARED in store.fresh() and
                                // coerced in fillDefaults — every reader is `settings.game !== false`,
                                // so the coercion fails open; only a literal `false` turns the layer off
player: {
  rating: { calls: [ {p, ok, w, skill, at} ], value: 5.0, n: 0 },   // calls capped at 50   ≈ 3.2 KB
  rank: 2,
  elo:   { player: 1000, house: 1000 },
  records: { bestBag: 0, bestChain: 0, bestRating20: 0, bestRating: 0, cleanJobs: 0, cracked: 0,
             walked: 0, cleanGetaway: false }   // bestRating (17 B): the rank ratchet's audit record
}

// ARCHIVED_KEYS — measures THIS unit's skills and errors
game: {
  crew:  { 'FAC2': 1, 'NOTE': 2 },                          // make → rank, ≤ 12 manned   ≈ 0.2 KB
  heat:  { press: { RECALL: 0, FIGURES: 0, WORDS: 0, ALGEBRA: 0 }, weight: 0, jobs: 0 },
  tags:  { 'dropped-gcf': { resolved: 4, triggered: 1, days: 2, lastDay: '2026-09-17',
                            cleared: true, sealed: false } },        // ≤ 68                ≈ 6.1 KB
  backchecks: { held: 2, mintedDay: '2026-09-17' },
  ledger:{ jobs: 0, tGame: 0, tAnswer: 0, phaseMeans: { board: 18, guard: 12, brief: 20,
                                                        getaway: 25, debrief: 65 },
           debriefAt: null },                              // rolling, last 5 · + the open debrief's stamp
  log:   [ { day, shape, targets, bagged, posted, rating, guard, cracked, tGame, tAnswer } ],  // ≤ 30
  commit:{ kind: null, byMin: null, honored: 0, bound: false }
}
// and, for kind 'job':
inProgress.game  = { shape, seed, bundles, picks, tokens, guard, loose, bagged, chain,
                     calls: [], briefs: [], vault, tGame, tAnswer, phase, phaseAt,
                     stakes, outcome, locked, posted, bc, last, ph, rating0, quiet }
inProgress.bench = [ { ...queueItem, from, sources, wing, posted, basePosted, x2,
                       critical, declined } ]              // the undrafted contracts, at their declined price
inProgress.queue = [ { ...pageQueueItem, from, sources, wing, posted, x2, critical } ]
                                                           // + basePosted, declined on a swapped-in entry
trophies         = { …, 'crew-held', 'index-25', 'index-68', 'chain-8', 'calibrated', 'clean-getaway' }
```

**`ledger.debriefAt`** is the instant `endJob` opened the debrief. `closeDebrief` folds `now − debriefAt` into `phaseMeans.debrief` — the one phase mean the job that opened it cannot measure, because `g.ph.debrief` is 0 by construction at that instant (G1 statement 2: *both numbers are measured, not claimed*). It is a declared, coerced key and a priced one: it shipped for a round as an undeclared pass-through of `normalizeGame`'s merge, which is exactly the drift `tags[].days` was caught for.

**`inProgress.queue` and `save.trophies` are SHARED keys the layer ADDS FIELDS TO**, which is why they are budgeted as deltas and not as whole keys. `composePage` writes a queue item; `page.draftUnion` — the job's own composer — spreads it and adds `from, sources, wing, posted, x2, critical`, and `state.swapIn` splices in bench entries carrying `basePosted, declined` as well. A `settings.game = false` page never produces one of them, so by the same rule that moved `inProgress.bench` they are the layer's bytes. The same holds for the six trophies at `data/trophies.js:308-334` (`crew-held`, `index-25`, `index-68`, `chain-8`, `calibrated`, `clean-getaway`): every one of their predicates reads `ctx.save.player` / `ctx.save.game` and nothing else, so only a job can earn them, and `js/trophies.js` writes `save.trophies[id] = { at }` into a top-level study key.

**`inProgress.bench`** is the GAME layer's third key on `inProgress` — `page.startPage` does not write it, `job.startJob` does, so it is part of the addition this section budgets and not part of the study layer. `benchFor` spreads a whole composed queue item per undrafted target (the swap has to re-price and re-queue a real target, and a cached board would not survive the reload G3.7 proof 6 requires), so it is ~370 B an entry, not a price tag.

**`tags[].days` is a count plus a last date, not an array.** An uncapped `days: [...]` array was the single largest error in the old budget: 68 tags × up to 20 ISO dates × 13 bytes ≈ 18 KB on its own, against a claimed 4.8 KB. Sealing needs a count and a last date and nothing more. `CAPS.game = { calls: 50, log: 30, tags: 68, bundles: 5, heat: 10 }` caps the rest. (G12 #18.)

**The measured budget — every figure below is a byte count `tests/job-save.test.mjs` prints, not an estimate.** *(Restated at the round-5 verify, which moved the `inProgress.bench` row for `params` and the `inProgress.game` row for `tellOff`; at the round-4 save audit, which added the `inProgress.meta` row; and earlier as described below. Stated ceilings are the measurement rounded up to 0.1 KB, are mirrored in `data/job.js` `SAVE_BUDGET_KB`, are asserted AT OR UNDER per line, and SUM to the headline — the eight stated ceilings `4.0 + 14.9 + 6.3 + 2.0 + 4.4 + 2.5 + 0.2 + 5.4` are exactly the `39.7` headline, and `job-save.test.mjs` asserts that sum on every run.)*

| key | measured | stated ceiling |
|---|---|---|
| `player.rating.calls` 50 × ≤ 74 B (`callEntry`'s widest: a 6-dp `q` — or, on the Mock's defined-weight form, a 6-dp `w` — plus the longest make id) | 3.63 KB | — |
| `player` (rank, elo, records) | 0.28 KB | — |
| **`player` total** (incl. `records.bestRating`, 34 B on a saturated carrier) | **3.95 KB** | **4.0 KB** |
| `game.tags` 68 × 122 B (27-char ids, the longest in `data/misconceptions.js`) | 8.10 KB | — |
| `game.log` 30 × 181 B (now including `queueTargets`, the drafted length `endJob` records; `rating` is `ratingDetail().value` at its 4-dp width) | 5.30 KB | — |
| `game.heat.window` 10 × 72 B (the stake-weighted press window G3.4's `x̂` reads) | 0.70 KB | — |
| `game.crew` / `heat` / `backchecks` / `ledger` (incl. `debriefAt`) / `commit` | 0.72 KB | — |
| **`game` total** | **14.83 KB** | **14.9 KB** |
| `inProgress.game`, a 12-target job live (5 bundles, **36 calls**, all 26 `STATE_KEYS` — `tellOff`, the brief window's declined tell, is the newest and costs 17 B) | 6.29 KB | **6.3 KB** |
| `inProgress.bench`, the undrafted contracts at their declined price (4 × ~496 B, `params` included) | 1.94 KB | **2.0 KB** |
| `inProgress.queue` `+{from, sources, wing, posted, basePosted, x2, critical, declined}` × 36 entries | 4.39 KB | **4.4 KB** |
| `inProgress.meta.before` `+{tags, index, rating, startedAt, seed, seedTag, composed}` — the seven GAME fields `captureJobBefore` adds to the snapshot the flat Page also writes | 2.40 KB | **2.5 KB** |
| `trophies` — the six ids only a job can earn, 6 × ~33 B | 0.19 KB | **0.2 KB** |
| **subtotal — what the layer costs TODAY** | **34.04 KB** | **34.1 KB** |
| `runs[]` `+{bagged, posted, ratingDelta, guard, cracked, tGame, tAnswer}` × 40 — **reserved, see below** | 5.35 KB | **5.4 KB** |
| **total added, stated with margin** | **39.39 KB** | **≤ 39.7 KB** |

> **The `runs[]` line is a RESERVED ceiling, not a shipped cost.** No path in `site/` *writes* those seven fields today — `job-save.test.mjs` "THE RESERVED LINE IS STILL RESERVED" scans every module under `site/`, **comment-stripped**, for a write of `ratingDelta`, the one of the seven nothing else could produce. Until one appears, **the layer's real cost is the 34.04 KB `subtotal`**, and the suite asserts both numbers on every run so the difference can never go quiet again. (The subtotal row is the serialiser's own figure — `without the reserved runs[] game fields (what the app writes TODAY): 34852 B = 34.04 KB`. The eight rows above it sum to `34.00 KB`, which is the second number the same test prints; the 36 B between them is JSON's own separators, and both are asserted at or under the 34.1 KB ceiling.) Note what this does NOT depend on: whether a finished job pushes a `runs[]` record at all. A record without the seven fields is an ordinary page record and costs the STUDY layer's bytes, so the job screen starting to record itself (the fix requested in `notes/save-fix.md`, which G9 #8's byte-identity claim needs) moves nothing in this table.

> **Why the figure moved from 26 KB to 31.6 KB, and why it was wrong before.** Round 1 caught that the table had been written before `inProgress.game` existed and was measured against a `_helpers.mjs` literal missing 8 of the serialiser's keys; it pinned the fixture as a FIXED POINT of `js/job/state.js` `serialize()` and re-measured at 25.69 KB. That fixed the SHAPE. The VALUES were still hand-typed, and round 2 found four of them narrower than the shipped writers emit — `rating: 7.1234` where `call.ratingDetail()` returns `6.685919999999999`, `elo: {player: 1187.5}` where `guard.elo()` returns `1113.257822343893`, `w: 0.2549` where `callEntry` rounds to 6 dp (`0.888889`), and a skill cycle shorter than the longest make id. Rebuilt off the writers, the same carrier measured **26.33 KB against a published ≤ 26 KB that carried 313 B of margin** — false by 340 B, with every assertion green (`scratchpad/save-fix-r2/verify-critic.mjs` reproduces both numbers off the round-1 fixture, kept beside it). Three real costs were also missing: `inProgress.game.calls` was priced at the **12 drafted targets** where a call is written per **answered** target and requeues plus brief-window swaps carry it well past that; `inProgress.bench` (~1.9 KB) was never priced at all and `withoutGameKeys` charged it to the STUDY half; and `ledger.debriefAt` was written by every job end and declared nowhere.
>
> **Why it moved again at round 3, from 31.6 KB to 37.1 KB.** Three more lines, each the same mistake one level deeper than the last fix reached. **(a)** The 8 fields the layer adds to every `inProgress.queue` entry were in no fixture and no row — one carrier wrote `queue: []` and the other 24 synthetic study-only items — so `withoutGameKeys` charged 4.4 KB to the STUDY half and the addition counted none of it. **(b)** The six trophies only a job can earn (199 B) were in neither half for the same reason. **(c)** The `calls` figure was not a measurement at all: `job-save.test.mjs`'s corpus called `brief(save, { swap: o[0].id })` where `state.brief` takes `{ swap: { id } }` and ignores anything else, so "260 seeded jobs that **take every swap**" took none and the 23 it reported was the maximum of jobs whose queue could not grow. Real JOB12s reach **28** — re-measured at round 4 over **8 000** all-overdue JOB12s across four answer policies, taking 15 909 brief-window swaps, at `max calls 28, queue 28, drafted 13, bench 2` — against a line that priced 26 with 61 B of slack. The count is therefore no longer observed but DERIVED: every queue entry is answered at most twice (`page.MAX_REQUEUE = 1`, and a re-queued copy carries `requeued: 1`), the only other growth is the declined contracts `swapIn` splices in, so `queue ≤ 2 × (drafted + bench) = 36` and `calls ≤ queue`. `job-save.test.mjs` asserts each link of that chain against the corpus, and asserts that the corpus actually takes swaps.
>
> **Why it moved again at round 4, from 37.1 KB to 39.6 KB.** The before-snapshot. `inProgress.meta.before` is written into ONE slot by both paths — `screens/run.js`'s flat-Page mount writes the five study keys (`skills, tiles, coverage, readiness, xp`) as `{ ...before, tiles: tilesBefore }` (`run.js:857-859`), `captureJobBefore` writes those five plus seven of its own, and `screens/job.js holdForDebrief` calls it inside `update()` on every job (`job.js:819`) — and `withoutGameKeys` had stripped `inProgress`'s key list and the queue ENTRIES and stopped there, so 2.40 KB of GAME bytes was charged to the STUDY half and priced in no row, with neither carrier holding an `inProgress.meta` to make it visible. The row list is no longer maintained by hand: `job-save.test.mjs` parses `run.js`'s own flat-Page literal and diffs it against what `captureJobBefore` returns over a corpus of real jobs — which is how the SEVENTH field, `composed`, was priced in the same round it was written. The five study keys stay charged to the study half, which is what the open T01 note below is about.
>
> **Why it moved again at round 5, from 39.6 KB to 39.7 KB.** Two keys the SHIPPED writers write and no fixture priced — the same defect class a fifth time, now on the study composer's side of the line. **(a)** `params`. `composePage`'s S7 algebra floor writes `{ mode: 'a1' | 'a2' }` onto the Variant it composes (`site/js/page.js:392`); `composeBundles` prices `page.queue` as it stands and `draftUnion` spreads `...t.item`, so the key reaches a JOB's drafted queue (198 of 200 seeded saves with no backlog), its bench, and disk. `freezeVariant` then PERSISTS it (`schedule.js:130`) and every later due of that frozen Variant re-emits it (`page.js:287`), so a worst-case queue of frozen-Variant reviews carries it on EVERY entry — it is priced that way. It is a STUDY key on the queue (a `settings.game = false` page writes it too, so `withoutGameKeys` leaves its 828 B in T01's half), but the whole `bench` key is the layer's, so its 92 B there land on this table: `inProgress.bench` 1.85 → 1.94 KB, ceiling 1.9 → **2.0 KB**. **(b)** `tellOff`, `js/job/state.js` EXTRA_KEYS' tenth, 17 B on `inProgress.game`. The guard that exists to catch exactly (a) — *"a real job's `inProgress.queue` entry carries keys `worstCaseJobQueue()` does not price"* — could not fire: its corpus was half week-off-catch-up saves and half a 65 %-overdue mixture, and a board with a backlog never composes an algebra floor at all, so it produced **zero** `params` entries in 546 000 observations. The corpus now carries a `lowDue` arm (no backlog, every make mastered) that produces them, and a second assertion fails if it ever stops. **Two more keys of the same class were found in the same pass and cost this table nothing:** `frozenKey` and `templateVersion`, which `page.js:287` writes on a frozen-Variant review. They reach a real queue (22 of 840 entries on saves carrying frozen Variants) and were priced in no fixture — but a frozen-Variant entry is a different SHAPE, not a wider one: it cannot carry `rename`, `bucket`, `overdue` or `sweep`, so it is 48 B narrower than the entry this line is priced on (665 B against 713 B). The suite now seeds `save.frozen` in a third corpus arm, prices the two keys on a second fixture shape, and ASSERTS both the dominance (`frozen shape ≤ card shape`) and the mutual exclusion it rests on (no real entry carried both `rename` and `frozenKey`, 840 of 840) — so if the two composer branches ever merge, the line is re-priced instead of quietly under-priced.
>
> Two rules now hold instead of a number. **(1)** Every leaf a shipped writer leaves unrounded is priced at the widest JSON form a finite double can take — 24 characters, 25 signed — so no writer can exceed it. **(2)** `job-save.test.mjs` drives a seeded corpus of REAL JOB12s through `startJob → applyTarget → crack` and fails, naming the leaf, if anything the shipped code writes is wider than the fixture prices it — and it measures the `calls`, `locks` and `bench` COUNTS separately, because round 1's `inProgress.game` line passed only by over-pricing one axis (5 × 12 locks, where a real board posts 33) by about as much as it under-priced the other.

**The bound is on the LIVE keys of ONE unit, and that is the whole of what the split can measure.** `game` and `inProgress` are ARCHIVED keys, so every finished unit leaves a second copy of this table's archived lines under `save.archive[<unit>]`: measured **35.23 KB** on a saturated carrier (**29.88 KB** of it written by shipped paths today — the `runs[]` line is still reserved), against a ceiling of `totalAdded − player − trophies = 35.5 KB`, because `player` and `trophies` are KEPT keys and are the two rows an archive entry can never hold. That copy is invisible to the split **by construction**: it sits in *both* halves of `withoutGameKeys` and cancels — and it has to, because deleting `archive` on one side only would charge an archived unit's ~227 KB of study cards to the game layer. So a student who finishes Unit 1A and saturates Unit 1B carries **≈ 74.6 KB** of game-layer bytes on disk, not 39.7 KB, and the S6 total is bounded **per unit, plus one archived copy each**. `tests/job-save.test.mjs` (“the bound is per-unit and LIVE”) measures all three numbers on every run and asserts the archived copy against that derived ceiling — and since round 4 put `metaDelta` on the archived lines, **35.23 KB measured against 35.5 KB stated is one of the two tightest assertions in the suite, 275 B of slack**, so the next line that moves has to re-measure this paragraph with it.

Against COMPOSED S6's **restated** arithmetic — T01's **500 000-char** bound on the saturated study layer and a **528 KB** total budget (COMPOSED.md S6, corrected at integration and restated again at the round-3 save audit; the old "≈ 210 KB worst case, 250 KB budget" priced a card at 300 B and is superseded) — **there are THREE measurements here, on two different carriers, and they do not say the same thing. They are stated separately because round 5 found them welded into one sentence, attributed to a test printout that does not exist.**

1. **The two BOUNDS close, by 19 B.** `500 000 + 39.7 KB = 540 652.8 B` against a `528 KB = 540 672 B` budget. This is arithmetic on two published bounds — T01's on the study half, G7's on the addition — and nothing else; `job-save.test.mjs` asserts it as such (`T01_STUDY_BOUND + SAVE_BUDGET_KB.totalAdded * KB < S6_BUDGET`) and, since round 5, PRINTS it under that label beside the measurement it used to be confused with: `the two BOUNDS (arithmetic, NOT a measurement): T01's 500000 + G7's 39.7 KB = 540652.8 B of COMPOSED S6's 540672 B — 19.2 B to spare`. It was 122 B at round 4 and is 19 B now, because pricing `params` and `tellOff` moved the headline 39.6 → 39.7 KB (below). **A margin thinner than one unpriced key is not a margin: the next line that moves restates COMPOSED.md S6 rather than being absorbed here.**
2. **`state.test.mjs`'s carrier measures `539 932 chars = 499 600 study + 40 332 game`**, i.e. 540 672 − 539 932 = **740 chars of headline slack**, with 400 chars between its study half and T01's 500 000. That is the saturated carrier *without* a live before-snapshot's study half.
3. **`job-save.test.mjs`'s carrier, which saturates BOTH halves at once, measures 546 004 chars and is OVER the 528 KB budget by 5 332 chars**. The whole of that is the study half: it is 5 671 over T01's 500 000, against which the addition's own 340 B of unused ceiling is the only offset (5 671 − 340 = 5 331). The suite does not publish that as slack: it prints it and **ratchets it** — `assert.ok(over > 0, …)` plus `assert.ok(over <= 8 * KB, …)`, so the overrun cannot quietly grow and, if Request D is ever answered, the arm fails and is rewritten. The line it actually prints is

   `COMPOSED S6, measured: 533.2 KB total = 493.8 KB study + 39.4 KB addition, against 528 KB (OVER by 5332 chars); study half vs T01's 500000: over by 5671`

   The overrun is the before-snapshot's STUDY half (`skills` alone is 3.6 KB of `readiness.skillStates()`), which the flat Page route writes too — so it is T01's bound to move, not this layer's, and it is named open in notes/repair-save.md Request D.

J10 asserts ≤ 39.7 KB added. (The old "≤ 13 KB" was unreachable and would have shipped a red test on day one; the old "≈ 525 KB, closing with ≈ 3 KB to spare" was arithmetic on a 37.1 KB headline that round 4 replaced. **Round 5 struck one more sentence from this paragraph: "`job-save.test.mjs` prints exactly that: `against COMPOSED S6: study bound 488.3 KB + added 39.6 KB = 527.9 KB of 528 KB`". No test on the tree prints that line — it was the round-3 printer, deleted in the round-4 repair along with the constant-only closure it described — and the suite the sentence cited as corroborating a closure measures, and asserts, an overrun.**)

> **Open, and not this layer's to close — and the sign has changed, so it is stated as two numbers rather than as slack.** On the carrier `state.test.mjs` builds, the study half measures **499 600 against T01's 500 000 — 400 chars of slack** (1 228 until round 5 priced the algebra floor's `params`, which is 828 B of STUDY bytes on a worst-case queue) (it used to read 480 409; nothing about the study layer grew, its worst-case carrier stopped under-pricing `inProgress.queue`, which T01 measured as 24 synthetic items (~2.1 KB) and which a real Page answered to the end reaches 43 entries and ~14 KB on). **But that carrier omits the before-snapshot's five STUDY keys, and the shipped writers do not.** `inProgress.meta.before` is written on every job and on every flat Page, and its study half is `skills, tiles, coverage, readiness, xp`. Driven through the shipped writers over 120 real JOB12s (`scratchpad/repair-save-v2/meta-study.mjs`, every one of the 19 skills present, which is what `skillStates` is widest on), the widest real study half is **4 255 B** — `skills` 3 972 · `tiles` 202 · `readiness` 39 · `coverage` 37 · `xp` 6 — against the same snapshot's 360 B of game fields. So the save the app actually writes is `499 600 + 4 255 = 503 855` chars of study half, **over T01's 500 000 by 3 855 with no pessimism at all**, and `503 855 + 40 332 = 544 187` chars in total, **3 515 over the 528 KB = 540 672 B budget**. The suite pins the pessimistic form of the same fact rather than hiding it: `tests/state.test.mjs` prints `a LIVE in-progress save (label block + full before-snapshot): study 505671 of 500000 — over by 5671` and **asserts that the overrun is still there**, with a ratchet capping it at 6 000 — **329 chars away**, deliberately not raised at round 5, so the next study-side key that gets priced forces the ruling instead of another re-measurement. So: the budget closes on the carrier, and does **not** close on the shape the app writes. The bound is T01's and COMPOSED.md S6's; the resolution is theirs to pick — raise the 500 000 / 528 KB pair to the measured total, or trim what the snapshot stores (`skills` is the whole of it — the debrief needs the delta, not 14 fields per make). Recorded for its owner as `notes/repair-save.md` Request D. Narrowing a GAME fixture to make room would be the wrong fix and is not done.

> **Why 25 → 26, ruled at integration** (kept for the record; the ceiling is now 39.7 KB for the reasons above). `game.heat.window` is `guard.pushHeat`'s own schema (notes/J3.md §5.5 asked J10 for it) and it was not on the table when the 25 KB line was written: `guard.xHatFrom` reads the last `GUARD.xHatWindowJobs = 10` jobs' press and posted, which is what makes G3.4's `x̂` a stake-weighted estimate rather than an all-time average, so the window is load-bearing and cannot be dropped.

### Today's Page ↔ jobs

**One queue, two skins.** Today's Page remains the source of truth for *what gets answered*. A job draws its board from `composePage`'s queue, writes to the same `inProgress.queue`/`idx`, and marks items through the same `markItem` / `requeueReview` / `finishPage` calls. Bag a job at 7 of 12 items and Home says `5 left on Today's Page`, offered as the calm page — answering them there is equally valid and pays identical XP, mastery and Leitner. The Page Summary becomes the **debrief** (same screen, same tile mint, same skill bars, same Readiness delta, plus the bag drop, the rating delta, the guard redraw, the regret lines, the split and the decision count). With `settings.game = false`, `#/run/page` is exactly what it is today and Home's primary button reads `RUN NEXT · 6 reviews + 8 new`. **Every study route, every grader, every schedule rule and every number in Readiness behaves identically with the layer on or off**, and `job-ledger.test.mjs` proves it byte-for-byte.

### The existing product, in the new frame

| COMPOSED object | in THE JOB | what changed |
|---|---|---|
| **Today's Page** | the board's supply. `composePage` runs first and owns what is studied; `composeBundles` partitions it and `draftUnion` re-interleaves the draft | nothing about composition, order constraints, caps or the seed |
| **Readiness** | printed unmodified as the hero number on Home and on the board's header | nothing. No game term enters `readiness.js`, and the two numbers may disagree in public |
| **Leitner review** | **coldness.** Dues pay `scope 1.25` and `cold ≤ 1.50` scaled by the card's own interval; a make's crew stands down on its own due review; clearing every due on a day with dues mints the day's Backcheck | nothing about intervals, the test clamp, frozen Variants or re-queue |
| **The 7 Bosses** | the **VAULT** shape's final target when `page.bossReady(save)` fires; `screens/boss.js` runs it | nothing: 3 hearts, no hints, CONTINUE? with the check question, KO keeps XP/mastery/tiles, `*` flag, same-seed ghosts |
| **The Mock** | **the big score.** No calls, no stakes, no crew, no chain — a test is a test. Its existing prediction slider is scored by `call.credit` and enters the rating window as one call at `w = min(4ŝ(1−ŝ), 0.25)`, where ŝ is the mean score of its own trailing ten papers — it has no MAKE and therefore no `q̂`, but its weight is **measured off that history rather than defined**, and capped at the smallest weight the window counts, so one Mock slot can never pay more than **2.50** of Σ(w·c). It is **0** on a first-ever paper and 0 whenever ŝ leaves the informative band (`screens/mock.js mockCallWeight`; verify r3 — this document published `w = 1.0` at six sites while the shipped cap was 0.25, and `screens/settings.js` rendered the 1.0 to the student, a 4× overstatement of the app's only calibration surface outside a job) | nothing about the blueprint, the timer, flags, the question map, autosave or the report |
| **Night Before (D−1)** | **no board.** `No board tonight · Night Before · ~30 min · ends 21:12` Completion pays the Clean Getaway stamp + 1 Backcheck | nothing about the four blocks, the elite ≥ Bronze rule, the cheat sheet or the 22:00 soft close |
| **Test Morning (D)** | the board shows the final ledger and `Go.` | nothing |
| **Rarity, Foil, Platinum, the Binder** | unchanged and still the app's collection; the Fault Index sits beside it, measuring a different thing | nothing; the tile mint stays the signature moment |
| **Streak, trophies, XP, levels** | unchanged; crew capacity is a function of `xp.levelFor` and boss stamps | nothing; six new trophy predicates are added |
| **BLITZ, Drill 5, JUMP, Full 36, Daily, Upgrade, Missed** | unchanged and pay **zero carry and zero rating** — that is what makes re-farming impossible | nothing |

---

## G8 Build plan

Ordered tickets. Each is one sitting for one agent; `node --test tests/` must be green after every one; each ends by writing `notes/<id>.md` per BUILD-POLICY §2. Later tickets import earlier ones, so the order matters. Every file under `js/job/` is DOM-free and imports nothing from `screens/`, so the whole game layer runs headless under `node --test` — the same discipline as the graders.

**the split printed on the board before the job matches the split the debrief HEADLINES within 5 points on all four shapes, on both paths **seasoned with their own path**, and on a shape the ledger has never seen** — **and NOT across a change of FIXED-PHASE COLUMN, which this file's 8-cell cross-shape arm cannot see (all eight are `clone(CORPUS[2])`, one clock, one driver, and each is seasoned with all three other shapes mixed, which averages the shape bias out): swept through the shipped machine over 50 saves × 12 ordered shape pairs × the four clocks `tests/job-board.test.mjs` defines, five COMPLETED jobs and no walk, the same-shape band is ≤ 1.19 and the within-column band (JOB ↔ JOB-12 ↔ VAULT) is ≤ 4.39 with 0 of 1 192 outside 5 points, while any pair involving RUN — the other `FIXED_PHASES` column, and the shape the school window posts every weekday — misses the band on 21 of 1 196 `ledger` cells, worst 6.35 (G1 statement 2's round-3 correction carries the two causes and why neither is closable from a five-entry log)** — **and NOT across a change of path, which no arm in this file exercises: seasoning and measurement share one clock and one path in `seasoned`/`walkWithClock`, and measured across the change the gap is up to 6.9 points tapping→full (20/20 over 5) and 7.4 full→tap (18/20), on a line still labelled `ledger` (G9 #1, verify round 2; the cross-regime arm is an open Request in `notes/repair-meta.md`)** — (the projection reads `save.game.ledger.phaseMeans` and spends them on THIS draft's own tiers and windows; a zero-target walk is not one of "your last N jobs"; on job 1 it falls back to the shipped defaults, labels itself `projected`, and is published as up to 7 points low on a full-use path); `tGame`/`tAnswer` use wall-clock `Date.now()` deltas and survive a `visibilitychange`; ## G9 Definition of ADDICTIVE-BUT-HONEST

Ten criteria, each checkable by a harsh critic who built none of it. **Each one now names a test that exists in this tree**, because a criterion whose only evidence is a sentence is not a criterion. All ten must pass — and **as of verify round 2, nine do**: criterion 6 has a measured exception (the RANK, bought with evidence the game does not price) and criterion 1 has a measured condition (the two surfaces agree per surface, not across a change of surface). Both are stated **inside** their own criterion, with the number and the open code change, because a definition that quietly drops the criterion it fails is not a definition.

1. **A large, measured, printed share of the session is decisions — and the app says which share, on which basis.** The board prints a projection computed from the student's *own* last-5 phase durations before the run; the debrief prints the measurement; **the two agree within 5 points on all four shapes, on the default path and on the full-use path — each measured against a history of its OWN surface, which is the condition this claim carries and did not used to state — on a shape the ledger has never seen **whose FIXED-PHASE COLUMN it has also seen — across the two columns (any pair with RUN) the band is 6.35 and 21 of 1 196 `ledger` cells are outside it, published in G1 statement 2's round-3 correction rather than defended, because the two causes are an information limit in a five-entry log and a modelling claim whose two directions cost each other; within one column it is ≤ 4.39 on 1 192 cells and same-shape it is ≤ 1.19 on 800** —, on a clock that is nobody's published table **and never one whose pooled rate sits ON a clamp bound, where the line now demotes itself to `projected` (round 3: at the bound the rate IS the bound, and one printed sentence served students the debrief separates by seventeen points — 45 of 50 corpus cells outside the band at a flat 8 s per stem; after the demotion, 0 of 716 `ledger` cells over the whole flat-clock family, worst 0.98)**, and — on a history of mid-job WALKs — per cell wherever the board prints `ledger`, which after the round-2 verification repair is every walk level **up to FOUR of five**, on four clocks (**4 000 cells** — 50 saves × 4 shapes × 5 walk levels × 4 clocks — none outside the band, worst 2.32), and at five of five, where the board prints `projected`, as a distribution whose shape is a property of the student's clock: on the arm's own clock 87 % of 200 cells inside 5 points, median 2.3, worst 5.9; 100 % inside it for a table-paced and for a slow-stem student; and none of them for a deliberator (median 14.2, worst 21.8)** (`tests/job-split.test.mjs`; the walk arm is `tests/job-board.test.mjs`, which owns the board, and asserts the first register per cell and the second as bounds — at most 16 % of cells over, median ≤ 3.0, worst ≤ 6.0). **The unqualified "up to five of five" this criterion used to claim is withdrawn**: at five of five the projection window contains no finished job, so the decision term has nothing unambiguous to rate and the board prints `projected` rather than `your last 5 jobs` — the same claim job 1 makes. A criterion may not rest on an arm that tolerates its own falsification, so the criterion is stated at the strength the arm asserts (SPEC-CORRECTIONS A-3; G1 statement 2; G12 #80). **Two bases exist and both are published, because printing one where the other was meant is exactly how this criterion went wrong:** the **nominal** JOB-10 measures 43.2 % tapping through and 49.8 % with the windows (`job-split.test.mjs`, reproduced exactly), and the **boards the composer actually posts** measure 22 / 30 / 35 % headline on 14.2 / 17.4 / 27.9 minutes (`PUBLISHED.shapeTableDrafted`, re-measured from `postBoard` by `tests/job-shape-measured.test.mjs`, which also asserts the nominal row does **not** bracket them). Decision density is **≥ 2 decisions per graded item on the default path and ≥ 3 with the windows**, with **0 ms** of any state where the app is neither accepting input nor showing a result. **The `≥ 3` is on `econ.decisionCount`'s basis — each brief window charged by its five options — not on the debrief counter's, which charges a window once and therefore tops out at 2.5 per item on the JOB-10 (G1's reframe; verify round 2, split-honesty).** `SPLIT.densityMinFull = 3` is asserted against the published count in `job-econ.test.mjs` and against a played full-use job in `job-split.test.mjs` §5, both on that basis; the debrief now names the basis of every numeral it prints on that line. There is no constant-ratio claim, because the no-padding rule (G10 #17) wins wherever one would be needed. **WHAT THE AGREEMENT CLAIM DOES NOT COVER, measured in verify round 2 — the night the surface CHANGES.** `projectFor` charges one blended `means.brief` per openable window rather than the student's own rate of *using* one, so the projection is right about a student who plays tonight the way they played the last five nights and wrong about one who does not. Taking the app's own two published fixed-phase columns as the two regimes (`FIXED_PHASES.JOB.default` tap-through vs `.full`), 20 corpus saves, 5 seasoning jobs each: **tap→tap worst 1.0 point (0 of 20 over the published 5), full→full worst 1.7 (0 of 20), tap→FULL worst 6.9 with 20 of 20 over, full→tap worst 7.4 with 18 of 20 over** — and the board's source label still reads `ledger`, so the line claims the student's own five jobs while being 7 points out. That is the ordinary case of a student who has tapped through all week and tonight opens the windows. Nothing in the suite exercises it: `tests/job-split.test.mjs`'s `seasoned(id, clock, jobs)` replays `walkWithClock(s, id, clock)`, seasoning with the very clock and path it then measures, so the regime change has no arm. **Two Requests are open on it** (`notes/repair-meta.md`): the cross-regime arm in `job-split.test.mjs`, and a path term for `board.js projectFor` — `game.log` already carries `briefs`/`committed`, so the window cost can be charged against the student's own rate of opening a window instead of a single blended mean. Until one of them lands the claim is conditional and says so. (G12, “Verify round 2 — the meta lane”, entry **VR2-SPLIT**.)
2. **No item is ever removed from the schedule by a game decision, and a job answers exactly what the composer composed.** `set(job items) ⊆ set(page items)`, `Σ answered over the day == Σ answered on the flat path`, and every unreached target stays due and returns on a later board — **1 162 of 1 162 boards over 400 saves × every shape; it does not always lead, and does not always make the NEXT Page, because `LIMITS.dues` caps the dues a Page serves (4 of 1 162) and a hard item is never first (14 of 1 162), which are the composer's rules and not game decisions** (SPEC-CORRECTIONS A-5) — driven end to end in `tests/job-board.test.mjs` (“a target a job does not reach stays due and LEADS the next board”). The wall-clock truth — a JOB-10 spends its game seconds on the same items the flat Page answers faster — is printed on the board button, where it already is. *(The old #2 claimed the job path answers more items per wall-clock minute than the flat Page, which a game split makes arithmetically impossible.)*
3. **Knowing the math is the only way to win.** Over 10⁴ random states a clear pays ≥ a miss in both currencies; no mechanic exists in which a wrong answer increases LOOSE or a right answer decreases it; all 2ⁿ outcome vectors for n ≤ 11 are monotone in clears under optimal play **on every shape the composer deals**, and the bounded exception off the dealt shapes is asserted too (`tests/job-monotone.test.mjs`, `tests/job-exploit.test.mjs`; §3.7(2) states the scope and the measured exception).
4. **Every probability is printed before the decision it affects — and no recommended action is.** Four random objects, four printed distributions, five Settings panels with the formulas; `tests/job-exploit.test.mjs` and `tests/job-screen.test.mjs` grep every pre-call surface for the EV-max rung, and `tests/job-guard.test.mjs` executes the three published guard formulae against the functions that claim them. A reviewer can recompute any number on any screen from the save, **with one exception, printed rather than hidden**: `player.rank` is a high-water mark, so it is recomputable from the save **plus its own history** — and, since G2's THE CAP, a high-water of `earned = ceiling` (what the calls were WORTH) rather than of the rating. **The exception runs in BOTH directions** — the rank can print above the live rating's band (the high-water) and below it (the calls were worth less than the dice paid) — and **both are printed**: `ratingDetail().offBand` fires on exactly those windows and the two audit surfaces then carry `· your calls were worth N.NN`, so the rank on the page stays derivable from the numbers on the page. **The save stores that same statistic**: `player.records.bestRating` is a high-water of `detail.earned` at all three writers, and `rankFor` is monotone, so the exception resolves to one line a reviewer can check — `player.rank === max(the rank this save started at, rankFor(player.records.bestRating))`. (Until verify round 2 the field stored `detail.value`, which on a capped window printed pairs like `Called 3 · best rating 10.00` and made the record unreconcilable with the rank it was there to justify; `bestRating20` remains the printed **rating**'s own record. G2 "Rank".) Both surface on Settings and Stats — pinned as a source assertion in `tests/job-meta-constants.test.mjs` — with each job's rating delta in `game.log`. The one thing deliberately withheld before a call is the argmax rung (Global law 6).
5. **A min-maxer is a good student — with the identity and the measurement kept apart.** Spearman ρ = 1 between the crew-value ordering and `weakSpots()`'s sort key is an **arithmetic identity, not a measurement**: `crewValue` is a positive constant of the shape times `w·(1 − m/100)`, which is that sort key, so ρ = 1 cannot fail for any save (`tests/job-align.test.mjs` §1, which carries its own falsifiability control). What is **measured**, on the drafted board a crew point is spent on, is **58 % top-three and 20 % exact-argmax against ΔReadiness over all nineteen makes** — 91 % / 44 % over the makes the board actually serves, which is the circular reading and is asserted as such (§6.2) — and, as a distribution over 24 corpora rather than one draw, **top-three 61 % ± 6, argmax 29 % ± 5, mean ρ(all) = 0.83 ± 0.03, no corpus below 0.7** (§6.3). *(Verify round 3: this criterion previously published 43 % / 19 % / 99 % / 57 % and ρ(all) = 0.65, which are §6.2b's — Today's Page truncated to ten items, not a board any job is played on — under §6.2's and §6.3's names.)* The token **press** `pᵢ = A − B/vᵢ` is increasing in `vᵢ`, hence interleaving weighted by `w × cold`; the guard's own mixing `yᵢ = 1 − k/vᵢ` is the other side of that game and is not the student's advice (`tests/job-guard.test.mjs`). The rating weight `4q̂(1−q̂)` is strictly decreasing in q̂ on `[0.5, 1]`, hence a monotone transform of `(1 − m/100)` on the domain where the game stakes anything; the rating is *earned* in the band `q̂ ∈ [0.763, 0.925]`, the ≥ 80 %-of-peak region of the continuous `w·E[c]` (`tests/job-econ.test.mjs`). **The unrestricted argmax claim this criterion used to make is withdrawn**: it is a per-board measurement, `crew.alignmentFor().claim`, true on 27 % of drafted JOB-10 boards and **equivalent to** two of the four conditions `crew.alignmentFor().domain` computes (rank ∧ supply, both argmax comparisons) rather than implied by all four — the round-4 phrasing, *"holds only on `domain.all`"*, was itself withdrawn at verify round 2 and G3.8 states why. The two argmaxes disagree on more than 50 % of every save population measured — 51–93 % across 24 corpora from two generators, of which round 4's 87.3 % was one draw. **And the domain is a limit case, not an evening:** all four hold together on **at most 5 %** of 300 drafted boards at the brief's own call and **at most 1 %** over the 19-make pool (`tests/job-align.test.mjs` §9, asserted as bounds), because conditions 1 and 4 are universally quantified and one untouched make, one weak spot at `m ≤ 40` or one mastered make at `m ≥ 85` empties them — and those two conditions are also the two that cannot change the claim, which is why the conjunction certifies nothing. This criterion rests on the identity and the measurements above it, never on the argmax theorem.
6. **Nothing can be farmed — except the RANK, and this criterion FAILS on it today.** The unqualified “Nothing can be farmed” is **withdrawn** (G11). What still holds: mastered-tier-1 grinding produces **no informative calls at all**, so the rating sits at exactly 5.00 and the loot/hour is the worst in the game; the renewable loot rate from a fixed card set is monotone non-increasing over 400 simulated days; `cold` is capped so delay never pays; `tell` dies on resolution so stockpiled errors depreciate; carry exists only on composer-drawn targets (`tests/job-exploit.test.mjs`). **The one lever this criterion used to deny is now priced instead of denied:** dropping Leitner buckets raises the renewable stock rate and *lowers* the realised take, both directions asserted with an uncapped-targets control (G3.7(3)(d)). **THE EXCEPTION, measured in verify round 2 and not closed:** the anti-farming weight `w = 4q̂(1−q̂)` is built from `save.cards[*].history`, which `screens/card.js` writes on every graded original — inside a job or on Today's Page. A sitting outside a job is therefore **evidence the game layer does not price**: it pays no carry, no chain and no rating, and it still moves `w`. Two arms, same start save, every job target cleared in both, identical call policy, differing only in the `ok` flag on free study sittings: **Called 2 on 8/8 seeds for the honest master, Called 4 on 8/8 for the student who marks one free sitting in five wrong, +6.91 % post-climb loot, and a byte-identical study ledger on 8/8** (`node notes/repair-meta-evidence.mjs`; §3.7 proof 8 carries the whole measurement, and the reason THE CAP does not stop it — the sandbagger reports honestly about evidence they chose, so the cap, which prices the report, has nothing to catch). The rank is farmable, it is farmed with the one currency this game deliberately refuses to charge for, and the close is a `js/job/call.js` change — build `q̂` only from staked job targets — filed as a Request in `notes/repair-meta.md`. Pinned at the mechanic, so it cannot regress unnoticed, in `tests/job-meta-constants.test.mjs` §8; the end-to-end arm is the script above, which is deliberately not in `node --test tests/` (48 simulated jobs).
7. **Quitting is free and never a strategy.** WALK in one tap under 3 s with the lossless option default-focused, 50 % auto-bag on any other exit, the job seed pinned so a reload cannot re-roll the guard, the bundles or the ×2 (`tests/job-save.test.mjs`), and bag-and-continue ≥ bag-and-quit in 10⁴ states (`tests/job-exploit.test.mjs`). A walk that answered nothing re-posts the byte-identical board; a walk that **answered** a target is a job on record, and what holds that door is `startJob`'s `page-in-progress` refusal and its toll — both asserted in `tests/job-board.test.mjs` (§3.7 proof 6).
8. **Study state is never at risk.** `tests/job-ledger.test.mjs`: the same answer sequence inside a job and through `#/run/page` produces byte-identical `cards`, `skills`, `xp`, `errors`, `counters` **and `forecastLog`**; `streak` and `jumps` are covered by something stronger than a comparison — `state.guardSave` **refuses** every write to them from `js/job/*`, asserted as a throw. (`forecastLog` is Readiness' forecast trace, not study progress. A **completed** job writes one, identically to `#/run/page` — pinned in `tests/run-lane-r2.test.mjs` and compared byte-for-byte here — and a job that does **not** complete writes none. **BAG is not a terminal state:** a job bagged at every beat and played to the end completes and writes all three; the gate is `commitJobRun`'s `debrief.complete !== true`. See §3.7 proof 11.) `P(losing study progress) = 0`.
9. **The app tells the truth about time and the week.** Every primary button prints targets, minutes, the wall-clock end time and the projected split. D = 2 drops the stakes, D = 1 posts no board at all, any shape that would end after 22:00 is refused before it starts, after 22:00 the board closes and a job in progress auto-bags at full value — and **none of it locks a single study door** (`tests/job-week.test.mjs`). And Home's first paint prints no number it cannot derive from the student's own save: `tests/job-week.test.mjs` asserts pass 1 returns `null` for every per-draft numeral on every week state and seed that posts a board, with no spinner and no `aria-busy`.
10. **It is still the study tool.** Readiness is computed by the published formula and printed unmodified even when it disagrees with rank; Today's Page, Leitner, the seven Bosses, the Mock and the Night Before all exist unchanged and are reachable in one tap; `settings.game = false` returns the product to byte-identical COMPOSED behaviour (`tests/job-ledger.test.mjs`, `tests/job-save.test.mjs`).

**What these ten do NOT cover, recorded here rather than left to be discovered.** The crew's second rung is dominated below the manned cap and forced above it, and the brief's crew grid does not print which side of the cap the student is on (G2). `crew.legalize` has no caller, so a rung a Mock miss has invalidated goes unannounced until the next allocation (G4). The collapsed board line is clipped at 375 px (G6). Three board strings — `pressLine`, `oneMakeLine`, `deferredLine` — are computed and rendered nowhere, and `COPY.repeat` and `COPY.cleanGetaway` have no call site (pinned by name and count in `tests/job-copy.test.mjs` §10, so the dead list cannot grow quietly). G6's four sound cues were never built. None of those is a dishonesty; all of them are open, and G12's round-4 section carries each with its owner.

---

## G10 Contradiction resolutions

**Every line names the winner. Where COMPOSED-GAME wins, it wins on presentation only; where COMPOSED or BUILD-POLICY wins, this document bends.**

1. **Appendix B deletes "currency/shop/Double-XP".** → **COMPOSED wins.** There is no persistent currency: LOOSE/BAGGED evaporate every job; CREW is capacity, a function of level and boss stamps, freely re-allocatable at zero cost, unbuyable and unhoardable. Heist's CUT/safehouse/intel/cases, incremental's cores/Lattice, duel's LP/dossiers/loadout, season's assists/store, conquest's doctrine, pushluck's Read ladder and deckbuilder's Bench purchases are all **deleted** (G11). The one consumable, the Backcheck, is minted by clearing dues and is capped at 3.
2. **Heist's binary payout vs defense's damage ladder.** → **COMPOSED-GAME wins (presentation).** The ladder (1.00/0.70/0.45/0.20/miss) replaces it. It also *is* the hint economy, which lets "hints become a priced resource" land **without** making hints scarce — hints stay free and infinite (COMPOSED Global rule 1); only the loot moves.
3. **Two proper scoring rules would be one too many.** The per-target sealed **Call** is kept. Defense's per-surge Forecast, duel's shift-level Brier and season's shot-call are **rejected as duplicates**.
4. **Three risk decisions would be two too many.** The **Call** and **BAG/PUSH** are kept. Every stake slider is rejected; Kelly survives as the `min(LOOSE, ·)` cap and the printed `q*`.
5. **Four adversaries would be three too many.** The **Guard** is the adversary. The Proctor, the rival roster, the clubs, the Marker and the Fade are deleted. The House's Elo survives as one symmetric number; **the vault grade is driven by `R_player`, not `R_house`** (§3.5).
6. **Defense's standing coverage removes problems from the session.** → **COMPOSED Global rule 5 wins.** Deleted. Crew's two effects are forgiveness and chain-hold; **crew never removes a card.**
7. **Heist's "Skinned" state** is replaced by season's **CALL IT**: stakes end, the loss is recorded, the remaining targets continue as no-stakes practice with hints on.
8. **Heist dropped LOOSE on an abandoned job.** Replaced by the **50 % auto-bag** plus a **pinned seed** on resume.
9. **COMPOSED Global rule 3 (no clock on thinking).** → **COMPOSED wins.** The tick law satisfies it by construction. The only wall-clocks in the layer are the truthful *end time* printed before you press, and the two measurement accumulators, neither of which enters a payoff.
10. **COMPOSED Global rule 4 (nothing auto-advances on a correct answer).** → **COMPOSED wins.** The bag/push prompt *is* the continue tap — it occupies that slot rather than replacing it, which is the whole of the resolution. (**Round-4 repair:** this entry used to end "HELD crew skips the *call* (a pre-stem decision), never the continue." No such path exists — `grep -rn "skip.*call\|autoCall\|skipCall" site/js/job/ site/js/screens/job.js` returns nothing. `state.lockCall` is unconditional on crew rank; the only other door to the stem is `beginAnswer`, which throws `stakes-on` unless the stakes are already off, and crew is read only afterwards, at `crewFor`/`forgivenessOf`, to move ρ. G1's own decision table counts `CALL | 10 | 10` for a JOB-10 with no crew-dependent reduction, and `econ.decisionCount('JOB')` returns `{mandatory: 24, full: 35}` to match. **The call is mandatory on every staked target at every crew rank.** G12 #76.)
11. **COMPOSED Global rule 1 (gates gate loot, not learning).** → **COMPOSED wins.** Rank gates the 95 call and the guard multiplier — loot. It gates no card, no boss, no Mock, no hint, no solution, no Variant.
12. **COMPOSED S4's optional `callYourShot`.** The Call replaces it inside jobs and its XP factor is **not** applied — the stake lives in LOOSE and rating, never in XP. Outside jobs `callYourShot` behaves exactly as specified today.
13. **COMPOSED S5 "the tile mint is the signature moment".** → **COMPOSED wins.** The bag drop is in-place in the header column, 600 ms, never full-screen, the **only thing animating** rather than the only thing on screen, and the debrief stays interactive under it.
14. **COMPOSED S1 "every extra screen is a place to not answer a question".** → **COMPOSED wins.** Zero new routes: the job is `#/run/job`, the board is a Home panel, the Ledger / Fault Index / crew grid are `#/stats` panels, the brief is a sheet inside the run screen, the debrief is the Page Summary. `ROUTE_PATTERNS` stays 13.
15. **Defense wanted `channel`/`class`/`tier` fields on all 68 tags.** Rejected: tag → wing is derived from `AREAS` by an 11-entry map. `data/misconceptions.js` is untouched.
16. **Two collections.** The Binder's ~140 rarity tiles and the 68-tag Fault Index are both kept; a third is rejected.
17. **The 50/50 ratio vs. the no-padding rule.** → **The no-padding rule wins and the app prints the true number.** The claim this document defends is not a constant ratio; it is that each shape's projection is computed from the student's own measured phases, that printed and measured agree within 5 points, and that decision density is ≥ 2 per graded item on the tap-through path and ≥ 3 with the windows, **counting a brief window by its five options as `econ.decisionCount` does** (the debrief's own counter charges a window once, reaches 2.5 on the JOB-10, and prints which basis each numeral is on — G9 #1; verify round 2). **The 43–51 % pair is the NOMINAL JOB-10's range and this entry used to print it without the word**; the boards the composer posts measure 22 / 30 / 35 % headline, and both bases are published (G1, G9 #1).
18. **COMPOSED S1's 10–25 minute session.** → **COMPOSED wins.** The VAULT is **7 targets, 17:26–18:33**, not 8 targets and 25:24. Every shape's full-use wall clock is inside the band. (This entry published **18:58** until verify round 3. The 25 s are the `crew re-allocation` fixed-phase cell that verify round 2 deleted as unreachable and double-charged — 1113 + 25 = 1138 s = 18:58 — so the range moved with it; `PUBLISHED.shapeTable.VAULT.wallS` is 1046 → 1113, and `tests/job-split.test.mjs` recomputes both ends.)
19. **`LIMITS.sameSkillRun = 2` and the 1→4 tier ramp (COMPOSED S1).** → **COMPOSED wins.** Contracts are a pricing and selection device only; the drafted union is re-interleaved through `spreadSkills` and the ramp before the first envelope, and contracts survive as a source label.
20. **`site/js/store.js` archives top-level keys only.** → **the code wins.** The handoff split is expressed as two top-level keys, `player` (KEPT) and `game` (ARCHIVED).
21. **`tests/home-r2.test.mjs` forbids a static `page.js`/`plan.js` import in Home.** → **the test wins.** The board paints in two passes; J13 asserts no layout shift and no spinner instead of no import.
22. **`site/js/app.js` already renders six header items.** → **the code wins.** During a job four are hidden and three added: five items, pinned.
23. **COMPOSED S9 #1 (cold open to first answer ≤ 20 s).** → **COMPOSED wins.** The pinned cold-open path is `board 6 → primary button → guard accept 4 → call 5` = ~15 s. The 12 s guard figure in the phase table is the observed re-press mean and is labelled as such.
24. **COMPOSED S6's save budget.** → **COMPOSED wins, on the carrier; and the carrier is not the whole story, which is said here rather than left to the table.** The layer adds ≤ 39.7 KB per unit, LIVE (measured 39.39 KB; restated at the round-2 save audit, which found the published 26 KB false by 340 B; again at round 3, which found the job's own `inProgress.queue` fields, its six trophies and its true call count unpriced; again at round 4, which found `inProgress.meta.before`'s seven game fields charged to the STUDY half and priced in no row; and again at round 5, which found `params` and `tellOff` written by shipped writers and priced in no fixture — see the budget table) from real JSON with a job in progress, and `state.test.mjs`'s worst-case builder is extended to prove it. The budget it is measured against is COMPOSED.md S6's **restated** one — T01's 500 000-char bound on the study layer and **< 528 KB** total, corrected at integration when the saturated study layer was first measured and restated at rounds 3 and 4. **The headline now closes by 19 B, not by ≈ 3 KB** (500 000 + 39.7 KB = 540 652.8 of 540 672), **and on the save shape the app actually writes — a live job whose before-snapshot carries its five STUDY keys — it does not close at all**: `state.test.mjs` prints `study 505671 of 500000 — over by 5671` and asserts the overrun. That is T01's bound and COMPOSED.md S6's number to move, not this layer's; G7 states both measurements and names it open. The "250 KB" this entry used to name was the superseded estimate; citing it here made J10 read as passing a criterion the shipped code does not meet.
25. **COMPOSED's 22:00 soft close and the Mock's 21:30 rule.** → **COMPOSED wins.** The layer adds no new clock constant: a shape is refused when *its own projected end time* passes 22:00.
26. **`site/data/cards/asn.js` contains zero `tag:` entries.** → **the data wins.** The tell is skill-keyed and read from `save.errors`, so it degrades gracefully and needs no card-data migration.
27. **`js/gen/*` and `data/templates.js` were listed as zero-edit.** → **this document bends.** One new generator file is added and `data/templates.js` gains exactly one entry (J5b); the existing generator files are still untouched, and G7's tables say so.
