## G12 Changelog — every murder-board item, what changed, and why

**Murder board of 2026-09-17, `designs/murderboard-game.md`: FATAL 9 · MAJOR 19 · MINOR 12 (40 items). All 9 FATAL, all 19 MAJOR and all 12 MINOR are addressed below.**

### FATAL

1. **Rating was a weighted mean → now a weighted sum over a fixed window of 50, with an informative gate at `w ≥ 0.25`.** A mean is invariant to its weights on a homogeneous window, so farming mastered tier-1 locks scored 9.41 (Ghost) while the doc asserted ≤ 5.05. New: `rating = clamp(0, 10, 5 + 2·Σ(w·c)/50)`, only `w ≥ 0.25` (`q̂ ∈ [0.067, 0.933]`) calls enter, and an unfilled slot contributes 0 — so a pure farmer generates **no** informative calls and sits at exactly **5.00**. RANK thresholds re-derived against the new scale (Called 3 at 6.5, Called 4 at 7.7, Called 5 at 8.9, from the achievable mean-`w·c` curve; sustained peak play is 2.499/call → 9.998). The "central alignment result" is restated in two honest halves: `w` peaks at `q̂ = 0.5` (leverage) and `w·E[c]` peaks at `q̂ = 0.854` (gain), because a Brier credit at a coin flip is exactly 0 and no formula can make `q̂ = 0.5` the rating maximum. §3.1, §3.8, G2 Rank, G9 #5, J2, J9 all updated.
2. **The free advisor line sold the answer → Global law 6 added; the envelope prints evidence only.** `FAC2 · Factoring a > 1 · grade 2 · cold 3 d · posted 41 · from B · tell · your last 10: 7/10`. The EV-max rung and the indifference boundaries move to a Settings formula panel and to the debrief's regret line (`envelope 6: you called 85, EV-max was 70`). J2 greps every pre-call template for `argmaxCall`/`EV-max`/`evTable`; J6 asserts the same on the DOM.
3. **BAG's effect on the chain was never stated → `chainAfterBag()` added as the fourth transition: BAG banks the pile and resets the chain to 0.** The `1` in §3.2's BAG branch is now grounded; every `q*` in the threshold table and both pinned rows (−12, +133.20) reproduce — the pair this entry originally recorded, (−40, +90.96), was superseded when verify round 1 put the premium on the STAKE and gave the BAG branch its own `W` (§3.2, `data/job.js PUBLISHED.workedRows`). Printed on the bag prompt (`bag 118 · fee 13 · chain 4 → 0`), pinned in J1 and J9's monotonicity brute force, and explicitly *not* `xp.comboTransition()`.
4. **Crew forgiveness was switched off where it was priced → idle is now per-target, not per-make.** Crew stands down only on the target that *is* its make's own due review; every other target of that make in the same job keeps its forgiveness. HELD's gate becomes `isMastered` alone (the "no due card in the make" clause made HELD undrawable), with the chain-hold suppressed on due-review targets. G2's encounter counts are re-derived per shape and J4 owns the measurement against `composePage` output over 1 000 saves.
5. **The build table mixed shapes' loot → a full 4×2 matrix, each cell with its own `L̄`.** `L̄` = RUN 6.0 · JOB-10 8.4 · JOB-12 12.7 · VAULT-7 23.1. The `E[ρ]` r1/r2 columns were also wrong (they did not reproduce from `LADDER`) and are recomputed: m85 0.940/0.972/0.989, m60 0.746/0.853, m40 0.562/0.725 — so `Δρ_steady` is **+0.163** and `Δρ_held` **+0.049**. HELD's forgiveness is therefore near-worthless and the document says so; the chain-hold is what you buy, and it now fires on **any non-clean outcome at chain ≥ 3** (a miss alone fires at 1 % on a mastered make — a dead mechanic). HELD's cumulative cost drops 3 → 2. Result, as G2's own constants price it: STEADY 1.1 vs HELD 0.8 on a RUN; HELD 3.5/7.4/7.3 vs STEADY 2.7/5.3/6.0 on JOB-10/JOB-12/VAULT. The parameters are measured by J4 against a ±15 % tolerance that **three of the twenty cells actually meet** (`m̄` on RUN / JOB-10 / JOB-12; the rest are pinned at their measured values with bounded deviations), under a rule that a moved winner means the doc is wrong. **ROUND 4: the winner moved on three rows of four, so the doc was wrong and is now corrected.** Priced from the measured parameters, STEADY wins every shape by more than 4× (1.50/0.09 · 3.33/0.25 · 5.95/0.48 · 6.44/0.40), and **“two flips survive” does not.** Flip 1 (shape) is gone: there is no shape on which HELD wins. Flip 2 (the mastery state, sold as the same function as `weakSpots()`) is withdrawn as a flip, because an identity between two expressions of one quantity is not a decision. What survives is the **manned cap** — one dominant rung below it, one forced rung above it — and G2 says so.
6. **The "growth-optimal band 0.52–0.63" was imported from a multiplicative economy → deleted, with its test.** `E[Δloose] = q·L·ρ̄·m·W − (1−q)·L·m·P` is strictly increasing in `q`; there is no interior optimum. Replaced by the **stake band `q̂ ∈ [0.763, 0.925]`**, derived as the ≥ 80 %-of-peak region of `w(q̂)·E[c](q̂)` (peak 2.50 at `q̂ = 0.854`, from `f(u) = 40u − 160u²`). J1 asserts the peak and the band; nothing asserts 0.52–0.63. §3.2, §3.8 #4, G9 #5 and J1 updated. The loot-vs-rating divergence is now stated as a feature: money prefers certainty, rank prefers consolidation.
7. **G9 #2 was arithmetically impossible → restated as the promise the brief actually makes.** A 51 % game split answers roughly half as many problems per wall-clock minute by construction. New #2: `set(job items) ⊆ set(page items)`, `Σ answered over the day == Σ answered on the flat path`, no item removed by a game decision — asserted in `job-board.test.mjs` — with the wall-clock truth printed on the board button where it already was.
8. **The Elo dial ran backwards → the vault grade is driven by `R_player`.** Symmetric Elo moves `R_house` down on a win, so a strong player's vault got easier with every win and flow control was sign-indistinguishable from losing. `vaultGradeFor(R_player)`: `< 1000` tier ≤ 2 · `1000–1199` tier 3 · `≥ 1200` tier 4. Flow control is `R_player −40`. `R_house` survives as the displayed symmetric opponent. J3 asserts win streaks raise the grade and loss streaks lower it.
9. **Skill-pure contracts broke `sameSkillRun = 2` → draft, then interleave.** `composeBundles` partitions for pricing and selection; `draftUnion(bundles, picks)` dedupes and runs the union back through `spreadSkills` and the 1→4 tier ramp before the first envelope. Contracts survive as the *source label* on each envelope (`from B · VOC`) and are labelled by dominant make, not skill-pure. J5 asserts `sameSkillRun ≤ 2` and a monotone ramp on the drafted queue.

### MAJOR

10. **`guardDist` could not satisfy its own test → the water-filling projection is specified.** Cap, redistribute the excess proportionally over the entries **not yet capped**, repeat — which is what bounds it at ≤ n−1 passes; re-freeing a capped entry reaches the same limit in 40 and 92 passes on the four-wing cases J3 pins. Proportional, not levelled, so “water-filling” is the loose name for it and the pseudo-code in §3.4 is the definition. (ROUND 4: the published pseudo-code said `i ∉ over` and did not terminate in ≤ n−1; the shipped loop always kept a capped entry capped.) Worked: `(1,0,0), ε=.10 → (0.750, 0.125, 0.125)`, Σ = 1.000. The pseudo-code is what the Settings panel prints. J3 asserts Σ = 1 within 1e-9 over 10⁴ random cases.
11. **A job's wings were not guaranteed to be three → support is guaranteed and printed.** `composeBundles` guarantees the 5 posted contracts span ≥ 3 wings and any legal 3-of-5 draft spans ≥ 2; `guardDist` handles `n = 2` explicitly (cap 0.75 → floor 0.25); the board prints `guard: 3 wings on the board`. Contracts being dominant-make-labelled rather than skill-pure is what makes this achievable.
12. **The guard's `x̂` was farmable with throwaway jobs → `x̂` is stake-weighted and per-job capped.** `ω_j = min(posted_j, 0.25·Σ posted)`. A RUN contributes ~⅕ of a VAULT and no job exceeds 25 % of the window. J3 asserts a 3-RUN + 1-VAULT farm moves `x̂` by < 0.08 **on a full ten-job window that holds plan-sized or VAULT work and spreads the honest press**, and records every window where it does not: 0.103 on a JOB-10-only window, 0.082 on 9-JOB + 1-VAULT, 0.087 on a ten-job VAULT window whose honest press never touched the farmed wing, and 0.200 on the all-RUN school-hours week, where the stake weighting has nothing to weigh and the leverage bound `0.30 × (1 − 1/3)` is exactly what the farm takes. (**ROUND 5**: the window length is part of the claim and "on every window" was false on the very window the sentence names. `heatWindow` is `jobs.slice(-10)`, so under ten jobs nothing is evicted and the three RUNs own three of four jobs instead of three of ten: three RUNs and one VAULT is a **four-job** window and it moves `x̂` by **0.300**, decaying through 0.188 / 0.136 / 0.095 at five, six and seven jobs and first reaching the published band at 0.078 on the eighth. Below eight jobs the only thing that holds is the leverage bound. `FARM_BAND.shortWindow` publishes that band and §5b measures every figure in it.) (ROUND 4: the `ω` ratio alone does not bound a job at 25 % of the window — `guard.js`'s uniform ballast `β` does, and it was in the code and in neither the document nor the panel; §3.4 now prints the law with it.)
13. **`cold = 1 + overdue/4` was uncapped → `cold = 1 + 0.5·min(1, overdue / intervalDays(bucket))`, capped at 1.50.** Proportional to the card's own interval, so a 1-day-overdue bucket-1 card is as cold as a 14-day-overdue bucket-5 card, and skipping study buys nothing. `v_i` and §3.8 #1 updated. J9 asserts weekly loot is monotone increasing in review compliance.
14. **`tell = 1.25` keyed on a permanent tag → it pays only while triggered, unresolved and unsealed.** `resolve()` sets `errors[].cleared = true` and drops the multiplier the same tick; sealing retires the tag from the tell pool. J9 asserts a save with 68 deliberate misses earns less over a simulated week than an honest one.
15. **54 of 164 originals could never carry a tell → the tell is skill-keyed, from `save.errors`.** `site/data/cards/asn.js` has **zero** `tag:` entries (verified), and the `asn` grader's tags are decided after grading, so a card-keyed tell was unreachable for ASN-PLP + ASN-ANG — 14 of the 100 skill weight. `index.tellFor(save, skillId)` returns the make's most-triggered unsealed, unresolved tag, which is knowable before the stem. No card-data migration. J7 asserts every one of the 19 skill ids can produce a tell.
16. **RECALL content starvation → stated, measured and answered three ways.** 73 originals have no generator; ASN-PLP + ASN-ANG feed ~8 targets/day against a wing carrying `Σw 32`. Fixes: (a) `composeBundles` posts `min(5, available)` contracts and narrows the draft, with per-wing supply printed on the board and a `thin board` state; (b) new ticket **J5b** adds `js/gen/asn-reason.js` built from the 54 one-line reasons already in `data/asn.js` (one `data/templates.js` entry, no new authored content); (c) untemplated recall repeats inside their interval are blessed at `scope 0.5` and labelled. J5 asserts no card twice in a job and no repeat inside its interval over a 30-run week.
17. **Critical replication had no dedupe rule → dedupe at draft, print gross and net.** Contracts print posted gross; the drafted union prints net with the shared amount (`posted 100 (−5 shared)`). J5 asserts `Σ targets(drafted) == |union|` and that the printed net equals the post-dedupe value for all `C(5,3) = 10` drafts.
18. **The save-size bound was wrong and `days` was uncapped → `days` is a count, `CAPS.game` has five caps, the budget is ≤ 39.7 KB.** `tags[].days: 2, lastDay: '2026-09-17'` replaces an unbounded date array (68 × 20 × 13 B ≈ 18 KB on its own). Budget measured from the shipped serialiser AND from real jobs driven through it (restated five times — the figures first written here were estimates; the round-1 fixture was 8 keys short of the record `serialize()` emits; the round-2 audit found its VALUES narrower than the writers emit and two real costs unpriced; the round-3 audit found the job's own queue fields and trophies in neither half of the split and the call count measured on a corpus that never took a swap; the round-4 audit found `inProgress.meta.before`'s seven game fields charged to the STUDY half and priced in no row; the round-5 verify found `params` — the S7 algebra floor's, which reaches a job's queue and bench and is re-emitted by every later due of a frozen Variant — and `tellOff` written by shipped writers and priced in no fixture): `player` 3.95 KB · `game.tags` 8.10 KB · `game.log` 5.30 KB · `game.heat.window` 0.70 KB · `inProgress.game` 6.29 KB · `inProgress.bench` 1.94 KB · `inProgress.queue` +8 fields 4.39 KB · `inProgress.meta.before` +7 fields 2.40 KB · `trophies` 0.19 KB · rest 0.72 KB = 34.04 KB, plus 5.35 KB reserved across the existing 40-run cap → **39.39 KB added, stated ≤ 39.7 KB**, **per unit and LIVE** (an archived unit carries a second 35.23 KB copy the split cancels by construction — G7), ≈ 540 K chars total against COMPOSED S6's restated 528 KB = 540 672 B — the two BOUNDS closing with 19 B, `state.test.mjs`'s carrier measuring 539 932 of 540 672, and the both-halves-saturated carrier 5 332 over (G7 states all three separately). `state.test.mjs`'s worst case gains a job in progress, its bench, its real drafted queue and its trophies.
19. **The handoff split was unimplementable → two top-level keys.** `site/js/store.js:191-205` archives top-level keys only (verified). `save.player = { rating, rank, elo, records }` → `KEPT_KEYS`; `save.game = { crew, tags, heat, log, backchecks, commit, ledger }` → `ARCHIVED_KEYS`. One rename, zero new machinery, one `MIGRATIONS[1]` step. The "one save key" line in the preamble is corrected to two, with the reason.
20. **Home could not paint the board from save state → two passes.** `tests/home-r2.test.mjs:147-148` forbids a static `page.js`/`plan.js` import (verified). Pass 1 static from `store` + `schedule.dueList` + `data/job.js` (the week line, the row frame and its reserve, the dues, the days cold, the cold-crew box, and the shape's name only when the week itself settled it); pass 2 writes **every** numeral after the dynamic import — the labels, lock counts, cold days, posted values, the per-wing supply and the whole meta line (`~N min`, the end time, the projected split) — because `job/board.js` is the only thing that knows tonight's draft. (ROUND 4: this entry used to give pass 1 the meta line, which is the defect and not a simplification; pass 1 and the pass 2 that replaced it disagreed by up to 13 points of split and 6 minutes, and on 2 of 16 boards did not name the same shape.) J13 asserts no layout shift and no spinner instead of "no import".
21. **The header would carry seven items at 375 px → five during a job.** `site/js/app.js` already renders six (verified: readiness, tminus, level, xp, combo, streak). During a job `hdr-level`, `hdr-streak`, `hdr-xp` and `hdr-combo` are hidden and LOOSE · BAG · chain are added. `job-juice.test.mjs` pins five in a job, six outside.
22. **The board collapsed on input focus → it collapses at call-lock.** A tier-3/4 diagram is read before it is typed, and stem + 400×260 figure + Answer Dock + key row do not fit under a 264 px board on 375×667. The board is needed only during DRAFT / PRESS / CALL, all pre-stem. J6 asserts the board is ≤ 36 px whenever a stem is in the DOM.
23. **The 51.3/48.7 split assumed every optional window was used → two published columns and a projection from observed behaviour.** Default path (primary button, `Enter` through the windows) fixed phases total **160 s** for a JOB and **104 s** for a RUN; full-use **257 s** and **130 s**. JOB-10 measures **43.2 % → 49.8 %**; RUN **51.1 % → 54.3 %**; JOB-12 **33.9 % → 39.1 %**; VAULT-7 **28.3 % → 32.6 %**. (The full-use JOB total was published as 282 s until verify round 2, on a `crew` phase no code path enters; see G1's fixed-phase table.) `save.game.ledger.phaseMeans` keeps a rolling mean of the student's own last-5 phase durations and the board prints *that* (`~48 % game · your last 5 jobs`), falling back to the shipped defaults labelled `projected` on job 1. G9 #1 restated; the constant 45–55 % band and its assertion are gone (G10 #17 already said the no-padding rule wins). The PRESS is no longer auto-completed silently — the primary button pre-presses the equilibrium mix and `Enter` accepts it, which keeps the cold open at ~15 s while making the press a real, countable beat.
24. **The 90-second trace and the phase budget disagreed → both numbers survive, labelled.** Guard reveal is **4 s to accept** on the cold-open path (`board 6 → primary button → guard 4 → call 5` = ~15 s, pinned by J13) and **12 s observed mean** when the student re-presses, which is the number in the phase table. The whole fixed-phase budget was rebuilt around the two columns of #23.
25. **COMMIT was a free button → it binds.** A declared walk-away minute auto-bags at full value and ends the job at that minute; you take +8 % on BAGGED and forfeit the chain in progress and the +10 % completion bonus if targets remain (net −2 % plus the forgone upside). No loss framing, nothing lost from Ledger A, and the remaining targets go straight to `3 left on Today's Page`, so no declaration can lock a study door.
26. **A Backcheck's effect on the rating was unspecified → the rating credit is always taken.** A Backcheck shields LOOSE and the chain only. Without this, three held Backchecks would make the first three targets of every job downside-free 95 calls and the scoring rule improper for them. J7 asserts a shielded miss writes a byte-identical `calls[]` entry.
27. **The first-90-seconds board did not add up → recut.** Old: `A B D = 3+4+2 = 9` printed as 10 targets, 7×T1 + 2×T2 against the JOB shape's 8×T1 + 2×T2, and envelope 1's make (`NOTE`) belonged to none of the drafted contracts. New: `A VOC 4 · D CS-LIN 4 · E PAIRS 4` = 12 raw, 2 shared → **10 targets, 8×T1 + 2×T2, 7.0 answer-minutes, posted 100 (−5 shared)**, three wings in support, and envelope 1 is `VOC` — from contract A. Contract labels are now skill ids (makes) rather than sheet names, which is what `composeBundles` and the wing map actually key on.
28. **No ticket owned the debrief → ticket J6b added.** `js/screens/run.js` Page Summary extension + `tests/job-debrief.test.mjs` (bag drop once per job and the only thing animating, both regret lines equal the solver's value for the realised order, tile mint / skill bars / Readiness delta byte-identical with the layer off, no layout shift). `site/index.html` is added to G7's Extended table for the one-line `css/job.css` link, which was previously listed nowhere.

### MINOR

29. **"Luck moves stakes, never outcomes" was overstated.** Global law 3 now reads: *luck moves stakes and which legal item is drawn; never how an answer is graded.* §3.7(1) matches.
30. **The ×2 was the same placement every job that day and undefined for non-multiples of six.** Now seeded `cyrb53(dateISO | jobIndex | targetIndex)` and defined as an **independent `p = 1/6` per target**; the realised count is printed on the board before the draft. J5 asserts 1/6 ± 0.01 over 10⁴ day seeds.
31. **The envelope's 0.4° rotation deleted.** It was the one off-grid element in a product built on an 8 px grid with a 1 px register. The face-down dim stays; J12 asserts no `transform: rotate` on the envelope.
32. **"The only thing on screen while it happens" → "the only thing *animating*".** The bag drop stays in the header column, the debrief stays interactive underneath, and S9 #7's full-screen moment stays the tile mint's alone.
33. **The rank ladder renamed.** Runner / Second / Wheelman / Boxman / Ghost → **Called 1 … Called 5**, printed beside the number (`rating 7.1 · Called 3`). Every mechanic is unchanged; the ladder now reads as the math it is.
34. **The envelope hid the two words needed to price the call.** It now prints the make id **and** its student-facing name, and the grade: `FAC2 · Factoring a > 1 · grade 2 · cold 3 d`.
35. **Two lines were written in a parent's voice.** "Nothing here beats sleep." → `Board quiet · Readiness 89 · 0 due`. "Night Before is 30 minutes and then you sleep." → `No board tonight · Night Before · ~30 min · ends 21:12`. `[ take a board anyway ]` stays. J12's lint pins both banned phrases.
36. **"CREW | 19 ranks" → "19 makes × 3 states"**, and J4's tautological acceptance ("`capacityFor` is provably ≥ the cost of any allocation it permits" — capacity *defines* which allocations are permitted) is replaced by **"no allocation exceeding capacity or the manned cap is reachable through `allocate()`"**, as a property test over 10⁴ random call sequences.
37. **Capacity stopped binding before the top of the ladder → a manned cap plus a cheaper HELD.** `manned ≤ min(capacity, 12)`, `COSTS = {STEADY: 1, HELD: 2}`. At the 22-point ceiling the maximal legal build is exactly 12 makes: 10 HELD + 2 STEADY, with **seven makes always bare**. At 8 points it is 8 STEADY or 4 HELD. Capacity binds at every level, and "19 makes, so you can never cover the board" is now true rather than aspirational.
38. **Backcheck minting was vacuous on a dry day.** Mint requires `dues ≥ 1 ∧ all cleared`.
39. **The decision count was 33 by generous counting → 24 mandatory / 35 full use, with the arithmetic printed.** DRAFT is one 3-of-5 choice (1, not 5); PRESS is one allocation (1); there are 9 bag/push beats, not 10, because the vault target ends in the getaway. The debrief prints both numbers and links the breakdown table.
40. **Small loose ends, all closed.** (a) The guard multiplier is defined as **the guarded wing's loot multiplier** and the ladder is inverted so rank rewards: Called 1 ×0.50 → Called 5 ×0.75. (b) §3.5's table is retitled "the three disclosed dials" with a driver column; `R_player` drives exactly one of them and rank drives the other two. (c) If placement is skipped, both Elo numbers seed at **1000**, and Settings says so. (d) The Mock's prediction enters the window at `w = min(4ŝ(1−ŝ), 0.25)`, where ŝ is the mean score of its own trailing ten papers — it has no MAKE and therefore no `q̂`, but its weight is **measured off that history rather than defined**, and capped at the smallest weight the window counts, so one Mock slot can never pay more than **2.50** of Σ(w·c). It is **0** on a first-ever paper and 0 whenever ŝ leaves the informative band (`screens/mock.js mockCallWeight`; verify r3 — this document published `w = 1.0` at six sites while the shipped cap was 0.25, and `screens/settings.js` rendered the 1.0 to the student, a 4× overstatement of the app's only calibration surface outside a job) — stated in G7 and Settings, and asserted end to end by `tests/job-call.test.mjs` (which drives `applyMockCall` and reads the entry back off `player.rating.calls`) and `tests/job-week.test.mjs`. (e) The VAULT is trimmed to **7 targets, 17:26–18:33**, inside COMPOSED S1's 10–25 minute session — the upper end read **18:58** here until verify round 3, which is this figure plus the 25 s `crew re-allocation` cell verify round 2 deleted as unreachable (1113 + 25 = 1138 s); `PUBLISHED.shapeTable.VAULT.wallS` is the source; the 21:30 refusal constant is replaced by the general rule **"any shape whose projected end time passes 22:00 is refused"**. (f) Loot per minute is published as **two rows** — answer-only 12.0/12.0/12.7/14.0 and as-experienced 8.2/9.5/10.9/12.5 — both monotone non-decreasing, so the conclusion survives and the published table is the one the student lives.

### Round 4 — the repair round

Twelve independent critics audited the shipped layer over three rounds and returned **20 BLOCKER, 28 MAJOR and 27 MINOR** findings (`designs/r3-findings.json`, each with a file:line citation and command output). Thirteen fixer lanes repaired the code where the code was wrong; **this section is the document's half**, one entry per changed claim: what was claimed, what is true, what changed here. Where a claim was deleted rather than repaired it is also on G11's withdrawn list.

**The scoring rule and the rank.**

41. **Claimed:** `q̂` is the first-attempt rate over the trailing 10 attempts (G3.1, and `data/job.js`). **True:** `call.js` counts a **CLEAR**, over trailing **sittings** — the same event `econ.settle` prices. **Changed:** the definition, with the reason. The code is right: re-pointing q̂ at the first attempt would make one button forecast two events, and a student would honestly call 50 on m60 material they clear 92 % of the time. `site/data/job.js:194`'s docstring already landed; two greps should be added to `tests/job-meta-constants.test.mjs` in the same commit as this text, so the old phrase cannot come back into either file.
42. **Claimed:** propriety, with its assumptions unstated. **True:** the theorem needs an outcome the student does not choose **and** a weight fixed at the seal, and the snapshot is `qHatDetail`'s DEFAULT (cut at `inProgress.game.locked.at`), not a courtesy a caller does. **Changed:** G3.1 states both assumptions, names `before: null` as the opt-out that may not feed `callEntry`, and prints the pre-fix argmax `p* = q·w_clear/(q·w_clear + (1−q)·w_miss) < q`. Findings 11 / 16 / 18 / 22 are **stale** against this tree, and finding 13 is stale too (`COPY.evidence` has no caller; `evidenceWordsOf` and `job-screen.test.mjs` are the enforcement). Neither is re-fixed.
43. **Claimed:** “Rank helps, monotonically” and “rank never removes a tool you own”, while the printed rank was a live recomputation. **True:** mastering the material empties the 50-call window, so the rank walked 5 → 4 → 3 → 2 with `measured === true` at every step — q̂ 0.90 → 0.95 cost three ranks, the 95 rung and guardMult 0.75 → 0.55. **Changed:** G2 “Rank” publishes the **ratchet** (`rankFor(value, { floor })`, passed by all three writers), the fact that it was a continuous slide rather than the `n = 0` corner, and the cost it buys — a badly calibrated student keeps the 95 rung, and G2's two-sided tension now runs one way.
44. **Claimed:** every number on every screen is recomputable from the save (G9 #4). **True:** under a floor, `player.rank` is not. **Changed:** G9 #4 carries the one exception, and G7's save table gains `player.records.bestRating` — the high-water rating, printed on Settings and Stats beside the rank, deliberately not on the board line.
45. **Claimed:** the board prints `rating 5.00 · 0/50 informative calls` beside Called 5. **True:** that reads as a demotion to a student playing their best. **Changed:** the printed line leads with the held rank and says the words — `Called 5 · rating unchanged · no measurement · 0/50 informative calls` — and G3.1 and G6 print both forms.
46. **Claimed:** the Sanity table's two 5.00 rows are one behaviour, and the 95 over-calling row is −4.90. **True:** one 5.00 is a measurement of cowardice and the other is the **absence** of a measurement; −4.90 is the **85** call and the 95 call is −8.10. **Changed:** the table gains a column saying which is which, and the mean is corrected. The rating column clamps either way, so no constant and no test moved.
47. **Claimed:** the two call-ladder disagreement bands are “2.1 percentage points wide”. **True:** 2.04 — 0.28 pp and 1.76 pp. **Changed:** the figure and both halves; `call.js` already said 2.04, so this also closes a doc-vs-code disagreement.

**Deliberate failure.**

48. **Claimed:** “Tanking is strictly dominated”, and a throw “lowers `q̂`, which moves you off the `w·E[c]` peak”. **True:** on the reachable grid `w·E[c]` is 0 / 0 / 1.344 / 2.240 / 2.268 / 0 at the deciles q̂ = 0.5 … 1.0 — and the deciles are not the grid (`h/of` for `of = min(10, sittings)`; the reachable peak is **2.4980 at 6/7**, corrected in verify r3) — so throwing walks a master **onto** the ladder; and an honest master's window empties to 5.00 while a thrower's does not. **Changed:** G3.7 #8 is rewritten around the **ratchet** — rank never falls, so a throw buys a tool already owned — with the measured price (carry non-increasing in the throw rate and strictly down at φ = 1/8; cost asserted > 9 % against a rank prize asserted < 2.5 %, and > 3× it) and the residual said out loud in G2 “what persists”. The stronger rating result is kept where it belongs: at a fixed true ability, throwing scores a **strictly lower** rating on 8 of 8 seeds, because the weight is sealed at the call.
49. **Claimed:** the renewable loot rate is “raisable only by clearing more of the packet” (G3.7(3)(d)). **True:** dropping buckets raises it and **lowers** the take, because a job serves a fixed number of targets and a thrown one pays 0. **Changed:** the clause, with all four asserted directions and the uncapped-targets control that proves the fall belongs to the job's fixed length rather than to Leitner.
50. **Claimed:** the unfilled-slot 5.00 is an anti-farming brake (G3.7(3)(c)). **True:** arithmetic, not a brake — it is the demotion mechanism the ratchet closes. **Changed:** relabelled, and the brake re-attributed. **Claimed:** systematic over-calling is braked by the rating it drives to 0 (G3.7 #9). **True:** since the ratchet, the rating falls and the rank does not. **Changed:** the brake is the **carry** ladder, `P(95) = 5.0` — up to a whole job's bag on one target.

**The crew.**

51. **Claimed:** “Three states, two flips, no dominant build”, with a 4×2 matrix whose winner column reads STEADY / HELD / HELD / HELD. **True:** priced from parameters measured off `composePage → composeBundles → draftUnion`, STEADY wins **every** shape by more than 4× (1.50/0.09 · 3.33/0.25 · 5.95/0.48 · 6.44/0.40), the published winner moves on three rows of four, and only 3 of the 20 parameter cells reproduce within ±15 %. **Changed:** G2 keeps the published table, labels it as G2's own constants reproducing their own winners — which is all `tests/job-crew.test.mjs` asserts of it — prints the measured table beside it, and replaces the flip structure with **one dominant rung below the manned cap, one forced rung above it**. Flip 1 and Flip 2 are withdrawn (G11).
52. **Claimed:** you buy HELD for the chain-hold. **True:** the chain-hold fires on a conjunction of three rare events — 0.0038 to 0.0119 per job, once per 84 to 266 — and `e_held` is measured **below** the published value on every shape, structurally: the best-supplied make of 19 on a 10-target queue tops out under 0.85× G2's 5.0. **Changed:** G2 says the chain-hold is what r2 adds and what it is worth, and G2's spaced-review sentence is re-attributed to the **idle rule**, which is what actually stops a build buying a way out of reviewing.
53. **Claimed:** `argmax(game) = argmax(ΔReadiness)` on one stated domain, with Spearman ρ = 1 over 1 000 saves as the evidence (G3.8, G8's J4 row, G9 #5). **True:** ρ = 1 is an **arithmetic identity** — `crewValue` is a positive constant of the shape times `weakSpots()`'s own sort key — so it cannot fail for any save; and the argmax claim holds only on `alignmentFor().domain.all`, which is **four** conditions (evidence `n ≥ 5`, rank, supply, and the `BAND_FLOOR = 40` band), off which the two argmaxes disagree on 87.3 % of the suite's population (one draw of one generator — corrected in (ii) below). **Changed:** G3.8 separates identity from measurement, states all four conditions where the claim is made, publishes §6.2's and §6.3's measurements, and withdraws the closing sentence. G9 #5 and G8's J4 row follow. The supply gap is published as an asserted range over 24 re-seeded corpora, never as a pair. **Verify round 2 re-opened this entry twice.** (i) *“holds only on `domain.all`”* is itself false: conditions 2 and 3 are argmax comparisons that together ARE the claim (identical on 300/300 drafted boards), conditions 1 and 4 cannot change it (0 of 120), and the claim is true on 27.0 % of boards while `domain.all` holds on 0.7 %. G3.8 now publishes the claim as `alignmentFor().claim` — a measurement — and keeps `domain` as a report. (ii) The 87.3 % / 38.3 % pair was itself seed-specific, the exact defect C-5 forbade for the supply pair: an independent draw reads 63.3 % / 46.0 % and 24 corpora span 51–93 % and 33–53 %, so G3.8 publishes the asserted bounds and the range. Condition 3's published wording (*“serves the make more than once”*) and condition 4's floor-only form are corrected in the same pass, and the `q̂ ∈ [0.5, 1]` restriction is returned to row 3 of the monotone-transform table, which is whose it is. **Verify round 3 re-opened it a third time, on the numerals themselves.** (iii) Every published alignment figure — **43 % / 19 %**, **99 % / 57 %**, **ρ(all) = 0.65** — was §6.2b's: `composePage(s).queue.slice(0, 10)`, Today's Page truncated to ten, which is not a board any job is played on, and 0.65 was that one corpus's own ρ(all) = 0.6546 rather than a mean over 24. On the shipped drafted board the same code reads **58 % / 20 %**, **91 % / 44 %** and **ρ(all) = 0.8334 ± 0.0305**, and the neighbourhood rate is published as its distribution (top-three 61 % ± 6, argmax 29 % ± 5). Every site follows. (iv) *“on every one of which `domain.all` is false”* was a universal that the code forbids — `domain.all ⟹ claim` — and that the corpus refutes at 2 boards of 300; `tests/job-crew.test.mjs` §16 asserts the implication now.
54. **Claimed:** re-allocation is free and unlimited **between jobs**. **True:** `crew.allocate` has one caller chain and it is inside the brief window; the Stats grid is read-only. **Changed:** G2. **Claimed:** a Mock miss drops the crew and “the debrief says so”. **True:** the demotion is computed and applied on the next allocation, but `crew.legalize` has no caller and `COPY.crewDemoted` is rendered nowhere. **Changed:** G4 says what the code does, and names the gap as open.

**The guard.**

55. **Claimed:** the brief window's re-press is a live tap that redraws whatever you do. **True:** the round-3 form redrew on the **LIFT**, so the freed token was placed against a wing already published — and the seed pin that defeats re-rolling is what made that second half a free, fully informed move; a bare redraw was legal too. **Changed:** G1 and §3.4 publish the **atomic staged press**, the four refusal codes, and “a press that moves nothing, and a lift that places nothing, are not options”, with the module-level residual named rather than claimed closed.
56. **Claimed:** the printed `x̂` law, and “no single job exceeds 25 % of the window”. **True:** `guard.js` runs a uniform ballast `β` that appeared in neither the document nor the panel; without it one job takes 52 % of the window in the document's own named farm and 85 % in a window of nine tiny jobs. **Changed:** §3.4 prints the law with `β` and says what it buys; G8's J3 farm clause gains the three windows where “< 0.08” is false, and the leverage bound that holds on all of them.
57. **Claimed:** the guard fixed point `(⅓,⅓,⅓)` and `(0.60, 0.40)`, and J3's convergence row. **True:** both are the **ε = 0** closed form; the shipped ten-job × three-token dynamic settles at `(4/11, 4/11, 3/11)` and `(7/11, 4/11)` when a tied argmax is settled by wing order, and at ε > 0 the target is the ε-floored equilibrium. **Changed:** §3.4 labels the idealisation and prints the shipped attractors; G8's J3 row states the condition.
58. **Claimed:** “the unexploitable strategy is to mix pressure in proportion to study value”, attached to `yᵢ = 1 − k/vᵢ`. **True:** three different vectors — the board pre-presses `pᵢ = A − B/vᵢ`, the unexploitable press against a minimising guard is the maximin `xᵢ ∝ 1/vᵢ`, and `y` is the **House's** mixing, worth 8.0 of an available 12.0 when a player presses it. **Changed:** §3.4, G3.8 #2 and G3.8's gradient table each name their own vector, with the worked triple at `v = (30, 20, 10)`.
59. **Claimed:** the cap projection is “water-filling” and terminates in ≤ n−1 passes with `free = { i : i ∉ over }`. **True:** that form terminates by asymptote — 40 and 92 passes on the four-wing cases — and the excess is shared in **proportion**, not levelled. **Changed:** the pseudo-code (capped stays capped), the name, and G12 #10.
60. **Claimed:** “the 0.75 cap guarantees the guard is never a certainty”. **True:** in two reachable, printed regimes it is a certainty — a board you walked out on, and a blocked wing on a two-wing board. **Changed:** §3.4 and G4 “Mercy” carry the bound **with both exceptions**, which is also what the Settings panel now renders (`CAP_PANEL_COPY`, asserted over 3 000 saves).

**The board, the screen, the week and the save.**

61. **Claimed:** the JOB row's wall clock and split (12:20–14:22 at 43–51 %) as what the session is. **True:** it is a function of the shape's nominal `tierMix`; the composer drafts a T2-heavy queue, and the row brackets **1 of 45** posted boards on wall clock and **0 of 45** on split. **Changed:** the budget table's two columns are labelled **nominal**, a note under it points at the measured row, and G1 statement 3, G9 #1 and G10 #17 each name which basis they are quoting.
62. **Claimed:** `composeBundles` replicates **every** critical item into ≥ 3 of the 5. **True:** every critical the shape has room for; the rest are posted as choice locks or named in `deferred[]`, still due, leading the next board. **Changed:** G1.
63. **Claimed:** a walk re-posts the byte-identical board. **True:** of a walk that answered nothing. A walk that answered a target is a job on record and the next board is a new one; what holds that door is `startJob`'s `page-in-progress` refusal and its toll. **Changed:** G3.7 proof 6, with both halves.
64. **Claimed:** proof 11's “five keys study progress actually lives in”, and that a job writes no forecast point. **True:** `streak` and `jumps` are study progress too and were compared by nothing — they are covered instead by `state.guardSave` refusing every write to them; and a **completed** job now writes the same forecast point, `runs[]` record and daily-goal check `#/run/page` writes, while a job that does not **complete** writes none — BAG is not a terminal state and a job bagged at every beat still completes and still writes (the "bagged" in this entry was itself corrected in verify round 3; see proof 11). The `runs[]` half carries one qualification (verify round 2): a DRAFTED page's row also carries `drafted`/`composed`/`partial`, and that triple is written by whichever screen closes the page, so a walk at the getaway followed by `#/run/page` writes the byte-same row — and earns the byte-same trophies — as the job played to the end. **Changed:** proof 11 and G9 #8.
65. **Claimed:** Home's pass 1 paints labels, lock counts, cold days, minutes, the end time, the split and the supply. **True:** none of those is derivable without `data/cards.js` or tonight's draft; pass 1 and the pass 2 that replaced it disagreed by up to 13 points of split and 6 minutes, and on 2 of 16 boards did not name the same shape. **Changed:** G7 and G12 #20 give pass 1 only what it can derive, and pass 2 every numeral.
66. **Claimed:** the debrief's tomorrow line prints `posted 186`; the regret line prints `cost 0.3 rating`. **True:** the first is the whole due queue, about 1.9× the job's own `Posted` fact eight lines above it; the second is a **credit** gap, and calling it a rating overstated it by 25 to 64×. **Changed:** both sample lines and G6's copy table, and the copy table gains the blank-slot payout line — which was on screen for five of ten targets on the repo's own recommended evening and appeared in no table.
67. **Claimed:** the collapsed board line as a four-field string. **True:** it is five fields including the wing's own multiplier, and it is clipped on the 375 px phone this paragraph specifies. **Changed:** G6 prints the real line and records the clipping; dropping a field is an open product decision on a published example.
68. **Claimed:** the save budget's subtotal 31.46 KB / `player` 3.92 KB / 36.84 KB added. **True at the time:** 31.53 / 3.95 / 36.88, because `player.records.bestRating` shipped. **True now, after round 4 priced `inProgress.meta.before`'s seven game fields and round 5 priced `params` and `tellOff`:** subtotal **34.04 KB**, `player` 3.95 KB, **39.39 KB added against a stated ≤ 39.7 KB**. **Changed:** G7's table (which gains the `inProgress.meta` row and moves the `bench` and `inProgress.game` rows), G8's J10 row, G10 #24 and G12 #18. **And the bound is per unit and LIVE:** every finished unit leaves a second copy — **35.23 KB** measured against a derived ceiling of 35.5 KB — under `save.archive[<unit>]`, invisible to the split by construction because it sits in both halves and cancels. G7 says so, with the derived ceiling and the **≈ 74.6 KB** two-unit figure.
69. **Claimed:** the escalation-direction paragraph in G3.2. **NOT changed, deliberately.** `screens/settings.js` still states the direction unscoped, and `tests/job-shape-measured.test.mjs` §3 asserts the **pairing** in both directions — so scoping this document while the copy is flat turns the suite red, and vice versa. The copy edit and the doc edit have to land in one commit; this pass owns only the document, so the paragraph stands as written and the pairing is recorded here as open.
70. **Still open and named rather than dropped:** the S4 code deletion (r2 → pure forgiveness) and the build inequality that replaces the matrix, both blocked on one coordinated six-file change with an open arbiter question under it; `crew.legalize`'s missing caller; whether the crew grid prices on `rec.m` or `m_shown` (it moves the answer on 16.5 % of boards); the brief grid printing which side of the manned cap the student is on; `state.js` recording `postedAnswered` so a walked job's answered value is exact rather than estimated; three board strings and two copy strings with no call site; G6's four sound cues; the short-desktop row missing from `qa/layout-audit.mjs`'s viewport matrix; and the layout matrix's findings in the two keyboard-open states **this round's own tests lane added** (`job-answer-kb`, `job-payout-kb`, `qa/audit-states.mjs`), measured against a fold this round also moved to the visual viewport (`qa/layout-audit.mjs`): neither the states nor the fold model exists in the last commit before this campaign, the matrix read **0** before them, and `pinKeyboard` still pins a 336 px keyboard with no field focused — so the state has to be made reachable and re-measured before its count means anything, and it is a **study-layer** question when it is. None of them is a claim this document now makes.

**Verify round 1 — seven claims the verification critics found still standing after the repair round.** Every one is a place where the code was fixed, or a claim was withdrawn, and *this document* did not follow. They are listed separately because they are a different failure from rounds 1–3: not a claim that was never checked, but a correction that landed at one site and not at its other one.

71. **Claimed (G1):** Home's pass-1 board prints `minutes` and `split` off `PUBLISHED.shapeTable`, and the swap is "a Request in `notes/econ-fix.md`, not a change this lane may make". **True:** the swap landed. `screens/home.js:20` says "NO SHAPE TABLE HERE (r3)", `grep -n PUBLISHED site/js/screens/home.js` returns only that comment, and `boardModel` returns `minutes`, `wallS`, `endsAt`, `ends`, `split`, `projection` and `projectionSource` as `null` **by contract** — its own docblock says "and that is the contract, not an omission". **Changed:** G1's paragraph now states the closure and cites `tests/job-week.test.mjs`, which asserts the `null`s on every week state and seed that posts a board. G7's pass-1 bullet, G12 #20 and G12 #65 already said this; G1 was the one site left contradicting them, and the pointer at a closed Request is deleted.
72. **Claimed (G1):** the worked board's primary button, `TAKE THE POSTED JOB · … · ~14 min · ends 20:31 · 48 % game`, on a draft the same section declares to be the nominal 8×T1 + 2×T2. **True:** no shipped path produces it. Recomputed through `board.js projectFor` on that draft with no ledger: `answerS 420 · decisionS 160 · gameS 255 · wallS 740`, so the button prints **`~13 min`** and **`38 % game`**; 48 % is none of the four published bases (43.2 / 49.8 table, 37.8 / 45.6 headline) and 14 min is neither 12:20 nor 14:22 after `Math.ceil`. `COPY.primary` also leads with the shape's own name, not a verb. **Changed:** the line is regenerated, the trace names its clock (20:18) so `ends 20:30` follows from `now + wallS`, the whole arithmetic is printed under the trace, and the example is labelled **NOMINAL** as G1's own basis rule requires.
73. **Claimed (G2 "Rank"):** the rating column `< 5.0 | 5.0–6.4 | 6.5–7.6 | 7.7–8.8 | ≥ 8.9`. **True:** those are `RANKS[i].bandTop`, a display rounding 0.1 below the next threshold, so four reachable ratings — 4.95, 6.45, 7.65, 8.85 — sat in **no printed band** while `call.rankFor` happily ranked them (`rankFor(6.45)` = Called 2, beside a printed band that excludes 6.45). The code half landed at `screens/settings.js rankBandCells()`, which builds the bands from `RANK_THRESHOLDS`, and `tests/job-meta-constants.test.mjs` walks the range in 0.01 steps with a negative control pinning the four orphans of the old rendering. **Changed:** the table prints the partition `rankBandCells()` renders, with the defect and its control named under it.
74. **Claimed (G2 "what persists", G3.7 #8):** "what a throw cannot buy is the **rank**", unqualified. **True:** true of a learner, false of a student who is already competent on the unit. That student never passes through the informative band, so they hold no rung to re-buy: at q̂ = 0.97 every call weighs `w = 0.116 < 0.25`, the window is empty, the rating is exactly 5.00 and the rank is Called 2; a throw rate that keeps material inside the band takes a full window to **9.536 · Called 5** through the shipped scorer, and the ratchet holds it (`held === true`) once the window empties again. The throw-rate pin cannot see this case — it holds `rank: 3` common across every arm, which is precisely what lets it price the throw's cost apart from the rank prize. **Changed:** G2 and G3.7 #8 both carry the condition, the ≤ 2.5 % residual bound is scoped to a student who already holds the rung, and the untested arm is named below rather than implied closed.
75. **Claimed (G3.7 proof 4):** carry and rating accrue only on targets with `role ∈ ROLES` **and `drawnBy: 'job'`**. **True:** `grep -rn "drawnBy" site/ tests/` returns **zero** hits; the field is on no queue item, in no serialiser and in no test, and was inherited verbatim from `designs/game-heist.md:272`. The proof's conclusion is correct and its cited defence was fictional. **Changed:** the field is deleted from the proof and replaced by the mechanism that is real — `state.applyTarget` is the only writer of LOOSE, the chain and a `calls[]` entry, `screens/job.js:1174` is its only caller, and the only other `callEntry` call site under `site/js/` is the Mock's one prediction per run at `w = min(4ŝ(1−ŝ), 0.25)` (G12 #40d, corrected in verify r3).
76. **Claimed (G10 #10):** "HELD crew skips the *call* (a pre-stem decision), never the continue." **True:** no such path exists. `grep -rn "skip.*call\|autoCall\|skipCall" site/js/job/ site/js/screens/job.js` returns nothing; `state.lockCall` is unconditional on crew rank, the only other door to the stem is `beginAnswer` (which throws `stakes-on`), and crew is read only after the answer at `crewFor`/`forgivenessOf`. G1's own decision table already counted `CALL | 10 | 10`, and `econ.decisionCount('JOB')` returns `{mandatory: 24, full: 35}`. **Changed:** the clause is struck; the bag/push-occupies-the-continue-slot half resolves Global rule 4 on its own.
77. **Claimed (G12 "left alone"):** that this revision left the guard fixed point "`k = (n−1)/Σ(1/v_i)` with `(⅓,⅓,⅓)` and `(0.60, 0.40)`" alone. **True:** the same revision withdrew exactly that pair — G12 #57 and G11's withdrawn list both say so, and §3.4 now prints the shipped attractors `(4/11, 4/11, 3/11)` and `(7/11, 4/11)` beside the ε = 0 closed form. **Changed:** the "left alone" entry keeps the closed form, which *was* confirmed, and says plainly that its two worked pairs were not.

**And one thing the verification round found that is NOT closed, named here rather than implied shut:** the already-competent arm of #74 is pinned only at the scorer (`tests/job-meta-constants.test.mjs`, the doc-claim lints at the end of that file), not end to end. `tests/job-exploit.test.mjs`'s throw-rate arms are all learners and all hold `rank: 3` common, so no test measures a whole week in which the rank itself is what the throw buys. The claim is published with its condition; the end-to-end arm is a request on the exploit lane (`notes/repair-doc.md`, Requests).

### Verify round 2 — four more claims the document was still making alone

A second verification round audited **this file against the shipped tree** rather than the tree against this file. All four findings below were reproduced by running the code before anything was edited, and none was refuted. The pattern in every one of them is the same and worth naming: **the code landed a repair, wrote down what it had done, and the document went on publishing the pre-repair rule.** A spec that is behind its own code is not merely stale — it is an instruction to a later round to "repair" the code back.

78. **Claimed (G2 "Rank", G3.1):** the ratchet is `rankFor(value, { floor })`, and "rank helps, monotonically in the rating". **True:** `call.js ratingDetail` prices the rank off `earned`, not off the rating — where `earned` was `min(value, ceiling)` when this finding was raised and is **`ceiling` alone** since the same round removed the `min` (`ceiling = Σ w·E[c](p, q̂)/N` mapped through the rating formula: the rating the student's own reports are *worth*, which reads `ok` on no slot) — and `grep -n "ceiling\|earned" COMPOSED-GAME.md` returned no hit describing any of it in G2, G3.1, G3.7, G3.8 or G12; `grep -rln slotCeiling` over the tree returned exactly two files, neither of them a spec. Two further published sentences were falsified by the unpublished rule: the rank is not a function of the rating at all (two windows with the same rating can hold different ranks), and `player.rank` is a high-water of a **second** statistic — `earned` — that the document never named, while `player.records.bestRating` then stored `detail.value`, so the audit record printed beside the rank was not the number that bought it (`Called 3 · best rating 10.00` on a capped window). **Changed:** G2 gains **THE CAP** as its own paragraph — the rule, the slot-by-slot propriety identity over the whole informative **reachable** grid (`h/of` for `of = min(10, sittings on that make)` — **31** informative values, whose best `w·E[c]` is **2.4980 at 6/7**; this entry and G2 both published it as the nine deciles `{0.1 … 0.9}` and `tests/job-call.test.mjs` asserted it as that set, so the identity was verified over a ninth of its domain until verify r3, when `call.reachableQHats` / `call.informativeQHats` made the grid the code's own and the arm swept all 31 — 0 mismatches), F1 (the double-root hole on `q̂ < ½`: published OPEN when this round began, and **closed by the call lane while the round was editing** — `callEntry` now stores the slot's own `q` and `slotCeiling(w, p, q)` reads it, so only a slot with no make, the Mock's, keeps the flattering double-root reading), and the cost (`player.rank` may be below `rankFor(player.rating.value)`; read rungs off the rank). G3.1 gains the same rule stated where propriety is proved, tied to the Sanity table by the asserted identity `ceiling === expectedRating(q̂)`. "Monotonically in the rating" is struck and replaced by "monotone in what the calls were worth". G9 #4's exception is restated on the statistic the rank is actually a high-water of, **in both directions**. **And the `min(value, …)` went with F1 at the same round**, because it was the other half of the same blocker: `ceiling` is conditional on the ESTIMATE `q̂` while `value` realises at the TRUE rate, and a window is selected into the honest rung precisely when `q̂` has run above that rate — so at a true `q` of 0.55 the honest player's rating sat at or above their own ceiling in 4.7 % of windows against the branch-conditional over-caller's 45.9 %, the `min` threw the truthful ceiling away 19 times in 20, and that policy out-banked truth at four of six true `q` even with the `q̂` stored. Priced off `ceiling` alone, truth is the strict argmax over all eight policies at all six rates. The rank's two-sided departure from the rating band is now **printed** on both audit surfaces (`offBand` → `· your calls were worth N.NN`), which is what the exploit-hunt and test-integrity blockers asked for: a student read `10.00 · Called 2 · 50 of 50 informative calls` above a legend saying 10.00 is Called 5, and the arm that should have caught it recomputed `rankFor(value)`, the number the panel had stopped printing. **And while this round was editing, the state lane landed `detail.earned` at all three writers of `player.records.bestRating`** (it had written `detail.value`, which on a capped window printed `Called 3 · best rating 10.00`), so the exception collapses back to one line a reviewer can check — `player.rank === max(the rank this save started at, rankFor(player.records.bestRating))`. That change was caught by this round's own lint within minutes of landing, which is the point of the lint: the document now fails a test when the code moves past it, instead of waiting for a critic. (`tests/job-call.test.mjs` S3-CAP §1–§7; found by call-propriety, exploit-hunt and test-integrity.)

79. **Claimed (G3.8, G8's J4 row, G9 #5):** the four-condition domain, stated as the scope of the alignment theorem, with no reachability anywhere. **True:** `crew.js`'s own shipped docstring says in writing that **`domain.all` is a limit case and not a description of an evening**, and `tests/job-align.test.mjs` §9 asserts it — `domain.all` on at most 1 % of 300 drafted boards over the 19-make pool and at most 5 % at the brief's own call — under a header saying the document publishes the domain and not the rate. It did not: `grep -n "limit case\|domain.all"` found two sites, both stating the domain as if it described play. A reader could not learn from this file that the theorem's domain is essentially empty. **Changed:** G3.8 publishes the reachability where the claim is made, names conditions 1 and 4 as the universally-quantified ones that empty the conjunction, and says the limit-case sentence in this document's own voice; G9 #5 and G8's J4 row carry the same bound. (Found by crew-alignment.)

80. **Claimed (G1 statement 2, the round-3 correction under it, G9 criterion 1):** the board/debrief split criterion holds "on a student whose last five jobs are mid-job WALKs, up to five of five, on every shape", worst gap 2.1 points. **True:** the arm that sentence cites was widened 50× in the repair round — 200 cells per walk level, all fifty corpus saves × four shapes — and at five of five it no longer asserts the criterion per cell. It asserts a distribution: 26 of 200 cells (13 %) outside `SPLIT.agreeWithinPoints = 5`, worst 5.88, against a published "worst gap 2.1". The wider sweep is a real improvement and the residue is an information limit — at five of five the projection window holds no finished job, so the decision term has no unambiguous measurement to rate — but a published sentence may not rest on an arm that tolerates its own falsification. `designs/SPEC-CORRECTIONS.md` A-3 had spelled out the replacement verbatim, including "do not publish 'up to five of five' unqualified", and it had not been applied at any of the three sites. **Changed:** all three sites now publish both registers — per cell to three of five, as a distribution at five of five (87 % of 200 cells inside 5 points, median 2.3, worst 5.9; asserted as bounds at 16 % / 3.0 / 6.0) — and the arm in `tests/job-board.test.mjs` is renamed to say which register it asserts where, so its name stops certifying a claim the numbers below it refute. (Found by test-integrity.)

81. **Claimed (G6's phone paragraph):** "Board sheet 264 px sticky during the decision phases." **True:** both halves are false at the 375 px phone the paragraph names as load-bearing. The shipped cap is `min(var(--job-board-sheet), 38dvh, var(--job-board-fit, 100dvh))`, so at 667 px of viewport height the `dvh` term binds at 253 px and the measured term cuts it further — 264 px is unreachable below about 695 px of height. And `position: sticky` is on `.job-board` in exactly one place, inside `@container jobscreen (min-width: 896px)`; `css/job.css` argues in the same file that the narrow form must **not** be sticky, because a sticky grid item cannot travel outside its own grid area. Measured cold-load at 375×667 in both Chromium and WebKit, through `qa/audit-states.mjs`'s own `prepare()`: `position: static` at every phase, height 232–253. The repair round edited this very paragraph (SPEC-CORRECTIONS N-2 rewrote its `Collapsed:` sentence and the shipped file carries branch B) and left the false clause two clauses earlier standing. **Changed:** the sentence now publishes the cap and the flow the CSS implements, and G12 #22's "under a 264 px board on 375×667" is superseded by it. If 264 and sticky are the intent rather than the record, `css/job.css` and the narrow form are what must move — one of the two has to, and this document is not the half that decides it. (Found by layout-safari.)

**What moved under this round while it was writing, named rather than hidden.** F1 was drafted here as an OPEN finding — the cap could be fooled on `q̂ < ½` and only the rating's own value term closed it — and the call lane landed the real fix mid-round: `q` on the stored call entry, `slotCeiling(w, p, q)` reading it, the theorem holding at every computable `q̂`. The state lane likewise moved `player.records.bestRating` from `detail.value` to `detail.earned` in the same window. Both are published above as shipped, not as requests; both were caught by this round's own lints rather than by a later critic, which is what the lints are for. Nothing in this section is a change to product code; where the document and the code disagreed, the code was right in all four findings.

### Verify round 2 — the meta lane

Two more, from the same round, filed under labels rather than numbers because two lanes were appending to this changelog at once and a duplicated `78.` is worse than a letter. Both were reproduced before anything was edited; the reproduction is in the repo, not in `/tmp`, at `notes/repair-meta-evidence.mjs`.

**VR2-FARM** · **Claimed (G3.7 proof 8, G3.8 #4, G9 #6):** that the throw which buys an already-competent student's rank is priced — "the throw costs the target itself, the chain, the XP, the bucket and the Readiness", residual prize "at most **2.5 %** overall" — and, flatly, "Desirable difficulty is the only path to rank" and "Nothing can be farmed". **True:** the throw the document prices is a staked job target; the throw an exploiter makes is not. `call.qHatFor` builds `w = 4q̂(1−q̂)` from `save.cards[*].history`, and `screens/card.js` pushes an entry there on every graded original — job or plain Today's Page — so a card failed OUTSIDE a job moves the anti-farming weight while paying **no carry, no chain and no rating** (proof 4's own result). Two arms, same start save, every job target cleared in both, identical call policy, differing only in the `ok` flag on free study sittings: **Called 2 on 8/8 for the honest master against Called 4 on 8/8 for the sandbagger, +6.91 % post-climb loot (+2.98 % overall), both currencies on 6/8 — on a byte-identical study ledger (`cards` box/due/reps/lapses/ease/rarity, `skills`, `xp`) on 8/8**. The 2.5 % bound is **1.19×** too small for this action on its own overall basis, and 2.76× on the post-climb basis. (**The pair here read +11.6 % / +5.6 % and the multiple 4.6× until verify round 3.** They were true when VR2 measured them and the economy moved under them — the same script's totals were 115 125 → 128 518 then and are 96 614 → 103 294 now — and nothing in `node --test tests/` executed the script, so four published sites drifted away from their only cited evidence. `tests/job-meta-constants.test.mjs` §8 now spawns `notes/repair-meta-evidence.mjs`, parses `POST-CLIMB … = X %` / `TOTAL … = Y %` back out of its stdout, and asserts them against the numerals parsed out of this document; test-integrity, verify round 3.) The cap does not save it, and the reason survives the one repair this finding did get: the sandbagger's REPORT is honest about the evidence they manufactured. (The finding's second half — `slotCeiling` taking the flattering root of `w`, so a *chosen* q̂ = 0.2 was priced as an earned q̂ = 0.8 and reached the same Called 3/4 band — was **closed mid-round by the call lane's q̂-in-the-entry change**; §2 of the script now reads ceiling 0.00 · Called 2 at q̂ ≤ 0.5. §1 is byte-for-byte unchanged by it, which is the measurement that separates the two halves.) **Changed:** proof 8 carries the whole measurement; G3.8 #4's italic and G9 #6's headline are withdrawn (G11); G9 now says nine of ten criteria pass; Settings' rating panel prints the mechanic and what it really costs in a new **the evidence** row; `tests/job-meta-constants.test.mjs` §8 pins the two mechanism facts so the regression is visible without the 48-job simulation. **NOT CLOSED:** the mechanic. The fix that prices the action rather than the report is to build `q̂` only from sittings that were themselves staked job targets (`js/job/call.js`), and it is an open Request in `notes/repair-meta.md`. The two alternatives considered and not chosen are recorded there too.

**VR2-SPLIT** · **Claimed (G9 criterion 1, G8's J8 row):** the board projection and the debrief measurement "agree within 5 points … **on both the default and the full-use path**", unconditioned. **True:** the suite only ever seasons with the clock and path it then measures (`seasoned(id, clock, jobs)` replays `walkWithClock(s, id, clock)`), so the regime change has no arm at all. Measured with the app's own two published fixed-phase columns as the two regimes, 20 corpus saves, 5 seasoning jobs each: tap→tap worst **1.0** (0/20 over 5), full→full worst **1.7** (0/20), tap→**FULL** worst **6.9** (**20/20** over), full→tap worst **7.4** (**18/20** over) — with the source label still reading `ledger`, so the board claims the student's own five jobs while being 7 points out. This is the ordinary student who taps through all week and tonight opens the windows. **Changed:** the criterion and the J8 row both carry the consistent-surface condition and the measured cross-regime error. **NOT CLOSED:** the projection itself. `game.log` already carries `briefs`/`committed`, so `board.js projectFor` could charge `means.brief` against the student's own rate of *using* a window instead of one blended mean; that and the cross-regime arm in `tests/job-split.test.mjs` are open Requests in `notes/repair-meta.md`.

### Verify round 2 — the board lane

Six more, filed under labels for the same reason the meta lane's are: two lanes were appending to this
changelog at once. Every number below was re-measured through the shipped path (`postBoard → startJob →
lockCall → applyTarget → push → walk`, and the study layer's own `applyOutcome`/`markItem`/`finishPage`
for the schedule laws) before anything was edited, and the arm that owns each one is named.

**VR2-WALK** · **Claimed (G1 statement 2, G9 criterion 1, as this round's own A-3 rewrite left them):** that
the split criterion holds per cell "up to **three** walks in the five-job window", and that at five of five
the residue is "87 % of those 200 cells inside 5 points, median 2.3, worst 5.9". **True:** the first half was
an understatement of the code's possible reach and the second half was a property of ONE clock. The arm swept
levels 0, 3 and 5 only, so it never looked at FOUR of five — the one level at which the board missed the band
under a MEASURED label (`projectionSource === 'ledger'`, the line that reads `your last 1 job`): 9 of 200
cells over on the arm's own clock, 18 of 200 on a deliberator, worst 5.87. The mechanism is that a walked
job's prefix only ever measures the CHEAP END of the ramp — the arm's clock answers a tier-1 stem at 1.47×
the published table and a tier-2 at 0.79×, so a JOB-10 walked at 5 reads 1.47 where the same student's whole
job reads 0.96 — and `personalRates` pooled four such prefixes with the one finished job as if they were four
independent observations of tonight's basket. **Changed, in code:** the answer term now rates the window's
WHOLE jobs whenever it has any and falls back to prefixes only when the window holds none, which is the case
the line already labels `projected`; the decision term is unchanged (it never rated a prefix). Measured after,
over 50 saves × 4 shapes × 6 walk levels × **four clocks** (4 800 cells): **every `ledger` line at every walk
level 0…4 is inside `SPLIT.agreeWithinPoints`, on all four clocks — 4 000 of 4 000, worst 2.32.** The prefix
fallback is what earns its place at five of five, and the control says so — the same 200 cells per clock with
the fallback disabled, i.e. the shipped tables instead of the student's own prefixes:

| clock at five of five | shipped (prefix fallback) | control (the tables) |
|---|---|---|
| TABLE_PACE | 0 % over · median 0.20 · worst 2.11 | 0 % · 0.20 · 0.48 |
| CLOCK | 13 % · 2.32 · 5.88 | 1 % · 1.84 · 5.68 |
| SLOW_STEM | **0 % · 0.78 · 2.69** | **100 % · 11.63 · 13.73** |
| DELIBERATOR | 100 % · **14.22** · 21.78 | 100 % · **30.22** · 32.47 |

The one clock the control wins on is the one whose whole-job pace is already within 5 % of the shipped table,
which is what a fallback to that table is flattered by; on the two clocks that are not, the fallback is the
difference between every cell outside the band and none, and it halves the deliberator's median error. **Changed, in the document:** both registers
are published with the walk level the arm actually asserts them at, and the five-of-five distribution is
published **with its clock population** — arm's clock 13 % over / median 2.3 / worst 5.9, table-paced and
slow-stem students 0 % over, deliberator 100 % over / median 14.2 / worst 21.8. That residue is an information
limit, not an open defect: at five of five no whole job is left to rate and `tGame − fixed` after `k` answers
lies anywhere in `[(k−1)·D + call, k·D + call]`. A `calls` count on the log entry closes it exactly
(`notes/repair-board.md` Requests 1).

**VR2-QUEUE** · **Claimed (G1 statement 1, "the board prints your own number, not the brochure's"):** of every
student. **True:** not of 3.5 % of them. `board.js queuedTargetsOf` had only the shape's published target
count to decide whether a logged job answered its whole queue, and a thin Page does not always serve that
count — on 14 of 400 corpus saves every JOB12 the student ever **finished** was read as a walk, so the
decision term abstained on all five jobs in the window, the line printed `projected` for ever, and the
decision half of the split was the shipped table's. Measured on corpus save 25 (five completed eleven-target
jobs, zero walks): 3.85 points from the debrief headline on the arm's own clock, 6.69 on a slow stem, 8.92 on
a deliberator — over the published band at two of the three, for a student who never walked out of anything.
**Changed:** tonight's own drafted queue length joins the shape's row as a second reading of the same
quantity and the SMALLER of the two is used, because misreading a nearly-finished walk as complete costs one
decision cycle of ambiguity while misreading a completed job as a walk costs the whole term. Scored against the
truth over 24 000 log entries (4 clocks × 6 walk levels × 50 saves × 4 shapes × the five-job window), the
shape's row misread 60 finished jobs as walks and tonight's draft alone misread 4; the smaller of the two
misreads none, in either direction, and `tests/job-board.test.mjs` asserts that zero on every run.

**VR2-DRAFT** · **Claimed (G8's J5 row, and the round-1 paragraph under "What the shape does to the Page"):**
"every legal draft serves the shape's target count exactly (99.5 % of drafts; ±1 otherwise)". **True:** 97.54 %
over 400 saves × every legal draft (15 984 drafts) — RUN 96.70 · JOB 99.25 · JOB12 96.37 · VAULT 97.82. No
shape reaches 99.5 %, and JOB12 leaves ±1 on 26 of its 3 996 drafts, down to **−3 of 12** on a Page too thin to
feed a twelve-target shape. The same histogram comes out of the pre-repair tree, so the repair did not cause it.
`tests/job-board.test.mjs` §18 had been shipping a passing assertion whose success condition was that the
published figure is false (`max(rate) < 0.995`, "a shape now reaches the published 99.5 % — tell the
document"), which is the strongest possible signal that the document rather than the code has to move.
**Changed:** both sites publish the measured rate, per shape, with the JOB12 tail named (SPEC-CORRECTIONS A-6).
The code consequence is VR2-QUEUE above: this row was `queuedTargetsOf`'s justification for using the shape's
nominal count as a stand-in.

**VR2-PIN** · **Claimed (G8's J5 row, the `site/js/page.js` J5 docblock, and the J5 row of the file table):**
that `composePage` is byte-identical to pre-ticket "EXCEPT where J5b's own new template is drawn — one save in
400". **True:** 13 of 2 000 saves (0.65 %) compose differently — 2.6× what the pinned window can see — and one
of them (save 656) draws no template at all: with `T-asn-reason` registered the weak slot builds a Variant the
minute budget rejects, `page.js` step 5 `break`s, and the ASN fallback card pre-ticket took is never taken, so
the Page comes out one item SHORTER (14 against 15). The exception clause described one mechanism and there are
two. **Changed:** both sites publish the rate, the window, and both mechanisms. **The suggested code fix is
refused with its measurement**: making that `break` fall through to the fallback moves 667 of the same 2 000
Pages (33.4 %), 123 of them inside the pinned 400, because every weak slot with a generator also stops breaking.
Global rule 5 is intact in both mechanisms — `fact-01` is still scheduled and returns on a later Page. For the
record, the FUNCTION really is untouched: `git diff` against the pre-ticket commit removes exactly two lines
from `page.js`, both `import` statements (SPEC-CORRECTIONS A-4).

**VR2-DEFER** · **Claimed at four sites (Global rule 5, G1 "What a contract is", G3.7 proof 7, G8's J5 row, G9
criterion 2):** that a deferred due "leads the next board" / is "on the next Page, ahead of every non-critical
item". **True:** the schedule is intact — no game decision ever unschedules an item, 1 162 of 1 162 boards over
400 saves × every shape, each answered through the study layer's own writers and re-composed the way Home
composes it — and the stronger sentence is false 18 times over: 4 dues are crowded off the NEXT Page by
`LIMITS.dues`, and on 14 boards the deferred critical does not lead the review block. The leading half also
fails STRUCTURALLY, not only statistically: `composePage` never seats a hard item first, so a tier-4 deferred
critical can never lead a Page at all. **Changed:** every site publishes the schedule guarantee, with the two
caps named as the composer's own (SPEC-CORRECTIONS A-5). `board.js`'s `deferredLine` — which no screen renders
— carried the same false clause in the board API and now carries the true one.

**VR2-POSTED** · **Claimed (G8's J5 row, G1's button):** that the primary button prints `posted N (−M shared)`.
**True:** it did, and the parenthesised minus is read as money coming off the top when it is not a deduction at
all. Measured on a real board: the button said `posted 419 (−239 shared)` where 419 is what the job pays (and
what the debrief prints as `Posted`), while `419 − 239` is 180, the three contract rows printed directly above
add to 526, `526 − 239` is 287, and 239's own basis — a live gross of 658 — appears nowhere on the screen. No
arithmetic a student can do on that screen produces the number the button promises. **Changed:** the payout
segment is `COPY.postedFlat` — `posted 419`, no operator — and the sharing toll keeps `COPY.postedNet`'s own
words on a line of its own (`board.sharedLine`), fed the one pair whose subtraction IS the payout by
construction. The suite parses both numbers back out of the rendered string and requires the difference to be
what `startJob` writes, so a printed subtraction that lands on nothing cannot ship again. (The `gross` →
`posted` rename asked of `data/job.js` is withdrawn: the parameter is now fed a gross.)

### Verify round 3 — the econ lane

Three, filed under labels for the same reason the round-2 lanes' are. Every number below was measured through
the shipped machine — `startJob → beginTargets → lockCall → applyTarget → push → brief → crack/walk → endJob`,
both exits driven to a terminal debrief — before anything was edited, and the arm that owns each one is named.

**VR3-GETAWAY** · **Claimed (G1's key table, §3.8 #6):** that CRACK / WALK is *"all-in on the vault, or leave
with the bag"* — a wager with two live branches — and that the min-maxer *"cracks the vault when `q > 3/7`"*.
**True:** WALK was **strictly dominated** — the phrase withdrawn about tanking at G11, and here exact and no
longer true after this repair — in every state, by a branch that could not lose. The vault's call
row contains the 50 rung (`CALL_LEVELS[0]`, granted at every rank, `P = 0`), so `econ.missFor` returns 0 on it
and a deliberate miss on the vault costs exactly nothing; answering the vault — including by missing it — made
`targetsLeft === 0`, which paid the **+10 % completion** on the whole BAGGED pile, and WALK, which banks the
same LOOSE at the same full rate, was refused it. Measured on 50 seeds × 4 shapes, both branches driven to a
terminal debrief: `CRACK@50 → deliberate miss` banked **×1.1002** of WALK, ahead on **200 of 200** getaways,
worst branch ×1.0976 (RUN ×1.1001 · JOB ×1.1000 · JOB12 ×1.1002 · VAULT ×1.1004). The min-maxer's threshold
was not 3/7 — no `(W, P)` pair on the shipped ladder produces 3/7 — it was **0, on every shape**. **Changed,
in code:** the end-of-job bonus rule moved out of `state.endJob` into `econ.exitBonusRate`, which prices the
getaway WALK at **parity** — it is the one exit whose only unanswered target is the vault, so it takes the
same completion. A mid-job QUIT still takes none (it leaves targets the student never faced) and still
auto-banks at 50 %. **The bonus was deliberately NOT gated on the vault CLEARING**, which was the other
candidate repair: withholding it from a knocked vault would make facing the last lock of the night worse than
skipping it, and §3.7 proof 6 forbids exactly that (*"there is no state from which a student is better off
closing the app than answering one more problem"*). **Re-measured after, same 200 getaways:** the miss branch
at 50 is exactly equal (**×1.0000**, ahead on 0 of 200), CRACK is ahead only on a clear (×1.3244), and at a
staked rung it loses — `CRACK@70 → miss` ×0.7987 and `CRACK@85 → miss` ×0.4912, behind WALK on 200 of 200. So
the getaway has two live branches again and the live decision is the **rung**: break-even against walking is
`P/(W + P)` = 0 · 0.300 · 0.526 · 0.694, and the EV-max rung changes hands at the published carry cuts.
`data/job.js PUBLISHED.getawayParity` holds the whole table; `tests/job-econ.test.mjs` §8 reproduces it from
the machine and asserts the parity as an equality, not a bound. **Changed, in the document:** G1's key table
row and the paragraph under it, §3.8 #6. (Found by exploit-hunt, verify round 4.)

**VR3-ROWS** · **Claimed (G8's J1 acceptance row, G12 #3, and the "left alone" list below):** that §3.2's two
worked push/bag rows are **(−40, +90.96)**. **True:** they have been **(−12, BAG)** and **(+133.20, PUSH)**
since verify round 1 put the call's premium on the STAKE and gave the BAG branch its own `W` — §3.2 itself
prints `30 + 28 − 70 = −12` and `30 + 163.20 − 60 = +133.20`, `PUBLISHED.workedRows` ships that pair, and
`tests/job-econ.test.mjs` drives `pushMinusBag`/`pushOrBag` on those two states. Three sites went on printing
the superseded pair for two rounds, and the third of them is a list titled *"left alone"* — the same failure
that list already records about the guard fixed point, one entry away, undetected for the worked rows.
**Changed:** all three sites now print the shipped pair, the worked rows are off the "left alone" list with
the cover named as the reason, and `tests/job-econ.test.mjs` §9 lints every site in the document against
`PUBLISHED.workedRows` so none of them can be retyped again. (Found by spec-fidelity.)

**VR3-VAULT** · **Claimed (G1's budget paragraph, G10 #18, G12 #40e):** the VAULT is *"7 targets,
17:26–18:58"*. **True:** the shipped row is `PUBLISHED.shapeTable.VAULT.wallS = [1046, 1113]` — 17:26 to
**18:33**. 18:58 is 1138 s, which is 1113 + the 25 s `crew re-allocation` fixed-phase cell that verify round 2
deleted as unreachable and double-charged; the figure moved with the cell and three sites did not, while G1's
own budget table two paragraphs up had already been re-measured to 18:33. Nothing in `tests/` or `site/`
matched `18:58` or `1138`. **Changed:** the three sites print 18:33 and name `PUBLISHED.shapeTable.VAULT.wallS`
and the deleted cell, so the 25 s are traceable rather than lost; `tests/job-split.test.mjs` already recomputes
both ends of that row, and `tests/job-econ.test.mjs` §9 now lints the document's copies of it. (Found by
spec-fidelity.)

### What the board confirmed and this revision left alone

The carry EV table and both indifference sets (.600/.778/.882 carry, .600/.775/.900 Brier) · the propriety proof of `c(p,o) = 10 − 40(p−o)²` · every `q*` in the §3.2 deep-pile table from `θ* = mP/(ρ̄W(m−1))` — but **not** the two worked push/bag rows, which this list carried as `(−40, +90.96)` and which were NOT left alone: verify round 1 moved the call's premium onto the STAKE and gave the BAG branch its own `W` (a bag empties the pile that backed the call, so `W_bag` falls to `W(50)`), and the pair is **`(−12, BAG)` and `(+133.20, PUSH)`** — the decisions unchanged, which is the point. §3.2 has printed the new pair since that round; this list, G8's J1 row and G12 #3 went on printing the old one for two further rounds, which is the same defect as the guard fixed point below and is caught the same way (`data/job.js PUBLISHED.workedRows`, lint in `tests/job-econ.test.mjs` §9) · the guard fixed point `k = (n−1)/Σ(1/v_i)` **as the ε = 0 closed form, and only as that** — its two worked pairs `(⅓,⅓,⅓)` and `(0.60, 0.40)` were NOT left alone: this revision withdrew them as a description of the shipped behaviour and published the ten-job × three-token attractors `(4/11, 4/11, 3/11)` and `(7/11, 4/11)` beside them (§3.4, G12 #57, G11's withdrawn list). A list titled “left alone” may not contain an item this revision rewrote, and it did, for one revision (G12 #77) · the wing partition (4 wings, 19 skills, Σw = 32 + 26 + 23 + 19 = 100, no skill in two wings) · the internal arithmetic of the split table · `minutesPerTier` read from the shipped constant · `patternLine`, the 11 `AREAS` and the 68-tag catalogue · Ledger A / Ledger B separation, the `min(LOOSE, ·)` floor, CALL IT, the 50 % auto-bag, the pinned job seed, the 22:00 auto-bag at full value, D−1 posting no board, and `settings.game = false`. **The honesty architecture is the strongest part of the document and none of it broke under audit.**
